- H26. Width changes when panels dock or float (#597) change wrapped-line heights. The vendor observes the root [API§3.16]; `slim-screen-panels` and `panel-resize` run under the switch in phase 5.
- H27. A context item added or removed above the line in view. It is a list replace (the `reconcile` writer), not a DOM insertion: the view is restored from a line target, and everything the vendor instances held is restored or folded (H81). N5 "does not move it" and the L2 `reconcile` tests.
- H28. A reused `cacheKey` on derived metadata (F7): silent non-render. One `_rekey` function, a unit test, an L2 test.

**Completion and error handlers that assume they are alone**

- H29. `_handleCommentFormSubmit` removes the form, reloads comments and expands the new card for the minimizer; a concurrent `loadUserComments` from a WebSocket event can overtake it, and a suggestion display can publish the same item. The card lookup goes through `findCommentCard` after the reload settles.
- H30. `refreshPR`'s `finally` and the Local refresh handlers restore UI state assuming the diff they started with is current. They go through `_setDiffStatus`, which is idempotent, and never touch the scroller.
- H31. The tour's exit path restores collapsed files and removes context files it added; `renderDiff` tears the tour down first. With `setFileCollapsed` these no longer need a mounted wrapper, but the order (exit tour, then `renderAll`) must be kept. Each removal ends in a `loadContextFiles` and a `reconcile`; `unmountAll` already returns a promise that settles when they have, and the next tour's open waits for it. A stop annotation on a context item that is being removed is destroyed with its record; `unmountStop` must treat "no record" as done.
- H32. `displayAISuggestions`' queue and epoch (#599) assume one display in flight. `_removeRenderedSuggestions` iterates `bridge.files`, which now holds every file; wrap it in `bridge.batch`.
- H33. The upgrade queue's drain assumes the item it fetched for still exists with the same metadata; compare `_renderGen` and the record's derivation counter before publishing.
- H34. The comment-minimizer's MutationObserver on `#diff-container` re-injects indicators on any subtree change. Virtualization produces many more mutations. Keep it for correctness; measure it in phase 9 and move it onto the lifecycle hook if it shows in the scroll profile.
- H35. Vendor callbacks run inside a render pass. Anything they call that publishes must not flush (F4); the depth counter enforces it.

**Vendor internals the bridge reads** (the same exposure as main [MAIN§e9], each pinned by an L1 test; with D2 = B also `expandedHunks` values of `Infinity`, the `type` field of the metadata, and the separator markup `CONTEXT_CSS` selects): `instance.hunksRenderer.expandedHunks` and `getExpandedHunk`, `instance.getLineIndex`, shadow attributes `data-line-index`, `data-column-number`, `data-line-type`, `data-line-annotation`, `[data-code]`, the slot name format, and the `FileDiffMetadata` shape used by `pierre-context.js`. No TypeScript-private CodeView method is called. Since phase 5 also `instance.resetLayoutCache` (H120), and since the phase-8 fix round `VirtualizedFileDiff.prototype.computeRenderRangeFromWindow`, `instance.height` and the `bufferAfter` / `totalLines` fields of the range it answers, which the bridge wraps (F30, H162).

**Found in phase 1**

- H36. The shared E2E fixture diff (`tests/e2e/test-server.js`) is not a well-formed unified diff: the first hunk of `src/utils.js` declares 5/8 lines and holds 4/7, the `src/main.js` hunk declares 6/10 and the parser accepts 4/9, and blank context lines lack their leading space. The vendor parser logs this on every page load and renders what it parsed. About 100 specs index rows of this fixture by position. Before phase 5 depends on it, phase 2 must establish what `hydratePartialDiff` does with this patch against truthful contents (it may throw), and decide between fixing the fixture and keeping it as a deliberate "malformed patch" case.
- H37. The E2E file-contents mock answers every unregistered path with a generic 40-line body. Besides the geometry churn the conventions already describe, it makes the vendor log `DiffHunksRenderer.processDiffResult: deletionLine and additionLine are null, something is wrong` when a highlight result for the patch-derived diff meets the re-diffed contents (seen on `q.sql` in `diff-header-lookalike-stats.spec.js`, Local mode). The message is allow-listed with that reason; phase 2's per-path registry of truthful contents should remove the cause, and the allowance with it.
  - *H36 resolved in phase 2.* The fixture was fixed, not kept as a "malformed patch" case: the headers now declare what the hunks hold (`-1,4 +1,7` and `-10,4 +10,9`) and the stray blank lines are gone. The vendor parser had been dropping exactly those lines, so the rows on screen are unchanged: a patch-only render of the old and the new fixture gives the same 22 rows, separators and expand controls in PR mode and Local mode (`fixture-rows-old-vs-new.txt` in the phase-2 evidence; the full suite passed with no row index touched). The fixture and its truthful contents are `defaultFixture()` in `tests/e2e/fixtures/diffs.js`. The four allow-list entries for the parser's reports were removed. What `hydratePartialDiff` does with this patch is no longer an open question in the sense H36 meant (it is a well-formed `change` diff with contents that match); the contract suite's F15 covers that type.
  - *H37 resolved in phase 2.* The test server answers every default-fixture and corpus path from a registry of the contents the patch was generated from (`fileContentsFor`), and a spec that writes its own diff supplies the contents with it (`customCorpus`). Over a full run no request reached the generic fallback (`E2E_CONTENTS_LOG` stays empty), and the one remaining source of the `processDiffResult` message was a spec that answered `''` as every file's contents (`diff-header-lookalike-stats.spec.js`), now fixed. The allow-list entry was removed.
- H38. `itemMetrics.lineHeight` is a measured sub-pixel value (17.390625 px at 100% zoom). Browser zoom and display scale change it. The bridge must measure it at construction, and a zoom change after that leaves the estimate for unmounted rows slightly off until they mount (mounted rows are measured). Phase 5 decides whether to re-measure on `resize`; the development-bundle E2E pass shows whether it matters.
- H39. Anything positioned by the app above the items inside the scroll root (padding, a border, a banner) shifts every item relative to the vendor's layout by its height (correction 2). The `#diff-status` sibling and the toolbar must stay outside the root.
- H40. Main logs about 5,500 `console.error` lines over one E2E run, all from a handful of causes (the table in section 11, phase 1). The console guard is only as good as its allow-list: an entry that is too wide switches the guard off for a class of errors. Entries carry reasons, "Failed to load resource" entries must name the request, and `tests/unit/e2e-console-guard.test.js` enforces both.
- H41. **E2E runs with CSS transitions and animations ON**, the same as production and the same as every E2E result main has ever produced. Main's `tests/e2e/fixtures.js` registered an init script to disable them ("for faster test execution"); an init script runs while `document.head` and `document.documentElement` are both null, on every navigation, so it threw a TypeError once per document and styled nothing (measured over a full run: 714 documents, 0 styled; a probe element with `transition: opacity 3s` computes `3s` in PR mode, after a reload, and in Local mode). Phase 1 deleted the script. Consequences for later phases: (1) a geometry or landing spec must wait for the settled value, never read once after a class change. The AI panel's `width` transition (`pr.css`, `.ai-panel`, 0.2 s) resizes the diff, re-wraps lines and so changes item heights while it runs (H26); popovers, badges and the add-comment button fade and move over 0.1 to 0.15 s; the line highlight keeps its class until `animationend` (3.5 s; `pierre-bridge.js:1266`, `pr.js:9516`, `ChatPanel.js:5745, 6029`). (2) The golden dump and the performance comparison (phases 2 and 9) are taken with transitions on for both engines. (3) Nobody may switch transitions off in the fixture as a side effect of another change: with a working injection the whole suite's timing changes (measured in section 11, phase 1), and that needs its own baseline. Smooth scrolling (`behavior: 'smooth'`) is not a CSS transition and is unaffected by such a style either way.
- H42. A Playwright init script cannot touch the DOM (same cause as H41: no `<html>` yet). The `PR_ENGINE` init script only sets a global, and it is registered on the browser **context**, so a second tab or popup opened by a spec gets the flag too. Registered on the `page` fixture, a page from `context.newPage()` would have run the default engine inside a `PR_ENGINE=codeview` run without any sign (measured before the change: flag `null` on the second page). No spec opens a second page today.

**Found in phase 2**

- H43. **On the current engine too, "upgraded" is not "displayed".** `pierreBridge.files.get(f).baseMetadata` is set when the upgrade is requested; the upgraded diff is on screen a worker round trip later (F5 is a `DiffHunksRenderer` behaviour, so it holds for `FileDiff`), and every row is re-created when it lands. Rows are re-created again on every annotation update. A test that waited for `baseMetadata` and then held a row was detached about 5% of the time at eight workers. The signal that works on both engines is `instance.getRenderedDiff() === instance.fileDiff` (correction 5); `waitForContentUpgrade` in `tests/e2e/diff-dom.js` waits for it.
- H44. Chromium refuses to navigate to some ports (`net::ERR_UNSAFE_PORT`); 4045 and 4190 are within reach of `4000 + workerIndex`, and the worker index grows with every worker Playwright replaces. Seen once in a 120-execution run. The fixture now skips those ports.
- H45. A scripted pointer drag that starts on the gutter comment button (`wrapButton` in `pierre-bridge.js`, the application's own drag) never selected more than two lines: after the first `setSelectedLines` the hovered row stopped following the pointer. A drag down the line-number column (the vendor's selection) selects any range, and `selectLineRange` uses that. Whether the button drag is limited for a real user on main was not investigated; phase 6 ports that code and must look. *(Phase 4.)* Part of the cause is the vendor's: once a line selection exists, the getter the vendor hands to `renderGutterUtility` answers the selection's last line (`InteractionManager.getHoveredLine` returns `gutterUtilityLine ?? hoveredLine`, and the utility is pinned to the selection), not the row under the pointer; the row under the pointer keeps the `data-hovered` mark. Measured through the new bridge (`bridge/reconcile.spec.js`, the focus-and-selection test). On the new bridge a drag from the comment button over two lines selects them and the next click acts on the range (`bridge/gutter.spec.js`); a longer drag was not measured and is still phase 6's to look at.
- H46. The context-files POST route requires the file to exist in the review's working directory. The E2E fixture reviews have none (PR mode: an empty directory; Local mode: a path that does not exist), so `context-files.spec.js` mocks the four context-file routes statefully. The spec proves the frontend; the routes rest on their integration tests.
- H47. What main shows for a file without rows, measured (`edge` corpus in the golden dump): a binary file shows **nothing** under its header in both modes (PR mode builds an empty legacy table because the server sets `binary: true`; Local mode never sets the flag and mounts a vendor host with no rows); a mode-only change, an empty added file and a pure rename are header-only vendor hosts in both modes. No message is rendered for any of them, so under plan section 3.3 none gets a `notice` annotation. The sidebar differs by mode for a binary file: "BIN" in PR mode, nothing in Local mode.
- H48. Collapsing a file while scrolled 300 px inside it, on main: the scroll position is kept, so in a long diff the collapsed header ends up 300 px **above** the viewport (`headerTop` -300 to -303 in the `many` and `edge` goldens); in a short diff the extent shrinks and the browser clamps the scroll (`headerTop` 394 and 540 in the `eviction` and `default` goldens). A sidebar jump lands the header 59 px below the top of the scroll container in every corpus (the sticky toolbar's height), or at the end of the diff when the target is the last file. These are the landings phase 5 has to match or deliberately improve (plan 3.12).
- H49. `src/routes/pr.js` assigns `module.exports._attachHunkHashes` and `_computeHunkHashesFromDiff` and then replaces `module.exports` with the router, so neither is exported. Not touched (`src/**` is outside this phase); `tests/e2e/fixtures/diffs.js` computes hunk hashes with the same two helpers the routes use.
- H50. Unchanged-file findings (#602) are not on this branch: the only server-side creator of a context file is `ensureContextFileForComment` (`src/routes/reviews.js:151`, a comment saved on a file outside the diff); the frontend creators are `ChatPanel` and `TourRenderer` through `PRManager.ensureContextFile`. If #602 lands on main before this branch does, its creation path needs its own N5 scenario. See H91.

- H51. **Nine behaviours of main that a spec states through a named expectation in `tests/e2e/diff-dom.js`**, because the migration changes them on purpose or because they are defects main has today. Each is a constant with its explanation next to it; the phase that changes the behaviour flips the constant and no spec is edited. Two were flipped in phase 3; the other seven have the value main gives them. The D2 decision adds two context-file expectations in phase 5 and takes one off the list of flips (`CONTEXT_FILE_PLACEMENT`). *(Phase 5.)* From phase 5 to phase 8 the suite runs on two engines, so an expectation that an engine changes has a value per engine: `diff-dom.js` holds the default engine's and `diff-dom-codeview.js` (`expectations`) the virtualized viewer's, under the same exported name. Phase 5 added three more (last three rows), each because a spec of its acceptance list stated the default engine's behaviour (correction 13).

  | Constant (value today) | What main does, measured | Changes in |
  | --- | --- | --- |
  | `CONTEXT_FILE_PLACEMENT` (`'interleaved'`) | A context file sits by path among the changed files | **Never flips** (D2 = B: a context item sits in the same place). Phase 5 removes the constant's `'after-diff'` value |
  | `LAYOUT_TOGGLE_KEEPS` (`'scroll-offset'`; `'first-visible-line'` on `codeview` since phase 5) | The unified/split toggle restores the pixel offset. Row heights above differ in the other layout, so the line that was at the top is somewhere else (2,000 px away in the 60-file corpus). The plan's matrix row 12 ("split toggle keeps position") is true of the offset only | **Per engine since phase 5**: `PRManager.handleDiffViewChange` hands the layout to the bridge on `codeview` and restores nothing itself; the spec that asks it (`scroll-landing.spec.js`) is phase 7's under `codeview` |
  | `UPGRADE_WAITS_FOR_FOCUS` (`false`; `true` on `codeview` since phase 7) | A content upgrade lands while a comment textarea in that file has the focus, once the pointer has left the file. The rows are re-created, the form and its text are kept, the focus is lost: further keystrokes go nowhere | **Per engine since phase 7**: `true` on `codeview`. A background upgrade waits for as long as the focus is in a text field of the file (correction 22); N12 "a draft typed before the upgrade is still in the form after it" types on through it. The default engine keeps main's behaviour |
  | `DELETED_COMMENT_RETURNS_ON_RERENDER` (`false` since phase 3; `true` on main) | On main, deleting a comment through its card removes the element and leaves the annotation. The next re-render of the file (a reveal of hidden lines) draws the deleted comment again until the comments are reloaded. Counts, which come from data, stay right | **Flipped in phase 3**: `PRManager.patchUserComment` removes the annotation with the comment |
  | `CHAT_LINK_REACHES_UNRENDERED_FILE` (`false`; `true` on `codeview` since phase 7) | A chat link to a line of a changed file that has never been near the viewport does not arrive: `ChatPanel._scrollToLine` asks `pierreBridge.files.has(file)`, which is false until the body renders, and its fallback has no rows. The diff scrolls toward the file once and usually stops short (2,810 px above the header in one measurement; in 1 of 10 runs the line happened to be on screen); the line is never flashed | **Per engine since phase 7**: `true` on `codeview`. `ChatPanel._handleFileLinkClick` decides from the bridge's record of the path, and `_scrollToLine` goes through `revealInDiff` (a line target), which the viewer serves for a file it has not mounted. The default engine keeps its own path and main's value (correction 21) |
  | `REVEALED_LINE_KEEPS_FLASH` (`false`; `true` on `codeview` since phase 7) | A chat link to a line hidden between two hunks reveals it and scrolls to it, and the flash is put on a row that is replaced when the revealed diff is drawn: the line is flashed for 1 to 3 frames (4 measurements) | **Per engine since phase 7**: `true` on `codeview`. The line is flashed once it is what the file draws (`whenLineDisplayed`), and the bridge puts the flash back on every row drawn for the line while it lasts (H96). A range is one flash over all its lines |
  | `BASE_CHANGE_KEEPS_CARDS` (`false`) | Changing the base branch of a Local review (the selector of a stacked branch) fetches `?base=<branch>`, re-renders every file and puts nothing back: the line comment, the file-level comment and the suggestion are gone from the diff while the counts (from data) still include them, until the next comment or suggestion reload. The sixth re-render path of H1 | **Not flipped (measured in phase 6).** `renderAll` discards every record with its annotations and this path reloads nothing afterwards, so the virtualized viewer does what main does; `rerender-overlays.spec.js` passes on `codeview` with the value `false` (22 of 22). A fix belongs to the path (a reload after the render), on both engines, and is not migration work |
  | `THREAD_ADDED_WHILE_DIFF_AWAITS_DISPLAY` (`'line'` since phase 3; `'file-level'` on main) | On main, an external thread whose list is answered while its file's upgraded diff awaits display, after any annotation publish on that file, is demoted to the file's comment zone and stays there until the next thread reload (H60) | **Flipped in phase 3**: the external manager decides from the visibility oracle and does not look for the card (plan 3.9) |
  | `GUTTER_BUTTON_STAYS_WITH_ANALYSIS_SUGGESTIONS` (`true`; `false` on `codeview` since phase 6) | With the suggestions of an analysis on the review when the page loads, the gutter button of the last hovered row stays showing after the pointer has left the diff (H66) | **Per engine since phase 6**: `false` on `codeview`. The viewer's own gutter utility leaves with the pointer whatever the review holds; the fallback positioner is not ported. The test's first half (the buttons show on the hovered row) failed on `codeview` before this phase for another reason, H129 |
  | `SMALL_CONTEXT_GAP` (added in phase 5; `'gap'` on the table renderer) | A hidden gap of 5 lines or fewer in a context file is a gap row | **Per engine since phase 5**: `'shown'` on `codeview` (the lines are drawn, the rule changed files have; D2-6); `expectedContextRanges` |
  | `contextGapLabel(n)` (added in phase 5; "N hidden lines" on the table renderer) | The label of a hidden gap of a context file | **Per engine since phase 5**: "N unmodified lines" on `codeview` |
  | `UPGRADED_DIFF_HUNKS` (added in phase 5; `'rediff'`) | The content upgrade diffs the two contents again: three lines of context around every change, and the last lines of a file are drawn when few enough are left | **Removed in phase 7.** Both engines diff the two contents again (decision D5a, correction 20), so there is one value and nothing to ask; N1 asserts one separator on both. The context is four lines, not three |
  | `MISSING_VIEWER_BUNDLE` (added in phase 5; `'table'`) | Without the viewer's bundle every file is drawn by the application's table renderer | **Per engine since phase 5**: `'message'` on `codeview` (decision D1: an error in place of the diff; N13) |
  | `WORKER_LOAD_FAILURE_ERROR` (added in phase 5) | A worker script that cannot load: the vendor logs one `Worker error: Event` line | **Per engine since phase 5**: on `codeview` the pool also logs `WorkerPoolWorkerError: WorkerPoolManager: worker failed: the worker script failed to load ...` once; the same viewer goes on highlighting on the main thread (N2) |
  | `SUGGESTION_BLOCK_HAS_EMPTY_LINE` (added in phase 6; `true`) | The block the suggestion button inserts holds an empty line after the code (H52) | ~~Never flips in the migration~~ **Flipped to `false` in the review fixes of PR #646 (H181)**: each line loses its line ending before the lines are joined, so the code is followed by the closing fence at once (main: the empty line, measured). `comment-forms.spec.js` asserts the exact text in both modes |
  | `COLLAPSE_INSIDE_FILE` (added in phase 7; `'scroll-offset'`) | Collapsing a file while scrolled inside it scrolls nothing: the collapsed header is left above the viewport and a later file is on screen (H48) | **Per engine since phase 7**: `'header'` on `codeview`. The header of the file that was collapsed stays where the reader was looking, flush under the toolbar, and the next file follows it (H111, H145). Deliberate, visible, listed for the user. **Corrected in phase 10: this is main's behaviour now, not a difference.** Main keeps a collapsed file's header where it was stuck since v5.4.3 (`760fe822`); the named expectation went with the engine switch in phase 8, and the golden comparison no longer lists the landing (section 11, phase 10) |

- H52. `CommentManager.insertSuggestionBlock` puts an empty line after the code inside the block on main: the row text it reads ends in a newline and the block template adds its own (`"```suggestion\nfunction log(message) {\n\n```"`, measured in both modes). A suggestion applied from such a block would add a blank line. `comment-forms.spec.js` tolerates one or two newlines and requires neither. Not fixed (application code); the phase 6 port of the form must not copy it blindly. **Fixed in the review fixes of PR #646 (H181, vendor fact F32):** the text is the diff's own line text, which keeps its line ending; each line now loses it before the lines are joined, and a block of several lines has no empty line between them either (main had one after every line).
- H53. **A comment saved on a file the server does not find in the review's stored diff creates a context-file row** (`ensureContextFileForComment`). A spec that routes its own diff and comments on one of its files therefore left a context file behind in the worker's database, and later tests on that review showed an extra file in the pane (`corpus-evict/a-target.js` and `src/added.js` turned up in two unrelated specs of one full run). `cleanupAllComments` and `cleanupComments` now also remove the review's context files, through `page.request` so that a routed context-files API cannot hide the real rows from the cleanup.
- H54. **On main the pointer gate for a background upgrade is geometric and a file can move out from under a parked pointer.** `_isPointerInsideFile` compares the last pointer position with the file's box. When a file above is upgraded and grows, the file under the pointer moves down, the pointer is no longer "inside" it, and its upgrade lands at once. `expansion.spec.js` parks the pointer on the first file for that reason. The same race puts a gutter-button container where a file used to be, on top of another file's button, which then takes the click: the "subtree intercepts pointer events" failures of `comment-crud` and `comments-review`. `hoverDiffLine` and `hoverSplitDiffLine` now wait for the diff to settle (`waitForDiffSettled`: no upgrade queued or in flight, every file displaying its current diff) before pointing at a row. That removed almost all of them (392 of 392 at eight workers; one occurrence in three full runs afterwards).
- H55. **On main, file positions are estimates until each file has rendered once.** The lazy placeholders are sized from the patch; tops moved by up to 2,000 px during the first scroll pass over the 60-file corpus and not at all from the second pass on (`scroll-landing.spec.js`). A smooth scroll that crosses files which have not rendered yet lands off its target (the chat link case in H51; `ScrollUtils.scrollIntoViewStable` corrects for it where it is used). Landing measurements in the golden dump are therefore taken on files that have settled, wait for the application's navigation to stop correcting (40 identical frames), and the sidebar-jump target is the file in the middle of the pane, not the last one: the last file's landing depended on the scroll extent below it and differed between two identical dumps (59 px and 424 px, edge corpus, PR mode).
- H56. **A collapsed file keeps its rows in the DOM on main** until its next re-render (`PierreBridge.setCollapsed` only sets the vendor option; the wrapper's `collapsed` class hides the body). `rowCount` in `diff-dom.js` counts rows that have a box, which is what "shows no rows" means on either engine.
- H57. The E2E test server answers `GET /api/local/:id/check-stale` with "not stale", as it always did for PR mode. Before, every Local page load found the fixture review stale (its directory does not exist), refreshed, failed with a 500 and showed an error toast, and "Analyze" asked whether to refresh first. The two allow-list entries for that 500 are gone. A spec about staleness routes the endpoint itself.
- H58. Other measured behaviours of main that later phases should know: the theme preference defaults to `system` and the toggle cycles light, dark, system; the two GitHub palettes share the comment grey; a worker script that fails to load falls back at once (rows and tokens within 320 ms) and the vendor logs one `Worker error: Event` line; the empty diff says "No files changed" in PR mode and "No changes in <scope> scope. ..." in Local mode; a pointer press in the padding left of a row's text starts no text selection; headless Chromium's clipboard is not the system clipboard (checked with `pbpaste` before and after `text-selection.spec.js`); the AI panel is collapsed at the suite's 1280 px viewport and a spec expands it (`window.aiPanel.expand()`) before clicking a finding.
- H59. **On the current engine a file that rendered before the highlight worker pool was ready is rebuilt from scratch when the pool reports ready** (`PierreBridge._handleWorkerStats`, `pierre-bridge.js:478-510`): a new vendor instance, new rows, and every revealed context range applied again (`_rebuildFileInstance`, line 688). The rebuild runs one animation frame after the pool's `initialized` report. Alone that is about 40 ms after the first rows are on screen; in a full eight-worker E2E run it can be later than the first few hundred milliseconds of a test. This was the cause of the `inline-feedback-context.spec.js:110` flake (in 2 of 6 full runs of session 2, never alone): the test put the base diff back by hand and the rebuild revealed the range again, for good. Proven by holding the worker script back: without the wait, line 150 is hidden right after `redisplayBaseDiff` and visible again after the pool becomes ready; with it, `redisplayBaseDiff` itself runs after the rebuild and the line stays hidden (`ifc-flake-proof.txt` in the phase-2 evidence). `diff-dom.js` now has `D.engineSettled()` (pool ready and no file still waiting for its rebuild; a pool that failed to start is settled), and every "settled" wait requires it: `waitForContentUpgrade`, `waitForFileSettled`, `waitForDiffSettled`, and `redisplayBaseDiff` before it acts. The same rebuild re-creates rows under a test that holds one, which is the other half of H43. Under `CodeView` nothing is mounted until the pool is ready (H23), so phase 5's adapter answers `engineSettled` from the pool alone. *(Fix round.)* The rebuild has a second victim, proven the same way: a manual expansion (the vendor's own expand control, `expandHunkBy` in the specs) lives in the vendor instance's expansion map, the rebuild creates a new instance, and the expanded lines are hidden again for good. With the worker script held, line 85 of the scenario's file is visible right after the expansion and hidden after the pool becomes ready, one rebuild in between, in both modes (`round3/probe-ifc77-before-fix.log`). That is the cause of the flake of `inline-feedback-context.spec.js` "comments on manually expanded lines survive another off-hunk anchor" (the phase 1 log's `:81`, 5 of 144 on the phase 0 tree at eight workers; `:77` now), which session 3 had not connected to this hazard. `expandHunkBy` now waits for the file's upgraded diff to be displayed on a settled engine before it expands, which is also the earliest moment the control it stands in for exists (`round3/probe-ifc77-after-fix.log`: the expansion survives; 80 of 80 for the spec at four workers). On main a reader who expands context in the first moments of a page, before the pool is ready, loses that expansion the same way; the window is about 40 ms on an idle machine.
- H60. **On main an external review thread can be drawn in its file's comment zone, styled as a thread that could not be anchored, instead of on its line, on one ordinary page load.** *(Rewritten in the fix round after verification round 1; the first version of this entry described the trigger wrongly, see the end of the entry.)*
  - *What happens.* `ExternalCommentManager._renderThreadPierre` adds the thread as an annotation and looks for its card in the same task (`external-comment-manager.js:637-653`). When the card is not there it removes the annotation again and renders the file-level fallback (`external-comment-row--file-fallback`). Nothing renders the threads again until the next thread reload (the panel's refresh, a whitespace toggle, a pull-request refresh, an analysis completing), so the thread stays in the zone with its line on screen.
  - *When the card is not there.* Two conditions, both needed. (1) The thread list is answered while the anchor file has a new diff that is not displayed yet: the content upgrade has been taken by the application (`baseMetadata` set) and the vendor is still showing the patch-only diff while the worker highlights the upgraded one (F5). The vendor keeps the old diff on screen only if that old diff had its own highlight result (`DiffHunksRenderer.canRenderDiff`); a file still showing plain rows is replaced at once. (2) The bridge has published annotations for that file since the file's last full render. While a diff awaits display the vendor draws the displayed diff with the annotations of the last adopted render (`FileDiff.prepareRenderData` → `getAnnotationsForDiff`: the pending set belongs to the new diff), so an annotation set in the window is not drawn until the swap. The exception is an accident of aliasing: a full render hands the vendor `fileState.annotations` itself, `_pushAnnotation` pushes onto that same array, and as long as no `_updateAnnotations` (which hands over a sorted copy) has run since, the vendor's "last drawn" list is the bridge's own array and already contains the new annotation. Any comment or suggestion load that touched the file after its body rendered ends the aliasing.
  - *Measured.* By the verifier, on plain loads of the N11 page at eight workers: 3 of 120 ended with the thread demoted; state at the moment the fallback class was added: `baseMetadata` set, `getRenderedDiff() !== fileDiff`, no upgrade in flight, pool ready (`verify/probe-ext746.log` in the phase-2 evidence). In the fix round, with the window held open (file contents held until the patch-only diff is highlighted, then worker results held; `round2/probe-h60-deterministic.log`, `probe-h60-variants.log`): thread list answered in the window after one comment reload: file level 10 of 10; answered in the window without a reload: file level 1 of 10 on the eviction target and 0 of 10 on `src/utils.js`, and the one was the execution in which the vendor's annotation array was already not the bridge's; answered after the diff had settled: on its line 20 of 20. A thread on a line hidden between two hunks was on its line in 20 of 20 plain and pinned loads (the reveal is a full render).
  - *User-visible scope on main.* Any page load of a pull request with external threads, not only a re-render: the window is one worker round trip per file (tens of milliseconds on an idle machine, the 760 to 1,000 ms of F5 and G12 for a 300,000-character file), and comments or suggestions arriving between the file's first render and its upgrade are the normal case. The same same-task lookup exists in `TourRenderer._mountStopPierre` and after a comment save (plan 3.9); neither was measured here.
  - *What the specs do.* `rerender-overlays.spec.js` "a thread list answered while a file's upgraded diff awaits display" holds the window open and asserts the outcome through the named expectation `THREAD_ADDED_WHILE_DIFF_AWAITS_DISPLAY` (`'file-level'` today, H51): demoted, still demoted with the upgraded diff on screen and the line in view, back on its line after the panel's refresh. Every other scenario of this phase that asserts where a thread is drawn (N9's whitespace and refresh scenario, N11's far-file scenario) holds the thread list until `waitForDiffSettled`, at the first load and at every reload. `external-comments.spec.js`, main's own spec, loads unpinned; its known flake "AI / user / external rows all render on the same line in the right order" (once in the phase 1 runs, `not-settled` after 10 s) fits this mechanism and was not proven to be it.
  - *For phase 3.* Remove the cause, not the window: the manager must decide from `isLineVisible` and must not read "no card in the DOM" as "line not rendered" (plan 3.9); then flip the constant to `'line'`. The scenario to keep green afterwards is the unpinned one.
  - *Done in phase 3.* `_renderThreadPierre` asks `bridge.isLineVisible(file, line, side)` after `ensureLinesVisible` and before it adds anything: shown, it adds the annotation and never looks for the card; not shown (no hunk or revealed range holds the line, or the file is collapsed, which is what main's missing card meant in those cases), it renders the file-level fallback without adding an annotation. The window is still there and no longer matters: the held-window scenario of `rerender-overlays.spec.js` ends with the thread on its line once the upgraded diff is displayed, and the unpinned `external-comments.spec.js` passed in every full run of the phase. The same change was made to the tour (`_mountStopPierre` takes the stop's element from the bridge; "not attached" is a mounted stop) and to the comment save (`findCommentCard`).
  - *Correction of the first version.* It said the demotion needed "two re-renders in quick succession", named `loadAndRender` joining the in-flight promise (`external-comment-manager.js:117`) as the mechanism, and recorded that "a pending upgrade alone is not the trigger (8 of 8)". The 8 executions had the aliased array of condition (2), which that session did not know about; the toggles it used to provoke the defect work because each re-render is followed by a comment reload and a new upgrade, which is conditions (1) and (2) again. The join exists in the code and was never shown to matter.
- H61. Counts in the review panel, as measured for N11: the User segment counts every comment the panel lists, so a posted comment is counted there and is not "pending" (`getPendingCommentCount()` and the review button's `has-comments` are the pending ones); with "Show dismissed" on it also counts every comment ever dismissed on the review, which in an E2E worker is every comment an earlier test cleaned up. A spec reads the segment count only where it knows the review's whole history.
- H62. **Isolation between E2E tests cannot rest on each describe's own cleanup hook.** *(Fix round.)* A worker's database is shared by every spec file that worker runs. The rewrite of `split-view.spec.js` in this phase lost the `afterEach` of its "one-sided files" describe, and for the rest of that worker's life both fixture reviews showed a third file (`src/added.js` as a context file, with two comments), the leak H53 had declared closed. A census of what every test leaves behind (a temporary fixture reading the worker's database after each test, run over the whole suite once the split-view hook was back; `round2/leak-census-full.jsonl`, 840 records) showed the missing hook was one of many. 310 of the 840 tests ended with rows on a fixture review, 171 of them with active user comments. Thirteen describes saved user comments and left them: on main already `comment-crud` "Multi-line Selection", "Comment Persistence" and "Comment API Integration", `comment-crud-advanced` "Comment Display", `edited-posted-comments`, `comment-publication`, `external-comments` and `analysis-history`, and, added in this phase, the Local variants of the first four, which did to review 2 what main does to review 1 (up to 5 active comments on each review at once). Five Local describes left a context-file row per commented file: in Local mode the server finds no file of the fixture review in a diff, because it asks git in a directory that does not exist, so every comment adds one, and `file-comments*`' own cleanup removes comments, not context files. Fourteen describes left AI rows (up to 95 on review 1 by the end of a worker). Which later spec saw them depended on how Playwright had distributed the files. Now the fixture does it: `fixtureReviewsLeftClean` (`tests/e2e/fixtures.js`) retires every user comment, deletes every AI suggestion and analysis run and removes every context file of reviews 1 and 2 after every test, after the browser context is closed, and fails the test if a row is left; `tests/e2e/fixture-isolation.spec.js` proves it in both modes. The per-describe hooks stay (the split-view one was restored) and are no longer what isolation depends on. Not covered, and as on main: reviews a spec creates itself (a worker ends with up to 14), settings, `localStorage`.
- H63. **The server writes a comment's context file after it has answered the save** (`src/routes/reviews.js:149-156`, "fire and forget"; for a Local review after a git child process has failed). Nothing orders that write against a test that has been told the comment is saved. Measured, the row was there by the time the page had the answer in 16 of 16 saves at four workers (`round3/probe-deferred-context.log`), so no failure is attributed to it; the reset of H62 waits for that work to finish all the same (`whenBackgroundWorkDone` in `tests/e2e/test-server.js`, a wrapper around the one deferred call, installed before the route module loads), and the isolation spec fails if a save stops coming through the wrapper. A cleanup that a spec runs itself right after a save has no such wait.
- H64. **A positioned Playwright click on an element taller than the viewport cannot recover from one miss.** After a first attempt that finds the target "not stable" (here: the diff container still changing height while a suggestion card and the review panel settle), Playwright retries with forced scroll alignments of the target (end, centre, start). On `#diff-container` with the eviction corpus (about 78,000 px) those scroll to 78,575, 39,317 and 59 px; at each the point (5, 5) is off screen or under the sticky toolbar, so the click never lands and the test spends its 30 s (the verifier's trace, `verify/probe-nav288-traced.log`; 3 of 180 executions). `offscreen-mutations.spec.js` now takes keyboard focus with a click on the toolbar, as `expansion.spec.js` already did. Phase 5 makes `#diff-container` the scroll root with a fixed height, which removes the geometry, not the rule.
- H65. **`pr-page.spec.js` "should scroll to file in diff when clicked" asserted the wrong element, on main too.** It clicked the second sidebar entry and expected `page.locator('[data-file-name]').nth(1)` in the viewport within 2 s. The second `[data-file-name]` of the page is the first file's comment zone, which the scroll moves out of the viewport about 100 ms after the click and which stays out (final top -221 px; the same on the phase 1 tree: 89 to 104 ms, -290 px; `verify/probe-prpage239-*.log`). The test passed when Playwright's first poll came within those 100 ms, which it stopped doing reliably once the suite had 839 tests (failed in 2 of the verifier's 3 full runs; alone, 30 of 30 for the spec and 10 of 10 for the test). It now asserts the file that was clicked: its header ends flush under the toolbar or the diff is at the end of its scroll range with the header on screen, the scroll position has moved, and the clicked entry is the active one.
- H66. **The suite's baseline "known flake" was a deterministic defect of main meeting leaked rows.** `gutter-buttons.spec.js` "clear when the pointer leaves the diff (PR mode)" (`:90` on main, `:83` since session 1) is the one failure of the baseline run on untouched main and recurs through the run tables of phases 0 to 2 in this ledger. It did not fail in any of the twelve full runs made after the fixture began resetting the reviews (H62). The reason, measured (`round3/probe-gutter83*.log`, `gutter83-stale.png`): with the six suggestions of the test server's mock analysis on the review when the page loads, the gutter button of the last hovered row is still showing after the pointer has moved to the page header, after a second pointer move outside the diff and 1.5 s later: 27 of 27, in PR and Local mode, unified and split. On an empty review it is cleared (15 of 15), and so it is with one comment or one suggestion on the hovered line, the line above, the second hunk, the other file, or at file level (119 of 119). What in the six rows does it (two of them share a line; one is a praise on the other file; they come with an analysis run) was not isolated. The test asserted a clean review's behaviour and met that state whenever an earlier spec in its worker had run an analysis and not cleaned up (fourteen describes did, H62); in the census run the one failure of the run is the one `gutter-buttons` test that started with such rows (6 AI rows and a comment on review 1), and the five that started clean passed. The clean-review test is unchanged and now always runs on a clean review. The defect is kept in the suite by a second test, "after the pointer leaves the diff, with the suggestions of an analysis on the review", in both modes, through the ninth named expectation `GUTTER_BUTTON_STAYS_WITH_ANALYSIS_SUGGESTIONS` (H51): 20 of 20 at eight workers, and in every full run since. Every earlier statement in this ledger that calls `gutter-buttons:90` / `:83` a flake of the fallback positioner under load is superseded by this entry. The same question was not asked of the other rotating failures of the fallback gutter (H54's intercepted clicks in `comment-crud` and `comments-review`); none occurred in the twelve runs.

**Found in phase 3**

The D2 amendment of the plan numbers its own hazards "H62 to H75, continuing after the ledger's H61". This ledger already had H62 to H66 by then, and H67 to H78 after phase 3, so those numbers name different things in the two documents. **In this ledger the amendment's hazards are H79 to H92** (transferred in phase 4, below under "Added by the D2 amendment"). To resolve a reference in `PLAN.md` or `D2-AMENDMENT.md`: plan H62 = H79, H63 = H80, H64 = H81, H65 = H82, H66 = H83, H67 = H84, H68 = H85, H69 = H86, H70 = H87, H71 = H88, H72 = H89, H73 = H90, H74 = H91, H75 = H92. H62 to H78 written in this ledger, in the phase logs and in the evidence of phases 1 to 3 always mean the ledger's own entries.

- H67. **On the current engine the collapsed state of a file is the `collapsed` class of its wrapper, and only that.** A file is collapsed when it is generated, viewed, or in `collapsedFiles` (`pr.js`, `renderFileDiff`), and `collapsedFiles` only holds what the reader collapsed. After the reader expands a generated or viewed file the class is gone and no data says "expanded", so nothing but the wrapper can answer. `PRManager.isFileCollapsed(path)` is therefore the single reader and asks the wrapper (data only when a file of the diff has no wrapper, which this engine never produces); `setFileCollapsed(path, bool)` is the single writer. Phase 5 has to give the state a complete data model (the entries seed it, `hooks.hostState` stamps it) before `isFileCollapsed` can stop asking an element; the callers do not change.
- H68. **`_rerenderAllOverlays` reached external comments in Local mode.** Its GET-only branch calls `externalCommentManager.loadAndRender()` directly, and only `_loadExternalComments` (the sync branch) checked `PAIR_REVIEW_LOCAL_MODE`. No Local path called the helper on main, so it never ran. With the three Local re-render paths on the helper it would have fetched a pull request's threads for a local review; the helper now returns after the comments and suggestions in Local mode (unit test in `pr-external-comments-wiring.test.js`).
- H69. **A card can be resolved and still have no place on screen.** `getAnnotationElement(file, id, { create: true })` returns an element for any annotation the bridge holds, including one whose line the diff does not show (a suggestion on a line that could not be revealed). Navigation must not scroll to it or report success: `whenAnnotationRendered` answers null at once when `isLineVisible` says the line is not shown (no four-second wait before "Could not reveal this finding"), and `revealInDiff` treats an element that is not attached after the wait as "nothing to reveal". Under `CodeView` step 3 of `revealInDiff` (`scrollToLine`) mounts the item first, so "not attached" will then mean less; phase 7 re-reads both rules.
- H70. **"An update is in flight" is data now** (`PostedComment.setUpdateBusy` keeps a set, so a card drawn while the update runs shows it). On main the busy state lived in the button and was cleared as a side effect of re-rendering the note. A flow that sets it must clear it itself: `updatePostedCommentOnHost` does, before it re-renders the note. A new flow that forgets leaves every card of that comment busy for the life of the page.
- H71. **A suggestion card keeps reader state that the suggestion's data does not have**: an adopted suggestion the reader chose to show again is not collapsed, with status `adopted`; and when its adopting comment is then deleted the data says `dismissed` while the card, on main, stays open with the label "Hide". Two rules follow, and phase 6 must port both or move "shown although the data says collapsed" into data.
  1. *The sync applies transitions only.* `SuggestionManager.syncSuggestionElement` compares the suggestion's effective status with the status the card last showed (a `WeakMap` of card to status) and touches the card only when they differ. A sync that re-stamped the card from data on every draw would collapse a shown adopted suggestion at the next re-render of its file.
  2. *Only a draw syncs; a lookup never does.* `PierreBridge._syncAnnotationElement` runs inside `_renderAnnotation`, which is the vendor's `renderAnnotation` callback and the build step of `getAnnotationElement(…, { create: true })` for a card that does not exist yet. `getAnnotationElement` hands back a card that exists exactly as it is. The adopt, dismiss, restore and comment-delete flows look the card up (`findSuggestionCard`), update it themselves the way main does, and then call `PRManager._setSuggestionStatus`, which writes the status to the data and records the card as showing it (`markSuggestionSynced`), so the next draw finds no transition to apply. In each flow nothing draws between the write of the status and that record: `dismissSuggestion` and `restoreSuggestion` write it inside `_setSuggestionStatus` itself; `_notifyAdoption` registers the adopting comment and calls it in the same task; in `deleteUserComment` the review panel writes `dismissed` to the suggestion object it shares with the annotation (`AIPanel.updateFindingStatus`) two statements before `_setSuggestionStatus`, with no bridge call between them (the re-render caused by removing the comment's annotation happens before, while the status is still `adopted`).

  The first version of this phase ran the sync on a cache hit inside `getAnnotationElement` as well, so every `findSuggestionCard` and `_setSuggestionStatus` applied the transition in the middle of the flow that was about to handle the card. Measured by the verifier (`verify/probe-suggestion-sync-trace.log`): in `deleteUserComment` the lookup at the top of the suggestion branch collapsed a shown adopted suggestion (label "Show") where main leaves it open ("Hide"), and `dismissSuggestion` left `data-hidden-for-adoption="false"` on the card where main leaves the attribute absent. Both are back to main's behaviour; see "Round 2" in section 11, phase 3. A transition is still applied by a draw for a card no flow handled: one whose status changed in the data while it was not looked up (unit test "a card the flows never saw is still corrected when the diff draws it").
  3. *The comment-restore scenario (phase 4).* A comment that adopted a suggestion is deleted (the suggestion becomes `dismissed`, the flow updates the card) and later restored. `restoreUserComment` reloads the comments and touches no card, so the data says `adopted` (an active comment has the suggestion as its parent) while the card still shows "dismissed". Nothing records the card as showing that status, so the next draw of the suggestion's annotation applies the transition and the card becomes "adopted" (collapsed, `data-hidden-for-adoption="true"`). On the current engine such a draw happens when the file's diff changes (a reveal, a re-render) or the page is reloaded. Under `CodeView` every remount of the file is such a draw, so the card changes when the reader scrolls away and comes back. What does not draw it: adding the restored comment's own annotation on the suggestion's line (the vendor re-invokes `renderAnnotation` only for entries at or after the insertion point of the sorted array, and a comment sorts after the suggestion of its line). **Decision for the new bridge:** the same rule as the current one, unchanged: a draw syncs transitions, a lookup never does. The end state is the one main reaches after its next re-render or a reload, which is as close to main as the data allows; the difference is only that it is reached sooner. The other half of H71 holds under remounts: a card the reader re-opened, recorded with `markSuggestionSynced`, stays open across a remount because the cached element is re-attached and the sync finds no transition. Both are pinned by `bridge/annotations.spec.js` "a suggestion whose status changed while its card was untouched ...", with the real `SuggestionManager`. **For phase 6:** if a card that changes by itself on scroll is not wanted, `restoreUserComment` should write the suggestion's status through `_setSuggestionStatus` at once, as the adopt and dismiss flows do; that is a change to main's behaviour and belongs with the annotation cut-over, not with the bridge.
- H72. **Two unit-test bridges that "slotted" a `<div>` on `addAnnotation` were what kept H60 green**, in `external-comment-manager.test.js` and `tour-renderer.test.js`; the AI panel and navigator tests stubbed the pieces of the navigation sequence instead of running it. Both kinds are gone. Modules that talk to the bridge are tested against the production `PierreBridge` without the vendor (`tests/utils/pierre-bridge-harness.js`: annotation list, visibility oracle and element cache are real, the vendor instance is a spy, nothing is ever attached), and modules that navigate against the production `PRManager` methods (`tests/utils/page-manager.js`). Phase 4 replaces the bridge under the harness; the harness's file record (`instance.fileDiff.hunks`) is the old bridge's shape and changes with it.
- H73. **`findFileElement` after the audit** (plan step 9). Call sites on this branch and what `null` means at each: `_canonicalFilePath` (added in round 2, H78: no wrapper, so the path is looked for among the changed files), `isFileCollapsed` (falls back to data), `setFileCollapsed` (writes the state, skips the DOM), `ensureFileBodyRendered` (path normalisation only), `_materializeDeferredDiff` and `scrollToFile` (need the wrapper they act on; both are replaced in phase 5), `expandForSuggestion`, the table branches of `ensureLinesVisible` and `_loadUserComments`, `SuggestionManager`'s row branch and `ExternalCommentManager._findDiffLineRow` / `_findFileWrapper` (all reached only for a file the bridge does not hold, i.e. a context file on this engine; `null` there is "no wrapper to put a row in"). The one site that read `null` as "the file is not in the diff" and told the reader so (`SuggestionManager`, "File … not found in diff") now asks `PRManager.isFileInDiffPane(path)`, which answers from `changedFilesByPath` and `contextFiles`. The table branches are deleted in phase 8 (plan 3.18); until then they must not be handed a changed file, which `pierreBridge.files.has` guarantees only for files whose body has rendered (H12).
- H74. **During a pull-request refresh the old diff is still in the document, hidden.** On main `refreshPR` wiped the container before fetching; now `_setDiffStatus(…, { hideScroller: true })` hides it and `renderDiff` replaces it. Anything that runs while the refresh is in flight (a comment reload from a WebSocket event, a navigation) finds the old wrappers and bridge records, as it does during a whitespace toggle on main. If the refresh ends without a render (`data.success` false) the `finally` takes its own message down and the old diff is back, where main left "Refreshing pull request..." forever. `#diff-status` reuses the container's box (`class="diff-container diff-status"`); the `hidden` attribute on either needs the explicit `display: none` rule added to `pr.css`, because nothing else sets their display and a later rule that does would make `hidden` inert.
- H75. **The zone registry has an explicit lifecycle on this engine.** `FileCommentManager.createFileCommentsZone` registers; `PRManager._removeRenderedFiles` (the whole-diff teardown, also used by Local mode's empty-diff path) forgets every zone; the three places that remove a context wrapper (`loadContextFiles` twice, `ensureContextFile` once) forget that wrapper's zone. A new place that removes a wrapper without `releaseZone` leaves a zone that every sweep still visits and that `findCommentCard` can still answer from. Under `CodeView` the record's lifecycle owns this (`renderAll`, `reconcile`).
- H76. **A tour stop whose file has a diff awaiting display is mounted now**, where main skipped it and probed the next stop ("pierre annotation did not slot"). `scrollToStop` goes through `revealInDiff` and so waits for the stop to be attached (up to four seconds, cancelled when the tour is left or reopened) before it scrolls; it returns a promise that `pr.js` does not await, as before. `TourRenderer._mounted` can hold an element that is not attached.
- H77. **Three E2E tests are processor-bound and ran at 60% of the default 30-second budget in every full run since phase 2.** `chat-file-links.spec.js` "a link to a line in a file the reader has seen and scrolled far away from ..." and "the same in split layout", and `scroll-landing.spec.js` "file positions and the scroll extent do not change between one scroll pass and the next", read through the 60-file corpus two or three times. Measured: 7.1 to 8.2 s alone at one worker (12 executions); 16.3 to 19.5 s in the three final full runs of phase 2 and 17.4 to 19.5 s in full runs of this phase, at eight workers; and once 31.0 s, a timeout inside `scrollThroughDiff`, in a full run during which the machine's load average went from 5 to 16 (`e2e-tree-fc082a7d-run1.log` in the phase-3 evidence; the same test's Local twin took 21.4 s in that run, and 17.7 and 18.5 s in the two runs after it on the same tree). The time is the same on the phase-2 tree and on this one, so it is not something this phase made slower. The three tests now call `test.slow()` (three times the budget); what they assert is unchanged. A test that needs most of its budget on an ordinary run fails on a busy one; every other test of the suite took 9.0 s or less in the same full run. *(Phase 7: measured again on both engines, as this ledger asked.)* In a full eight-worker run on `codeview` the two chat-link tests took 15.7 to 18.6 s and the scroll-pass test 8.2 and 21.5 s (`e2e-codeview-whole-wip1.log` in the phase-7 evidence); on the default engine 17.2 to 19.9 s and 7.1 and 12.9 s (the phase-6 verification run). The virtualized viewer does not make them fast enough to fit the default budget with room to spare (they read through 60 files two or three times; the time is the test's scrolling and the highlighter's work, not the layout), so all three `test.slow()` calls stay. No other test of the suite has one, and the slowest other test of the `codeview` run took 6.5 s.
- H78. **State keyed by a file's path must be addressed by the path the diff pane knows the file by.** `collapsedFiles`, the bridge's file records, the lazy bodies and a wrapper's `data-file-name` are keyed by a changed file's `file.file`. Navigation does not hold that form: the server validates a suggestion's path by normalized comparison and stores it as the model wrote it (`src/ai/analyzer.js`, the path validation of parsed suggestions), so `./src/a.js` reaches the page for the changed file `src/a.js`; external threads and tour stops can differ the same way. `findFileElement` accepts those forms ('./', '/', git rename syntax), so a function that finds the wrapper through it and writes its state under the caller's string addresses two different things. On main the two navigation callers canonicalized by hand (`toggleFileCollapse(wrapper.dataset.fileName)`); when phase 3 replaced them with `revealInDiff` → `setFileCollapsed(file)` the hand-made step was lost and a collapsed file reached by such a path had its wrapper class removed while `collapsedFiles` and the bridge kept it collapsed: the header showed it open, and the next re-render of the file drew no line. Now `PRManager._canonicalFilePath(path)` resolves any accepted form to the canonical path (the exact key of `changedFilesByPath`; else the `data-file-name` of the wrapper `findFileElement` finds; else a changed file matching with `normalizeFilePath` / `pathsMatch`, the tolerance `isFileInDiffPane` has), and `isFileCollapsed` and `setFileCollapsed` resolve their argument through it before anything else, so the state key, the per-path request counter, the wrapper and the bridge call are one path. `ensureFileBodyRendered` already did the same for its map. Rule for phases 5 to 7: every new function that keys state by path (`setFileCollapsed` on the data model, `scrollToFile`, `revealInDiff` step 3, the item id resolver) takes any accepted form and resolves it once at its entry; a caller never canonicalizes. The context-file resolver the old attempt needed (`_pierreItemIdForPath`) is the same rule for item ids.

**Added by the D2 amendment** (transferred in phase 4; plan numbers in brackets, mapping above H67)

- H79 [plan H62]. **Two writers of the list.** `renderAll` (from `renderDiff`) and `reconcile` (from `_syncContextItems`). Both go through `_writeList`. A `reconcile` computed for an older render must not run: `_syncContextItems` is only reached from `loadContextFiles` after its two generation checks, and `reconcile` itself drops an entry list whose changed-file ids are not the records' changed-file ids.
- H80 [plan H63]. **`loadContextFiles` has five callers and no guard on main:** `renderDiff`, the `review:context_files_changed` listener (debounced, deferred while hidden), `removeContextFile`, `ensureContextFile` (after a PATCH and after a POST). Sequences that must come out right, each a unit test of `load-context-files` (phase 5): two loads in flight (the later one wins, the earlier one stops after its next `await`); a dismiss while the same file's contents are being fetched (the rows of the later load have no such path, so no item appears); a `renderDiff` during a load (the load stops on `_renderGen`, the one `renderDiff` started finishes); a contents fetch that fails (no item, no cache entry, the next load retries); the dismiss button's sequential deletes, each followed by a load (the item goes when its last row goes; no intermediate list write when rows remain, because the ids are unchanged). **Amended in phase 5's fix round (section 6, item 19): what the caller that awaits an overtaken load finds is part of the contract. An overtaken load settles when the load that overtook it has; the unit tests of "a load that is overtaken" in `load-context-files.test.js` and N5 "a push that arrives while the tour is adding a stop's file ..." (both modes) pin it.**
- H81 [plan H64]. **A list replace discards what the vendor instances held.** Per item: the manual expansion map (folded first, F24), measured heights (the restore is a line target, which does not depend on them), a pending diff swap (the new instance renders the published metadata from scratch). Per viewer: the line selection, the focused element and its caret, the hovered row, a scroll target in flight, a text selection being dragged. The first three are restored by `_writeList`; a `reconcile` the user did not ask for waits for the last two to end. Each is an L2 assertion. `renderAll` restores position, selection and focus the same way but not expansions, as on main.
- H82 [plan H65]. An item whose metadata was re-keyed by the fold is drawn without tokens until the worker answers (F24). Only records with a non-empty expansion map are re-keyed. Re-using the old key for the folded metadata would avoid the plain frames and is not done: F7's rule (a new key for every derived metadata) is simpler than the exception.
- H83 [plan H66]. **`pierreBridge.files.has(path)` is true for a context file.** That is what routes context files into the existing annotation paths, and it is wrong wherever the call meant "is a changed file". Sites to re-read in phase 6 (the H12 list): the content-upgrade candidates (safe: it filters the changed files), hunk-summary anchor registration and `_summaryHashesByFile` (must skip kind `context`), `_removeRenderedSuggestions` and the comment clear loop (correct for both kinds), `ExternalCommentManager._isPierreFile`, `TourRenderer._isPierreFile` and `prepareStop`'s `existingWrapper` test (a DOM lookup that an unmounted changed file fails; it must ask `fileKind`), `ChatPanel._scrollToLine`, `comment-manager.js:321`. The rule: "is a changed file" is `fileKind(path) === 'diff'`.
- H84 [plan H67]. **Side on a context item.** Every annotation is published on the additions side (F21); the stored side stays in `data`. Places that derive a side from the DOM or from the vendor: the gutter (`getHoveredLine().side` → `RIGHT`), `resolveClickTarget` (a range on a context item is `RIGHT` to `RIGHT`), the file-annotation side (always `additions` for kind `context`), the E2E adapter. A comment stored `LEFT` on a file that later left the diff is the case that would silently disappear (the vendor lays out no deletions-side annotation on a one-sided item); N5 gets a scenario for it in phase 5.
- H85 [plan H68]. **State keys.** `collapsedFiles` and `viewedFiles` hold `context:<path>` for a context entry and the bare path for a changed file, in storage that outlives the page. Writers and readers: `setFileCollapsed`, the viewed toggle, `_buildPaneEntries` (initial collapse), `hooks.hostState`, `_syncFileHeader`, `renderFileItem`, `_scrubRemovedContextState`, `saveViewedState`. All resolve the key through `_fileStateKey`. A path that changes kind on a re-render (a context file enters the diff, or leaves it) must pick up the other key's state, not carry its own over: N5 "marking a context file viewed" asserts the key, and the live drive does the scope change.
- H86 [plan H69]. **Three places read a context file's ranges from the DOM on main:** the dismiss button (`tbody.context-chunk[data-context-id]`), `ChatPanel._handleFileLinkClick`, `scrollToContextFile`. On the `codeview` engine there are no chunks; all three read `contextFiles` and the bridge's visibility oracle.
- H87 [plan H70]. **`pierre-context.js` is shared by both bridges during phases 4 to 7**, and F23's correction changes the metadata the old bridge publishes too. `FileDiff` has no layout that reads the line starts, but it does write `data-line-index` from them. Phase 4 proved the correction harmless with the full default-engine E2E run and an unchanged golden dump before anything else in the phase was built on it (section 11, phase 4).
- H88 [plan H71]. **`CONTEXT_CSS` and the `type: 'new'` override depend on vendor internals** that are not API: the separator markup and the vendor code sites of F21. D6 pins the version; the L1 tests and G13 fail first on a bump; the fallback is to drop the override (context files are then drawn in both columns in split layout, every function still works, and that change of look goes to the user). `CONTEXT_CSS` must also keep winning against `ANNOTATION_CSS`'s one-sided rules when either is edited: they live in one file (`pierre-styles.js`) and G13 loads both.
- H89 [plan H72]. **Duplicated paths.** Context-file code is in `pr.js` only; `local.js` has none, so PR mode and Local mode share one implementation and differ only in who calls `renderDiff`. The duplication that does exist is temporary: the table renderer on the default engine and items on `codeview` during phases 5 to 7. The switch-site test bounds it and N5 and N8 run on both.
- H90 [plan H73]. **Contents can go stale.** The endpoint reads the file at the review's head. `renderDiff` (refresh, new commits, scope change) reloads the contents of every context path and publishes only the ones whose text changed (`seq`); between two renders the cache is trusted, as the drawn rows are on main. A comment on line N of a context file whose contents then change keeps its line number, as on main.
- H91 [plan H74]. **Unchanged-file findings (#602)** are not on this branch (H50). If they land on main first, their creator of context rows and their suggestions on context lines arrive through the paths the plan describes for context items (3.15.4, 3.15.6); the rebase adds their N5 scenario on both engines before phase 8.
- H92 [plan H75]. **The first list on a page load has no context items** (the rows are fetched after the first `renderAll`, as the wrappers are drawn after the diff on main). Anything that runs between the two and addresses a context path (`loadUserComments`, a suggestion display, a deep link to a context line) finds no record and skips it, as it finds no wrapper on main; `loadContextFiles` reloads the comments when a context record was created. A navigation target in a context file that arrives in that window is retried by its caller's existing "file not found" path (`ensureContextFile`).

**Found in phase 4**

- H93. **Every script of the app's pages is a classic script: a top-level `const`, `let` or `class` is a binding shared by all of them, and a second declaration of the same name is a `SyntaxError` that stops the second file from loading at all.** A `const COLLAPSED_CONTEXT_THRESHOLD` added to `pierre-context.js` collided with the one in `pierre-bridge.js`; the bridge did not load, no diff rendered, and the first E2E run of the phase had 89 failures in its first minute. No unit test can see it (each file is `require`d into its own module scope). The four new modules are wrapped in a function, and `pierre-context.js` keeps its threshold as a default parameter. Phase 8's rename of the bridge must keep the wrapper or check every top-level name against the other scripts of `pr.html` and `local.html`.
- H94. **"The card is connected" is not "the card is on screen".** The vendor attaches the wrapper of an annotation whose line it does not draw (a line hidden in a gap), with no height (pinned in `vendor/annotations.spec.js` since phase 1). `whenAnnotationRendered` on the current bridge asks `isConnected` first and would hand such a card back; the new bridge asks the visibility oracle first and answers null at once (`bridge/annotations.spec.js`). Phase 7's `revealInDiff` must keep deciding from `isLineVisible`, never from the element.
- H95. **F23 needs a wrapped line to show.** The vendor keeps the measured height of a row that is taller than the estimate by the row's index in the file's layout, and takes that index from the hunk line starts. With starts that leave out the hunk's own hidden lines, a file whose rows are all of the estimated height stays exact through any expansion; with one wrapped line above, "Expand all" on the gap before the first range, after the gap between two ranges was expanded from both ends, leaves the item a row taller than the layout, for good (`vendor/context-items.spec.js`, the two "constraint" tests, which fail if the vendor stops depending on these fields). A test of layout exactness after expansions must have wrapped lines in the file, or it proves nothing; gate G13 and the F23 tests do.
- H96. **A row a flash was put on is replaced by the next update of its file.** Every update of an item replaces all its rows (F16): a line that was just revealed is drawn plain, flashed, and drawn again a worker round trip later without the class, which is main's `REVEALED_LINE_KEEPS_FLASH` defect (H51). The new bridge keeps the flash as data on the record for the length of the animation and puts it on the new row from `onPostRender`, continuing from where it was (a negative `animation-delay`). `scrollToLine` on a line of a file that was never mounted ends with the line flashed (`bridge/context-records.spec.js`, `bridge/view.spec.js`). Phase 7 flips `REVEALED_LINE_KEEPS_FLASH` on this.
- H97. **Files without a hunk are not all alike once their contents are known.** The plan's upgrade table says "zero hunks: none; mark upgraded". On main the upgrade re-diffs the two contents, which for a pure rename or a mode change gives a diff without hunks that holds the file's lines, so a line of such a file can be revealed afterwards (a comment or a finding on a renamed file). Marking the patch's metadata as the base would lose that: the patch holds no line, and a range merged into it names lines that do not exist. The new bridge has a third strategy, `'reparse'`: `parseDiffFromFile(oldFile, newFile)` is taken as the base when it has no hunk either (same text on both sides) and the contents are text; otherwise the patch's metadata stays and ranges are not merged into it. Nothing is published by the upgrade itself (the item is header-only either way). `bridge/contents.spec.js` reveals a line of the pure rename; whether main really reveals one was read from its code, not run, and phase 7 compares with main's golden as the plan asks.
- H98. **A list write scrolls, and the scroll events are the bridge's own.** `_writeList` restores the view with a scroll target, which fires `scroll` on the root a frame later; the rule "hide the gutter buttons on scroll until the pointer really moves" (F12) would then hide the buttons of the row under a resting pointer after every reconcile. The bridge ignores scroll events for 250 ms after a list write. A scroll correction the vendor makes later than that (heights settling far above) hides the buttons until the pointer moves, as any scroll does.
- H99. **The bridge treats `onPostRender` as "inside a render pass" too.** F4 found a flush from inside `onPostRender` clean; the page's `onItemLifecycle` hook runs there and may publish, and a flush nested in the vendor's pass was not something to rest on. A publish made from any of the four callbacks is flushed in one microtask; the "same task" guarantee therefore does not hold for a publish made from the lifecycle hook (`bridge/annotations.spec.js`, flush guard).
- H100. **During phases 4 to 7 the two bridges each have their own copy of patch parsing, the diff-position map, `getCodeFromLines` and the side helpers.** They are instance methods of the current bridge with unit tests of their own, and moving them would have meant rewriting those tests for an engine that is deleted in phase 8. The copies do the same thing (the new `computeDiffPositions` takes the parsed metadata only; the raw-patch fallback of the current one is not ported, because every patch is parsed once by `renderAll`). Flagged as duplication: a fix to one before phase 8 must be made to the other. The card renderers, the display order, the split layout pass and the CSS are not duplicated (shared modules).
- H101. **`pr.html` and `local.html` load two more scripts** (`pierre-styles.js`, `pierre-annotations.js`), before `pierre-bridge.js`. The plan's phase 4 says "not touched: HTML" and also "annotation renderers and `ANNOTATION_CSS` move out of the old bridge into the shared files so both bridges use one copy"; both cannot hold, and the second is what keeps the engines from drifting (H6). A unit test pins the order of the tags. `pierre-gutter.js` and `pierre-codeview-bridge.js` are not loaded by any page until phase 5.
- H102. **`pierre-codeview-bridge.js` is about 2,550 lines (roughly 27,000 tokens), over the repository's 20,000-token guideline.** About a third is documentation of rules that the old attempt broke silently. Phase 8 decides whether the diff-position and code-extraction part moves to a module of its own when the current bridge's copy is deleted (H100).
- H103. **A list write asked for from inside a viewer callback: the records change at once, the viewer's list when the pass has ended, and for that long the bridge holds two sets of records.** A `renderAll` or `reconcile` called from a hook or a card renderer cannot write the list there (a list write flushes; H35, H99), so it is written in a microtask. The first implementation of this phase queued the list it had built at the call, and the first verification rejected it: on the real bundle an annotation added in the same task was never drawn (on the new record and on a kept, mounted one), a newer list written directly in between was overwritten by the older one (the bridge then held 10 records and the viewer 31 items, with validator errors), and `destroy()` in between was undone by the microtask constructing a new viewer; none of it logged anything (`evidence/phase-4/verify/probe-deferred-list-write*.log`). The second verification rejected two things the first fix round had left wrong, both stated below where they belong (rule 3: an item drawn for the first time after its record had gone; rule 5: the view restore of a write deciding from `files`). The rules now, each with a browser test in `bridge/list-write-in-callback.spec.js` and a unit test, and each shown to be needed by a mutation of the bridge that fails both (twenty-two mutations, the three rejected implementations among them: M8, M12, M14; `evidence/phase-4/round3/mutations/browser-mutations.log`, `unit-mutations.log`; the first round's eleven are in `round2/mutations/`):
  1. *The write is made from `files` as they are when it runs* (`_writeList()` takes no list). What is added, revealed or collapsed between the request and the write is part of it.
  2. *At most one write waits* (`_listWriteQueued`). A list write made directly in between is that write; `destroy()` cancels it and is final (`renderAll` answers false afterwards, `attach` does nothing); the microtask then does nothing. *A list write never runs inside another* (`_listWriteRunning`): the view restore of a write tells the page of the selection it puts back (the viewer's `setSelectedLines` calls `onLineSelected`) and moves the focus, and a handler of the page that asks for a list write there is not inside a render callback; it gets the write in a microtask after the one in progress has ended, with the records changed at once, as from a hook (mutation M23: written at once, the second write runs inside the first one's view restore).
  3. *A viewer callback resolves its record in `_listed`*, the records the viewer's list was last written from, and not in `files`. A record that was removed or replaced goes on drawing its item until the write and is discarded by the write (`_dropRecord`). Measured: when such an item answers nothing instead (no header), a redraw of the old list before the write (a layout change in the same task) logs `reconciled item height does not match DOM height` for each mounted item and `sticky container height does not match computed layout` (`round2/stale-item-answering-nothing-validator.log`); and when the callbacks resolve in `files`, the new record of the same path takes the old item's host, instance and header (`round2/stale-item-resolved-in-files.log`), and with the instance the old item's manual expansions, which the write would then fold into a diff that starts from the patch.

     *Nothing of the page runs for an item whose record is no longer the pane's.* Such an item is drawn from what the bridge already holds, whether it was drawn before or not:
     - `renderHeader` is not called: the item gets the header slot it has, and an item that has none gets an empty slot that carries the height the viewer was told as an inline style (`_emptyHeaderSlot`);
     - `hostState` is not called: the host keeps the classes it carries (a host that is new to the item gets the bridge's own: `d2h-file-wrapper`, `context-file`, `collapsed`);
     - no card renderer and no card sync is run (`_renderAnnotation` hands back the card the annotation has, as it is; an annotation that was never drawn stays without a card);
     - `onItemLifecycle` is not called with `'mount'` or `'update'`, and `onLineClick`, `onLineSelect` and `onLineSelectionEnd` are not called;
     - `'unmount'` goes by the id, not by the record (`_mountsTold`): it is told exactly when the page was told `'mount'` under that id and the pane still has the file. The old item of a replaced record, unmounted by the list write or by the viewer before it, is the page's `'unmount'` for the `'mount'` it heard (measured: the reader moved to the top and the old list drawn again in the same task makes the viewer unmount it while the write is still waiting); an old item that was first drawn after its record had been replaced was never announced, and its unmount is not told either (before this round it was: an `'unmount'` without a `'mount'`); the item of a file that was taken out is unmounted without a word, as it always was.

     **An item can be drawn for the first time in that state** (second verification, finding 1).
