The viewer draws the items of one pass one after another: each instance emits its `onPostRender` at the end of its own render (`FileDiff.js:799-811`), and the measured order of callbacks in one pass is `header:file25.ts, mount:file25.ts, header:added-at-run-time.js, header:file26.py, mount:file26.py`. So a hook that fires on the first item of a pass can take out, or replace, a file whose item the same pass draws next. The first fix round kept "the header slot it has" and fell through to the page's hook when there was none: `renderHeader` was called for the forgotten file, answered nothing, and the validator logged `reconciled item height does not match DOM height {id: ctx/added-at-run-time.js, index: 25, expectedHeight: 816}` and `sticky container height does not match computed layout {delta: -40}` (`verify/round2/probe-round2-stale-first-mount*.log`; three of three). Now: the page is not asked, the slot is empty and 40 px, the item has its lines and no cards, validator 0, and the verifier's probes run clean (`round3/verifier-probes-after-fix.log`). Two things measured on the way: a slot root that takes its height from the header inside it is 0 px when empty (the bridge-in-browser page styles only the stand-in header; the first version of the fix relied on the class alone and failed its own browser test with the same two validator lines), which is why the empty slot sets its own height; and an item that is drawn for the first time without cards is a layout the validator accepts (the viewer measures cards, it is told nothing about them).
  4. *A record that is not in the viewer's list yet is never published* (`_publish` answers false; the waiting write carries its state). `findFileElement`, `isDiffDisplayed`, `getHoveredLine` and `getLineSelection` answer for it as for a file that is not mounted.
  5. *`scrollToItem`, `scrollToLine`, `selectLines` and `clearLineSelection` run after the write*, in call order (`_onList`); a target whose file is gone by then, or whose line is no longer shown, is dropped. Sent at once they would address the old list and be undone by the write's view restore; measured against a direct `reconcile` followed by the same calls: the same line at the same pixel and the same selection. The same holds for one sent by a hook *while a list write is drawing* (`_listWriteRunning`: from the write's own flush, before its view restore): it runs after the restore, where sent at once the restore put the reader back and the page's navigation was lost (`bridge/list-write-in-callback.spec.js` "... sent by a hook while a list write is drawing ...": the same row at the same pixel as when sent after the write; mutation M17).

     *Whatever the bridge sends to the viewer's list is decided from that list*, never from `files` (second verification, finding 2). The view restore of a write runs after the write's flush, and the flush runs the page's hooks: a hook that asks for the next list write there has already replaced `files`, while the viewer holds the list just written. The first fix round's `_restoreView` asked `files`: with the file being read given another diff in the intermediate list it sent `scrollTo({type: 'line', lineNumber: 90})` to an item that does not show line 90 and the reader ended 40 px off (two direct writes: 0 px); with the file absent from the intermediate list it sent a line target, and a `setSelectedLines`, for an id the viewer had no item for, and the selection survived where two direct writes drop it (`verify/round2/probe-round2-adversarial.log`, `probe-round2-nested-restore.log`, `probe-round2-v2-repeat3.log`). Now `_restoreView`, the scroll commands and `selectLines` resolve the file in `_listed` and ask `_listShowsLine`, which reads the item the viewer holds (`codeView.getItem(id)`: its `fileDiff` and `collapsed`). The item and not the listed record, because a record that `reconcile` keeps is changed in place (`_applyContextEntry`) and is ahead of its item until the waiting write: measured with a kept context file whose ranges the nested reconcile changed, the record shows the line being read again and the viewer's item does not (mutation M16 sends the line target there). Each of the four scenarios ends where two direct writes end: same first visible file, same pixel, same selection.

  Nothing in the plan's phases 5 to 7 writes a list from a hook; the rules are what makes it safe if something does (a lifecycle hook that ends in `loadContextFiles`, a renderer that triggers a refresh). A hook must still not call `destroy()`: `cleanUp` inside the viewer's own pass is not something this phase measured. Phase 5 must give `.pr-file-header-slot` its fixed height in `pr.css` (the gate CSS of G9 does): the bridge sets the height itself only on the empty slot of rule 3.

**Found in phase 5**

- H104. **"The file has an element" is not "the file is in the pane", in the application and in the tests.** The viewer mounts what is near the viewport and nothing else; in the E2E fixture pane (two small changed files) a context file two entries down has no host, and a changed file behind a 40-line context file has none either. Consequences that were met in this phase: a spec that asserts a locator's visibility or count for a file states the table renderer's model (correction 13; `expectContextFile`, `filesInPane` from data); `waitForContentUpgrade(file)` for a file that is not mounted would wait for ever, because a file's contents are fetched when its item mounts, so on `codeview` the in-page `isUpgraded` also answers true for a file that rests outside the mounted window once the viewer has drawn its first pass (`D.restsUnmounted`: pool ready, the viewer has rendered items, the file has a record and no host). A test that then needs the file's rows fails on the rows, not silently. Phases 6 and 7 meet the same in every spec that waits for or points at a file further down the pane; the helper to bring a file into the mounted window is `ensureMounted` (engine half) behind `scrollFileIntoView`.
- H105. **The viewer keeps a window of a long item's rows in the DOM, and its separators with them.** After two expansions of a context file (61 lines shown) the separator after the last range was not in the DOM. The E2E adapter therefore takes a context file's lines, ranges and gaps from the bridge's visibility oracle and the record's metadata (`D.contextShownLines`, `D.contextGaps`) and uses the DOM as the cross-check: every separator that is in the DOM must show the count, the controls and the "Expand all" the data gives for its gap, and every row in the DOM must be a line the oracle calls shown, or the read throws. A gap's label is the drawn text and is null for a separator outside the window. The golden dump reads a changed file that is taller than the pane window by window (`dumpFile` of the engine half), merging rows by their index in the file's layout. *Since the second session of phase 5 (H114) an item that shows fewer than 5,000 rows is drawn whole, so what this entry describes happens only in longer items; the adapter is unchanged and stays correct for both.*
- H106. **`test.use({ expectedConsoleErrors: [a, b] })` with two patterns is read by Playwright as `[value, options]`**: the fixture then gets the first pattern alone, not an array (`expectedConsoleErrors.some is not a function`). A describe that expects two messages passes one pattern with an alternation.
- H107. **A flake of main's suite, on both engines: N5 "marking a context file viewed ... after a reload", PR mode.** `Failed to save viewed state: TypeError: Failed to fetch` from the console guard, 1 of 30 on `codeview` and 1 of 60 on the default engine at six workers (`wip-viewed-repeat-*.log` in the phase-5 evidence). The mock of the viewed-state API changed the state the test polls after `route.fulfill()` had resolved, which is when the browser has taken the response, not when the page's `fetch` has it; the test's reload could abort the save in between. `mockViewedStateApi` now waits for the response to finish in the page (`response.finished()`): 120 of 120 on each engine afterwards (`viewed-repeat-*-after-fix.log`).
- H108. **The host's classes are the bridge's.** `PRManager` used to toggle `collapsed` and `summaries-hidden-file` on the wrapper it found. A viewer host is pooled: a class written from outside is not in the bridge's record of what it stamped, survives the unmount and shows up on the next file that gets the host (a file drawn as collapsed, or with its summaries hidden). `_setFileElementClass` writes a wrapper the page built and, when the bridge has `refreshHostState`, asks the bridge to stamp from data instead (`collapsed` from its record, `generated-file` and `summaries-hidden-file` from `PRManager._fileHostClasses`). Phase 6 must not add a third class the old way. *(Phase 9: one writer had been left the old way. `_fetchHunkSummaryMap` restored the stored per-file choice with `wrapper.classList.add('summaries-hidden-file')`. When the page's configuration is answered after the first files are drawn, that class is not among the host's stamps, and the next file drawn on the host carries it: reproduced in both modes, the class on `corpus-evict/z-tail.js` after a scroll to the end. It now asks the bridge, `refreshHostState`, as `toggleFileSummaries` does. `hunk-summaries.spec.js` "a file whose summaries were hidden in an earlier session ...", both modes. `_setFileElementClass`, which this entry names, was deleted in phase 8.)*
- H109. **Local mode's empty diff.** `local.js` does not call `renderDiff` for an empty diff: it empties the container and sets its own message. On `codeview` that is `_clearDiffItems` (an empty list, the changed-file map cleared, a running context load cancelled). One visible difference from main is left: main removes the context wrappers there and draws only context rows that are new from then on, so stored context files stay away until the next render; on `codeview` the next `loadContextFiles` (a push, an added range) makes items of every stored row again, as PR mode's empty diff shows them. Not seen in any spec; the live drive of phase 9 does the scope change.
- H110. **Resolved in phase 5's fix round: under `codeview` the pane and every changed file look as on main** (section 6, item 18). The first report of this phase recorded three differences "read from the CSS and the code, not measured": no 1 px line at the top and bottom of the pane, the toolbar and the tour bar fixed instead of sticky, and H111. The verifier measured three more on every changed file (header 45 px against 53, 0 px against 8 under the header, no frame). All of them were then measured and compared on screenshots with `tests/parity/visual.mjs`, and made equal: the pane's box (border, radius, padding, shadow) scrolls with the files again; the toolbar and the tour bar are in the same pixels at the top of the pane and scrolled, with and without a tour (positions `top`, `pinned`, `tour-top`, `tour-pinned`); header, space under it, frame, space between files and after the last. What is left is listed in the phase-5 log ("What still differs from main under `codeview`"). H111 is unchanged and is phase 7's.
- H111. **Collapse while scrolled inside a file: main loses the header, the viewer keeps it.** On main the scroll offset is kept, so the collapsed header is 300 px above the pane's top after a collapse 300 px inside a long file (H48; `collapseInside.belowPinned` -359 in the edge corpus). On `codeview` the collapsed item is at the top of the pane (`belowPinned` 16 in the same dump: the viewer's top padding). Plan 3.12 allowed "match or deliberately improve"; this is kept as the viewer does it, because the file the reader just collapsed stays in sight, and it is listed as a visible change of behaviour. `scroll-landing.spec.js` asserts main's landing; phase 7, which owns N4 under `codeview`, gives it a named expectation. *(Phase 7: what the viewer did by itself was not that.)* In the 60-file corpus, with the reader 300 px inside the thirty-first file, the collapse left to the viewer moved the pane by 779 px and the collapsed file was not even mounted afterwards (the viewer anchors on a row that no longer exists); the first file of the edge corpus had only looked right because the pane was clamped at its top. See H145 for what is built and `COLLAPSE_INSIDE_FILE` in H51. *(Phase 10: the title is history. Main at `6f06bbb1` keeps the header too, by `HeaderAnchor.keepHeaderInPlace`; the merged tree keeps it by the viewer, and the two land on the same pixel in every case measured. H145, H172.)*
- H112. **`itemMetrics.lineHeight` is measured once, at the first list write, and not again** (H38's decision). Mounted rows are measured by the viewer whatever the estimate; a zoom change during a session leaves the estimate for rows that are not mounted off until they mount, and a reload measures again. No E2E run changes the zoom, so this is a decision from reasoning, not from a measurement; phase 9's live drive is where a zoom change is tried.
- H113. **`tests/e2e/diff-dom.js` was at the size guideline.** The module is three files now: `diff-dom.js` (the surface every spec imports, what both engines share, the named expectations), `diff-dom-filediff.js` (the table renderer's context files) and `diff-dom-codeview.js` (the virtualized viewer). The boundary test allows vendor DOM in these three and nowhere else, and checks that only `diff-dom.js` loads the other two. Phase 8 deletes the first engine half.
- H114. **Resolved: what the development validator reported was a vendor defect that a reader can see (F26), not a passing state.** The first session of this phase met `CodeView: reconciled item height does not match DOM height` and `sticky container height does not match computed layout {delta: -40}` in 17 to 19 of 60 executions of N3's sidebar-navigation tests under the development bundle, described it as "for a frame or two while the pane is scrolled upward, silent at rest", and let the fixture tolerate it. The second session reproduced it on the raw viewer with nothing but a `layout` option and found the mechanism (`s2/probe-raw-*.log` in the phase-5 evidence; the vendor's `FileDiff.applyPartialRender` and `trimColumnRows`): a row window that shrinks to end exactly at a file's last row trims the separator that follows it, and growth appends rows only. It is not transient. Scrolling up 60 px into that band and back down leaves a file whose contents are loaded without its last separator and its expand control, with the end of the file on screen and the file below drawn 40 px too high, at rest, until the file is unmounted or its last row leaves the window and returns (`probe-raw-4.log`: 47 reports, still wrong after 300 ms of rest). The first session's walks ended at scroll position 0, where the file is unmounted, which is why the pane looked exact "at rest". The same trim drops the card of the row a shrinking window ends at; the row is measured again without it, so the validator says nothing, and the card stays missing with its row on screen (`row-window.spec.js`, second test). On main neither can happen: `FileDiff` draws a file whole. **What was done.** (1) The defect is pinned on the raw viewer by three deterministic tests (`tests/contract/vendor/row-window.spec.js`; 45 of 45 in a 15-fold repetition, `s2/row-window-repeat15.log`). (2) The bridge gives every item a row window of 5,000 rows (`ROWS_PER_ITEM_WINDOW`, `itemMetrics.hunkLineCount`): an item that shows fewer rows has the same window at every scroll position and is never trimmed. `tests/contract/bridge/view.spec.js` "what is drawn of a mounted file does not change with the scroll position" walks eight upgraded files with a card on the last line of every hunk a row at a time, up and back down: it fails on the default window (`s2/bridge-rowwindow-before-fix.log`: rows, cards and the last separator change from step to step) and passes with the option. (3) The fixture's tolerance is gone: in a development-bundle run every validator report fails the test, and the pass at rest after every test stays. (4) `_clearExpansions` now also tells the viewer to compute the item's height again (H120). **Cost.** A mounted item of up to 5,000 rows is drawn whole, which is what main does for every file up to 20,000 patch lines. In the contract harness a 20,000-row file drawn whole mounts with one 251 ms frame and scrolls at the same frame times afterwards (`s2/probe-w1.log`); with the 5,000-row window such a file is drawn 5,000 rows at a time. Phase 9's performance comparison is where this is judged against main.
- H115. **The viewer holds a navigation's scroll target until the reader takes over** (H25: a wheel, a key, a pointer press on the pane). A position written by a script is not one of those and is undone by the target: after a sidebar jump a test that set the pane's `scrollTop` to 0 found it at 22,988 px ten seconds later (N3 "there is no scroll-spy", 3 of 60 in a development-bundle repetition; it passed in every shipped-bundle run of this phase). The E2E helpers that scroll (`scrollDiffTo`, `scrollDiffToEdge`, `scrollDiffUnderPointer`, `scrollFileOutOfView`) now go through `D.setScrollTop`, which on `codeview` dispatches a wheel event on the pane first, as a reader's scrolling would. The application writes the pane's scroll position in one place under `codeview`, the bridge's own view restore, which the bridge orders against its scroll targets itself (H103, rule 5). A later phase that writes `scrollTop` from application code while a navigation may be in flight will meet this.
- H116. **A scroll container with `display: none` makes the viewer measure every item as 0 px** (the validator: `actualStickyHeight: 0`, `reconciled item height does not match DOM height` for both fixture files, in `stale-badge.spec.js` "auto-refreshes silently", one development-bundle run). `_setDiffStatus(..., { hideScroller: true })` hides `#diff-container` with the `hidden` attribute while a refresh loads. Under `codeview` the hidden container keeps its box and is not painted (`visibility: hidden`, absolutely positioned over the pane, no pointer events; `pr.css`), so what the viewer measures while a message stands in place of the diff is real. 148 of 148 in a development-bundle repetition of the specs that hide it.
- H117. **A development-bundle E2E run** is `PR_VENDOR_BUNDLE=dev PR_ENGINE=codeview npx playwright test ...`: the fixture serves `tests/.cache/pierre-diffs.dev.js` in place of the shipped bundle (a route on the browser context, so a spec that blocks the bundle on its page still wins) and gives the viewer `__devOnlyValidateItemHeights` by wrapping the bridge's class as the page defines it; no application code knows about such a run. `fixture-isolation.spec.js` "The layout validator of a development-bundle run" asserts in every run that the bundle and the option are what the run asked for, so a run in which neither took effect cannot be green. The console guard fails a test on any validator report (the first session's exception for H114 is gone), and after every test one more validating pass with the pane at rest must report nothing.
- H118. **On main the content upgrade changes the changed rows themselves, not only the context around them.** Decision D5 expected "context rows next to hunks can differ by a line" and the comparison rule says post-upgrade changed rows must be identical. Measured in this phase's dumps: on the default engine the changed rows after the upgrade differ from the rows of the patch in 40 of 624 files times dumps (five files of the 60-file corpus in all eight of its dumps: `d10/file10.md`, `d15/file15.css`, `d31/file31.ts`, `d42/file42.js`, `d46/file46.md`). The re-diff of the two contents pairs the lines differently from git: in `file46.md` the patch deletes old lines 71 to 74 and adds new lines 70 to 72, and after the upgrade main shows old line 71 deleted, 70 to 72 added, then old lines 73 to 75 deleted. On `codeview` the changed rows after the upgrade are the patch's in 624 of 624. So the two engines agree before the upgrade everywhere and disagree after it in those five files, and the one that changed is main. Phase 7's post-upgrade comparison needs the rule "the changed rows after the upgrade are the changed rows of the patch" for `codeview`, and lists the files where main's are not; `compare.mjs` reports them as regressions today. *(Phase 7: resolved by doing what main does.)* The orchestrator decided that the display after a file's contents load is main's (decision D5a). The bridge diffs the two contents again and keeps the patch's lines, so the rows after the upgrade are main's in every file, the five above included; the rule this entry asked for is not needed and `compare.mjs` is unchanged.
- H119. **What is left of F26 with the 5,000-row window.** An item that shows 5,000 rows or more is still windowed, in steps of 5,000 rows, and the trim is still the vendor's: a card on the last row of a window (the 5,000th, 10,000th, ... row drawn) can be dropped when the window shrinks, and a file whose row count is an exact multiple of 5,000 can lose its last separator. Changed files reach that size only between the highlighting cap and the deferral threshold (3,000 to 20,000 patch lines) or after "Load diff"; context files only when the reader expands them that far. Not covered by a test of the application; the vendor tests pin the trim itself, so a vendor fix is noticed. Not drawing such files whole is deliberate: with `Number.MAX_SAFE_INTEGER` the 20,100-row file of `lazy-diff.spec.js` (materialized by "Load diff") stood 39.4 px taller than its layout at rest in both modes (`s2/wip-accept-dev-1.log`), which the same file drawn in 5,000-row windows does not (`s2/wip-lazy-dev-5000.log`), and which 4,900 and 20,000 rows drawn whole in the contract harness do not show either (`s2/probe-whole-rows.log`). The cause was not found. Phase 6, which owns the cards, should decide whether a card on such a row needs a guard, and phase 9 should look at a 20,000-line file on screen. *(Phase 6: measured, no guard built.)* Bridge and real bundle, a 20,000-row added file, cards on lines 4999, 5000, 5001 and 10000: the card on line 5000 is drawn on the first visit and gone after the reader has scrolled 2,600 rows away and back, and stays gone through further scrolling; the card on line 10000 was not drawn at all; 4999 and 5001 are drawn throughout; the layout stays exact and the validator is silent (the row is measured without its card). The bridge holds all four annotations, so counts, the review panel and the submitted review are unaffected: it is the card in the diff that is missing. The loss depends on how the window moves (a shorter sequence lost the card in 1 of 3 executions), so no contract test was kept; the record is `h119-probe.txt` in the phase-6 evidence. Affected: a comment, suggestion, summary, thread or tour stop whose row is the 5,000th, 10,000th, ... row drawn of a file that shows that many rows. Candidates for a guard, none decided: a row window larger than the longest file of the pane (phase 5 met an unexplained 39 px mismatch on a 20,100-row file drawn whole in the application), anchoring such an annotation on the row before, or the vendor's fix (F26). *(Phase 6, second session: a guard is built.)* The mechanism was read in the vendor source (`components/FileDiff.js`, `trimColumnRows`): the loop counts a line row before it looks at the row after it, so when the rows after the last kept line are removed (`visibleLineIndex >= postTrimStart`) that line's annotation row is the first one removed; `applyPartialRender` then appends the newly covered rows from the next line on. The card is therefore missing in the middle of the drawn rows, not at their end. A draw from scratch has it, and a publish is always a draw from scratch (`canPartiallyRender` answers no when the annotations or the version changed). Measured with the bridge on the real bundle (`s2/h119-probe-2.log`): after a publish the card is drawn in every case in which its row is inside the drawn rows. `PierreCodeViewBridge._restoreTrimmedCards` runs one frame after every draw of a file: when a column has 5,000 children or more and the row of an annotated line is followed by a row that is not an annotation row, the file is published again, once per state (the same rows and the same lines are not asked for twice, so a card that cannot be drawn does not loop). A line at the very end of the drawn rows is left alone: the window ends there, the cards of that row are not drawn by a draw from scratch either, and they would be below what the window shows. Pinned by two bridge tests on a 20,000-row file with cards on lines 5000, 9999, 10000 and 10001 (`bridge/view.spec.js`): with the check, every card is drawn with its row on screen after the window has moved away and back in both directions; with the check switched off, a card on line 5000 or 10000 is missing with its row and the next row on screen, which is the defect itself and fails when the vendor fixes it (16 of 16 in an eight-fold repetition). What a reader can still see: the card is absent for one frame after the window moved, and the redraw of a 10,000-row window costs one long frame, in files of 5,000 rows or more only. What is still open of F26 in such files: a file whose row count is an exact multiple of 5,000 can lose its last separator (no guard; the separator is not the bridge's). *(Fix round of phase 8: the cause is found, and one more thing was left of the row window than this entry says.)* The 39 px are the viewer's own measurement, F31: rows more than 2^18 px from the viewport are measured through 32-bit floats and come out a 64th of a pixel off at the application's line height; the DOM was right and the computed height was not. That is a property of drawing more than about 15,000 rows of 17.4 px at once, so the window stays at 5,000. What this entry did not have: in every file that shows more than 5,000 rows the viewer left the rows after each 5,000th undrawn while the middle of the pane was before them, and never drew a short tail at the end of the pane (F30). That is corrected by `coverPaneWithRowWindow` (H155, H162). The trim of F26 at a block boundary is unchanged by it: the window still grows and shrinks by a block as the pane passes a boundary, now when the pane's lower edge reaches it instead of its middle, and `_restoreTrimmedCards` still draws the file again when a card was dropped.
- H120. **Folding what the reader expanded by hand leaves the old diff on screen without those lines until the new one is displayed, on any row window.** `addContextRanges` (and a list write) fold the viewer's expansion map into the record's ranges, clear the map because it is keyed by hunk index (F17), and publish the derived diff, which is displayed when the highlighter answers (F5). Until then the viewer draws the old diff again, now with an empty map, so the lines the reader had expanded are gone for those frames and come back with the new diff. With the default row window the item's cached height kept those lines and the difference sat in the window's trailing buffer as blank space, which the validator does not look at; with a window that does not move there is no buffer, and two tests of phase 4's suite reported it (`contents.spec.js` "addContextRanges, isLineVisible and convertOldToNew on a mounted file" and `context-records.spec.js` "the reader expands a context file with real clicks ...": `sticky container height ... delta: -440`, `s2/contract-after-fix-1.log`). `_clearExpansions` therefore calls the instance's `resetLayoutCache({ includeEstimatedHeights: true })` when it empties a map that had entries, so layout and DOM agree during the wait (both tests pass again; unit tests in `pierre-codeview-bridge.test.js`). That is a second vendor internal next to the expansion map itself. The momentary disappearance of the expanded lines is what a reader could see; it was there before this phase and belongs to phase 7 (reveal and expansion), which should fold without clearing before the swap if the vendor offers a way. *(Phase 7: resolved, H146, in two sessions.)* The map is kept for the diff that is on screen and dropped when the viewer has the next one ready; the lines stay through every frame of the wait. The first session did this for `addContextRanges` and `upgradeFileContents` only: a context file that gets another stored range goes through `reconcile` and `_applyContextEntry`, whose fold (`_foldManualExpansions`) still emptied the map at once and without the height reset, so the expanded lines left the screen for 6 to 7 frames and the development validator reported the item 382 px shorter than its layout (found by the phase's verifier: `evidence/phase-7/verify/probe-context-fold-codeview*.log`). Since the second session the fold only moves the lines into the record's ranges, and every caller that derives a mounted record again retires the map after the derive (`_retireExpansions`); a list write still empties it, in the task that replaces the instance.
- H121. **A gate that loads one of the application's stylesheets says nothing about the application.** G9 and G13 loaded `pr.css`; `styles.css`, loaded after it by both pages, has `!important` rules on `.d2h-file-header` and `.d2h-file-wrapper` (section 6, item 17). A token, a fallback and a ledger row rested on the 45 px that came out. Gates take their stylesheets from the pages (`appStylesheets()`); `tests/CONVENTIONS.md` has the rule. The same holds for anything else measured "under the app's CSS": the measurement is the running application's, or it loads what the application loads.
- H122. **What is slotted into the viewer inherits from the viewer's shadow tree, not from the page.** The vendor's `:host` rule sets `font-family`, `font-size` (12 px through `--diffs-font-size`), `line-height` (1.45), `color` and `color-scheme: light dark`. The file header in its slot therefore had another font stack, a 20.3 px line height where the page gives 21 px, another text colour (36, 41, 46 against 36, 41, 47) and the system's colour scheme for its checkbox; its children were up to 0.6 px shorter than on main (the verifier's `file-stats-summary: 17.39` against `18`, `file-viewed-label: 25.39` against `26`). `.pr-file-header-slot` sets each of them to the page's value. Cards and the file-comment zone are slotted the same way (they were on main's `FileDiff` too, so nothing changes for them), and anything new that is slotted must be compared on `visual.mjs`.
- H123. **The viewer paints its host, its sticky header box, its code block and the rows of file-level annotations with the code background** (`--diffs-bg`: white in light, `rgb(36, 41, 46)` in dark, where the pane is `rgb(13, 17, 23)`). In light theme none of it shows; in dark theme the header's rounded corners, the frame's translucent border and a file without lines each came out in another colour until `FRAME_CSS` made the host and the header box transparent and gave a file without lines no background. A look check in light theme alone would have passed. `visual.mjs` runs both themes.
- H124. **The 8 px under a header and the 1 px line of an empty diff are measured by the viewer, not told to it** (they are in the file's comment zone, the item's first annotation; section 6, item 18). For an item that is not mounted the viewer's estimate is therefore 8 px (or 1 px) short until the item has been mounted once, as it is short by the height of every card and every wrapped line. Mounted items are exact (validator 0 in gate G9, gate G13 and the development-bundle E2E run). The alternative, `itemMetrics.paddingTop`, is exact for unmounted items and puts 8 px under the header of a file without lines, which a reader sees; it was not taken. If a vendor bump makes an ordinary item mount without its file-level annotation while it has rows, the 8 px would be missing there and the validator would say so.
- H125. **A file's comment zone with something in it is inside the frame on `codeview` and above it on main.** On main the zone is an element of the page between the header and `.pierre-diff-body`: as wide as the file, outside the frame's borders, and the code block's 8 px are below it. On `codeview` the zone is the item's first annotation: inside the code block (so between the frame's left and right border, and in split layout stretched over both columns by the full-width pass), and the 8 px are its `::after`, so they are below its content as on main. The empty zone, which is what this phase's look check covers, is identical. Phase 6 owns the zone with a comment, a form or an external thread in it: `visual.mjs` gets a position with a file-level comment, and the zone's box is made equal there (negative inline margins of the frame's 1 px are the candidate). *(Phase 6: partly done; the box is still open.)* The zone's inherited text properties and its background over the 8 px are equal now (H132, H133). Its horizontal extent is not: with a comment in it, in unified layout at 1440 px the zone is 1106.02 px wide starting 38.98 px from the file's left edge (the line-number gutter) and ending 1 px before its right edge, where main's is 1146 px from edge to edge; its cards are 1074.02 px wide for 1114. The gutter strip and the frame's two 1 px borders beside the zone show the code background where main shows the zone's grey. One attempt was reverted: the code block is a size container, so `width: 100cqw; margin-left: calc(100% - 100cqw)` on the file-level annotation's content gives x 1 and 1144 px in unified layout, but the viewer's sticky gutter column paints its own background over the zone's first 39 px (a `z-index` on the content and a transparent first buffer cell both changed nothing), which would cut the cards off. What a fix needs: the gutter column not painted beside a file-level annotation, the frame's borders (the `pre` clips at its padding box and the code block has paint containment), and split layout, where a two-sided file's code columns generate no box and so no container. *(Phase 6, second session: resolved.)* The zone now spans the file from edge to edge in every layout: `ZONE_CSS` in `pierre-styles.js` stretches the row of line 0, and only that row. Measured on both engines with a comment in the zone (`zone-probe.mjs` in the phase-6 evidence): zone `x` 0, width 1146, card `x` 16, width 1114, in unified and split layout, for a modified file, an added file, a deleted file, a binary file, an empty added file, a renamed and changed file and a context file. How, per layout: in unified layout and on a context file the row ends at the right edge of the one code column, which is a size container, so `100cqw` is that column's width without knowing the line-number column's; on a changed file the column is widened by 1 px on both sides (a negative inline margin and the same padding, so its rows stay where they are), because it is a scroll container with paint containment and clips at its padding box. In split layout the columns of a two-sided file generate no box, so the row is sized from its own width and the gutter width the full-width pass already publishes (`--pr-split-gutter-width`); an entirely added or removed file has its own two rules (the lone column is boxed into its half). The row is above the line-number column (`z-index: 4`, on the row: a `z-index` on its content, which the first attempt used, is inside the row's own stacking context and changes nothing) and transparent, so the frame's borders and the code background show beside the 8 px under the zone's content, as on main. Two things followed from letting the row over the frame's borders. The code block can no longer clip at its padding box (`overflow: clip` on the `pre` is gone, H136), and the code columns of a changed file have no background of their own (the `pre`'s, clipped to its padding box, is behind them), or the widened column would paint over the translucent borders.
- H126. **What the look check cannot see.** `visual.mjs` compares numbers to a hundredth of a pixel and pixels with a tolerance for anti-aliasing (a pixel counts when it is outside the range of the 3 x 3 pixels around the same place in the other image by more than 8 of 255). Measured causes of the pixels inside the tolerance: the 1 px translucent frame border composites to 228 on one engine and 229 on the other in some rows (and the other way round in others; main is not uniform itself); glyphs of the toolbar, the tour bar and the headers are anti-aliased differently by 1 to 40 of 255 on single edge pixels because those elements are in other compositing layers on the two engines (sticky inside the scroll container on main, above it or inside the viewer's sticky element on `codeview`); 4 pixels at each bottom corner of a frame differ by 4 to 7 of 255 (the curve is clipped by the body's overflow on main and by the code block's own on `codeview`). Not covered at all: hover and focus states, a scrollbar (headless Chromium draws none), the file-comment zone with content (H125), a "Load diff" file, cards on lines (phase 6), zoom levels other than 100%, viewport widths other than 1440 px. *(Phase 6: the zone with content, cards on lines, forms, a selection, minimized comments, review threads, a comment in a context file's zone and a "Load diff" file are covered now.)* One position is decided, not equal: the end of the pane in split layout with the edge corpus. 1,143 pixels are outside the tolerance there, all of them in eleven text lines, each the second or third line of a wrapped row, drawn one pixel higher or lower; no box, line or colour differs, every file's box is equal in the numbers, and so is where the last file ends (40.875 px above the pane's bottom edge on both engines, a number the check now takes at that position). The files at that position are at fractional offsets (the heights of wrapped rows are not whole pixels), and the two engines paint them in different layers (the page's scrolling layer on main, the viewer's pinned container on `codeview`), which is the cause already measured for single edge pixels above; that it is also the cause of a whole glyph row landing one pixel away was not measured separately. In unified layout the same position has 28,238 pixels that differ by more than 2 of 255 and none outside the tolerance. `visual.mjs` lists the position as accepted with this text (`ACCEPTED`, `where: 'pixels'`).
- H127. **`itemMetrics.paddingBottom` is one number for every item that has a line.** A changed file needs 9 px (8 px and the frame's bottom border). A context file has no frame on main and no space under its last row; on `codeview` it gets the same 9 px (a transparent 1 px border and the vendor's 8 px), where the screenshot the D2 decision was taken on had 8 px. One pixel more than accepted, nine more than main, on context files only.
- H128. **`has-diff-lines` and `has-diff-body` are two stamps from two owners.** The bridge knows whether an item's metadata has a hunk and stamps `has-diff-lines` (both kinds, collapsed or not, also on a record that is no longer the pane's); `PRManager._fileHostClasses` knows whether main would draw a body for the file (a patch, not binary, not deferred) and answers `has-diff-body` through the `hostState` hook. `pr.css` keys the 8 px on the first and the empty diff's line on the second without the first. A "Load diff" file changes from neither to both when its lines arrive; `refreshHostState` is not needed for that, because the item's update re-stamps the host.

**Found in phase 6**

- H129. **The gutter buttons after a scroll: main re-derives the hover from the resting pointer; the phase-4 bridge did not.** Plan 3.13 says "on scroll it hides its gutter element and shows it on the next real `pointermove`, which is what main does". Main does more (`pierre-bridge.js`, `_onAnyScroll`): one frame after a scroll it clears the buttons and dispatches a pointer move at the last pointer position, so the buttons are back on the row that is under the pointer now. With "hidden until the pointer moves", a scroll event that arrives after the last pointer move leaves the buttons hidden for good, and that order is ordinary: a programmatic scroll's event is dispatched in the next frame, after a pointer move made right behind it (Playwright's `hover()` scrolls the target into view and moves; a navigation's landing followed by a move). Measured: `gutter-buttons.spec.js` "with the suggestions of an analysis on the review" never saw a button on `codeview` (the row is below the fold with the cards above it, so the hover scrolls), `style="visibility: hidden"` on the buttons with the hovered row marked. Now the bridge hides the buttons while the pane moves, waits until the pane has not moved for two frames, tells the file under the pointer where the pointer is (the viewer handles the move in the same task) and shows them. A file the pointer is no longer over drops its hovered row by itself: the browser reports the pointer as having left an element that moved away from under it (a synthetic `pointerleave` was written, shown to change nothing by a mutation run, and removed). `bridge/gutter.spec.js`: "hidden while the pane scrolls ..., back on the row under it when the pane rests", "after a scroll that leaves the resting pointer on no row ...".
- H130. **A drag that starts on the gutter's comment button stops growing after its second line on the virtualized viewer too** (H45, measured: lines 11 to 12 selected, the selection unchanged over three more rows). The cause is the viewer's (once a selection exists its hovered-line getter answers the selection's last line), and the limit is main's, so it is kept; `bridge/gutter.spec.js` has it as a characterization. A drag down the line numbers selects any range on both engines. Since H184 its `selectLines` call passes the line it started on first (the cut is made from it), and neither drag crosses a separator.
- H131. **A header that is drawn again on every mount must not derive more than the header that is built once did.** `_syncFileSummaryToggleButton` enables a file's summary toggle when the file has registered hunk hashes, which is "has hunks", not "has summaries". On main that is right by the order of calls: the header is built before the hunks are registered (disabled, "No summaries available") and the button is synced again only when a summary is drawn on the file, when its hidden state is restored, or when the reader uses it. `_syncFileHeader` called it on every draw, so on `codeview` the toggle of a file without any summary was enabled ("Hide file summaries") as soon as the header was drawn a second time. Found by the look check (a 16 x 12 px icon at 40% opacity on main, opaque on `codeview`, in every screenshot once summaries were enabled). `PRManager._summaryToggleSyncedFiles` records the three events (`_noteSummaryToggleSynced`); `_syncFileHeader` passes `live` from it. The same question applies to anything else `_syncFileHeader` stamps: it must be data that main's one-time build plus its later syncs would also have shown.
- H132. **The comment zone inherits from the viewer's shadow tree** (H122 for the header, now the zone): the code font, 12 px, line height 1.45, the code colour, `color-scheme: light`, `word-break: break-word`, `tab-size: 2`, where main's zone inherits the page's (system font, 14 px, 1.5, `--color-text-primary`). A file-level suggestion's title was monospace. `pr.css` sets the seven properties on `.file-comments-zone` under `codeview`. Cards under lines are slotted into a shadow tree on both engines and inherit the same there.
- H133. **The 8 px under a zone are drawn inside the zone element** (H124), so a background or a border on the zone covers them: with a comment in the zone its grey ran 8 px further down than on main and the line under it was below the 8 px. Under `codeview` the background and the line are `.file-comments-container`'s (the zone's one child), the zone has neither.
- H134. **`ensureMounted` in a spec is where a spec states "this needs an element".** `tests/e2e/diff-dom.js` exports it for both engines (a no-op on the default engine, a scroll into the mounted window on `codeview` when the file has no element). N5's tour scenarios call it for the stop's file before they look for the stop's card: with a context file above the changed files, the first stop's file is outside the mounted window, and the tour's own scroll to a stop in a file that is not mounted is `revealInDiff` step 3 (phase 7). Once phase 7 is in, the calls do nothing there either; they hide nothing on the default engine, where `toBeVisible` never asserted a landing. *(Phase 7.)* The tour navigates to a stop whose file is not mounted, and N5's two calls are removed: its `toBeVisible` and `toBeInViewport` test the tour again. One call was added, in `scroll-landing.spec.js` "an AI-panel click on a finding that names its file in another form ...": that finding has no card, so nothing scrolls to its file on either engine (the file is opened where it is, thirty files below), and the assertions on its element need it mounted; the spec first asks the page's data that the file is open.
- H135. **A count over the document is a count over the mounted window.** `offscreen-mutations.spec.js` asked `page.locator('.hunk-summary-row')` for 3 with one of the three in a file 70,000 px away. `expectLineCardsInPane(page, selector, type, count)` states it for the pane: the bridge holds `count` annotations of the type, every file that has an element shows exactly its own, and on the default engine the document holds `count` as before. *Session 3 addendum:* the same count was behind a navigation failure in "Drawn on a far file: external threads" (`page.locator('.external-comment-row')` for 3 with the tail's thread 70,000 px away; found by running the test with its navigation replaced by `scrollFileIntoView`, `s3/n11-probe-codeview.log`: 2 for 3). It is `expectLineCardsInPane(page, '.external-comment-row', 'external-comment', 3)` now, so that phase 7 does not meet it after `revealInDiff` step 3.
- H136. **Nothing clips a changed file's code block any more, and two things do its work.** On main the body around the vendor's host clips at its padding box (`.pierre-diff-body`, `overflow-x: auto`), and until this phase the `pre` did the same (`overflow: clip`). With the comment zone drawn over the frame's side borders (H125) that clip had to go. Tried first: `clip-path: inset(0 round 0 0 6px 6px)` on the `pre`, which clips at the border's outer edge; a clip path is anti-aliased where `overflow` snaps to pixels, and on a file 441.67 px tall the bottom border came out 246 of 255 where main has 232 (every position of the look check failed on one line of pixels). Without any clip, the look check found what the clip had been hiding: the active tour stop's card fills its row and draws a 2 px ring around itself (`box-shadow`), which then covered the frame's right border and, in split layout, one more pixel beyond the file. So every annotation's content except line 0's is clipped at its own right edge and nowhere else (`clip-path: inset(-100px 0 -100px -100px)`, in `ZONE_CSS`): the row ends at the frame's inner edge, which is where main clips; on the left main shows the ring over the line-number column in split layout; above and below nothing clips on main. In unified layout the ring over the border was inside the look check's tolerance (a blend of the ring and the border lies between the two neighbouring pixels of the other image); it was found in split layout and fixed in both. What keeps the rounded bottom corners clear without a clip: rows end 8 px above the bottom border, the code columns have no background, and the `pre`'s own is clipped to its rounded padding box. Anything new that draws outside its own box next to a file's edge must be looked at on `visual.mjs`. *(Fourth session.)* One more consequence of the column drawn over the border: the `pre`'s `scrollWidth` is 1 more than its `clientWidth` in unified layout, which a test of phase 5's list read as a horizontal overflow (H142).
- H137. **A card slides in when it is attached, and a box measured during that is not the card's.** `.ai-suggestion` has `animation: slideUp 0.3s` (a transform). The look check measured the suggestion cards 0.03 to 0.08 px away from their place, differently in two runs of the default engine alone, and reported four numbers as different between the engines. `annotatedShot` now waits for every finite animation of the document before it measures. The E2E suite does not measure boxes to that precision; a test that does must wait the same way.
- H138. **The check for a trimmed card reads the viewer's rows** (H119): `[data-content]`, `[data-line]`, `[data-line-index]` and "the element after a line's row is its `[data-line-annotation]` row when the line has cards". The first three are what `_findRow` and the E2E adapter already read; the fourth is new, and is the reverse of what `FileDiff.trimColumnRows` itself assumes. If a vendor bump puts something else between a line and its annotation row, the check would redraw a windowed file once per window move for nothing (one attempt per state, so it cannot loop), and the two bridge tests named in H119 would still pass; the unit tests of `_restoreTrimmedCards` state the rule.
- H139. **A panel that opens beside the diff makes the development validator report for as long as its width animates.** `.ai-panel` has `transition: width 0.2s`. While the review panel opens, the pane gets narrower in every frame, the lines of every drawn file wrap again, and the viewer follows each frame from its resize observer, before that frame is painted (F27; `vendor/layout.spec.js` "the pane gets narrower frame after frame ..."). A render forced in such a frame (any publish) is reported, and two frames later the pane is still moving and the viewer is behind by another amount, so the fixture's first rule ("equal two frames later") failed the test. That is what the two unexplained failures of this phase's first session were, and three more in this session's development-bundle runs: `offscreen-mutations.spec.js` opens the review panel in its `beforeEach` (`window.aiPanel.expand()`) over a pane that has a 4,000-line file drawn, and a test whose first statement publishes (a comment reload) can land inside the 0.2 s. Reproduced on purpose (`s2/panel-probe-1.log`: the panel opened and a render forced in every frame; pane width 1019, 1000, 966, 918, 836, 766, 719, 704, 700; the viewer behind by 104.34, 3773.77 and 104.34 px in three of those frames and exact from the frame the width stopped changing; the fixture's message carried `104.34 items 0-1 top 0 [evict/m-big.js 104.34]` at the report and two frames later). The reports of the failed runs have the same numbers (`delta: 104.34375` and `17.390625`, six rows and one row of the big file, `stickyTop: 0`). The fixture now counts its two frames from when the pane's box (the drawn files' height and the pane's width) last changed: a report whose comparison agrees in a later frame is followed; one that still disagrees after two frames at rest fails the test, with what the pane was doing in the message (`fixture-isolation.spec.js`: "a sticky-height report with the pane at rest and the layout still off reaches the console guard", "a frame in which the pane's box changed does not count ...", 150 of 150 in a 15-fold repetition). Nothing a reader sees is wrong in those frames; on main there is no layout to be behind.
- H140. **A click on a file's header, its comment zone or a card is lost when the file is drawn between the press and the release.** Every draw of an item takes what the page slotted into it out of the document and puts it back (the header, the zone, every card). A browser sends `click` only when the pressed element stayed in the document from the press to the release; otherwise it sends no click, to anything (measured on the real bundle: press on a button in the header, `updateItem` and a forced render of that file, release: no `click` event reaches the document; the same for a button in the zone and in a card; `bridge/gutter.spec.js` "without the bridge, a draw of the file between press and release leaves the control without its click"). An item is drawn whenever something is published on it: its contents arriving and being displayed (once per file, a moment after it is mounted), a comment reload, a suggestion landing. On main the header and the zone are elements of the page outside the vendor's host and are never taken out, so a click on them cannot be lost this way. This is what the fourth unexplained failure of the development-bundle runs was: `file-comments.spec.js` and `file-comments-edit-delete.spec.js` click the header's comment button right after a page load, and in 1 of 240 executions under load the file's upgraded diff was displayed between Playwright's press and release ("form not visible within 5 s": the screenshot shows the upgraded diff, the pointer on the button and no form). The bridge now repairs it (`_handleRootPointerDown`, `_handlePointerUp`): a press on something the page put into a file is remembered with how often that file's element had been drawn; when the release is on the same element, the file was drawn in between and no click has arrived by the end of the release's task, the element (or the control an icon is in) is clicked. Not for the viewer's own rows, which are in its shadow tree and work on pointer events. `bridge/gutter.spec.js` "a click whose press and release straddle a draw of the file still reaches the control, once" (header with an icon, zone, card; exactly one click with and without a draw in between; none on a row), five unit tests. What it does not cover: a press that the reader holds while the element under it is replaced by another (the target differs at the release), and focus, which a draw still takes from a focused control (F24). *(Phase 6, fourth session: the rule was wrong for the application's own header, and is replaced.)* The first version clicked only when the press and the release were on the same element. The application re-stamps a file's cached header every time the viewer asks for it, which is every draw (`PRManager._renderFileHeader`, `_syncFileHeader`), and the re-stamp writes the collapse chevron's icon again (`DiffRenderer.updateFileHeaderState`, `innerHTML`). So the `svg` that was pressed is out of the document for good at the release, the release is on a new `svg`, and the press was dropped: a press on the chevron with a draw of the file before the release collapsed nothing (the verifier's probe `verify/probe-click-repair-2-codeview.log`, both modes, every run; the Viewed checkbox and the comment button, whose elements are not rewritten, worked). The contract test used a stand-in header with a static icon and the unit tests one fixed element, so neither saw it. The rule is now the browser's own, read with the press's ancestors as they were when it began (`_pressedControl`): the click goes to the innermost element the press was inside of that is still in the file's element and has the release inside it. For the chevron that is the button. For a press and a release on the same element it is that element, as before. For a press on one control released on another it is the header, which is where a browser sends the click when nothing was drawn (measured: the last block of the same contract test, `['any:div']` with and without a draw). `updateFileHeaderState` is left as it is: it has callers on both engines, and a control whose contents are re-stamped is the case the repair has to handle in general. Tests: `tests/e2e/file-controls.spec.js` "A header control pressed while its file is drawn again" (the application's header in a browser, both modes, both engines: chevron, Viewed, the file comment button, the header's background; press, `redrawFile`, release; each acts once); `tests/unit/header-click-repair.test.js` (the header as `_buildFileHeader` and `_syncFileHeader` produce it, on the production bridge; its first test pins that a draw replaces the icon); the contract test has a control whose icon is rewritten on every draw of the header (`BH.onHeaderDraw`); three more unit tests in `pierre-codeview-bridge.test.js`. With the first version's rule put back, six unit tests, the contract test and the chevron E2E test in both modes fail (`s4/mutation-old-rule-unit.log`, `-contract.log`, `-e2e-codeview.log`). What it does not cover, corrected: a control that is itself replaced by another element between the press and the release (nothing the press was inside of is left in the file; the application keeps one header, one zone and one card element per thing, and a draw replaces none of them); a file whose item moved to another host element between the two; and focus, which a draw still takes from a focused control (F24). *(Phase 6, fifth session: the repair is for a mouse press only.)* The rule as it stood delivered a tap twice. A browser makes a click in two ways. For a mouse press it sends `mousedown` in the task of `pointerdown`, remembers the element that was pressed, and at the release sends `click` only if that element never left the document: the case this entry is about. For a touch it sends `touchstart` after `pointerdown` and remembers nothing; the tap is recognized after the release, and `mousedown`, `mouseup` and `click` go to whatever is under the point then, in the task of `pointerup` for the first tap after a page load and 3 to 8 ms after it for every later one. A draw between touch start and touch end changes nothing about that click. The bridge's timer ran at the end of the release's task, saw no click yet, clicked the control, and the browser's own click followed: two clicks, so a tapped chevron or Viewed checkbox ended where it began and the comment button was clicked twice. The first version did this for the controls whose element survives a draw, on taps after the first of a page (not on every one: when the browser's click happens to come in the task of the release, the timer sees it); the fourth session's rule extended it to the re-stamped chevron (the verifier's round-2 finding: `verify/r2/probe-touch-repair-2-codeview.log` 16 of 16 failed, `probe-touch-repair-3-codeview.log` 8 failed, the default engine 10 of 10 passed; no test of the phase used a touch pointer). Measured in the fifth session, in the application, with the repair switched off in the page: a press, `redrawFile`, the release, on the chevron, the Viewed checkbox, the file comment button and the header's name, both modes, after one plain gesture of the same kind (`s5/probe-kinds-codeview-repair-off.log`). Mouse: `pointerdown`, `mousedown`, `pointerup`, `mouseup`, no `click` (8 of 8). A pen as the browser's protocol delivers one (`Input.dispatchMouseEvent`, `pointerType: 'pen'`): the same four events with `pointerType` `pen`, no `click` (8 of 8). Touch (`Input.dispatchTouchEvent`): `pointerdown`, `touchstart`, `pointerup`, `mousedown`, `mouseup`, one trusted `click`, and the control acted (8 of 8). With a capturing `pointerdown` listener that prevents the default (`s5/probe-kinds-codeview-repair-off-pointerdown-prevented.log`): mouse and pen have no `mousedown` and still no `click`; touch still has `touchstart` and its one `click`. On the default engine every kind gets one trusted `click` (`s5/probe-kinds-filediff.log`, 24 of 24). So the repair is made only for a press the browser handles as a mouse press (`_isMousePress`), and which kind a press is, is read from what the browser did during it, not from the device: a trusted `mousedown` on the pane between the press and the release is a mouse press, a trusted `touchstart` is a touch (`_notePressKind`, capturing listeners on the pane's root), and when neither came, the pointer type recorded at the press decides (`touch` is a touch; anything else is a mouse press, which is the default-prevented case measured above). The pointer type alone would not do for a pen, which a platform reports either way. *Not measured:* a pen reported the way a touch is (`touchstart` with `pointerType` `pen`); the test browser's protocol cannot produce one. By the rule it is a touch; a unit test pins the rule for it, nothing pins the browser. A tap now gets exactly the click the browser sends, as on the default engine. Tests: `tests/e2e/file-controls.spec.js` "A header control pressed while its file is drawn again", now with a mouse, a pen and a touch (five tests each, both modes, both engines; every test begins with a plain gesture of its kind so that its own is not the first of the page; each asserts that exactly one `click` reached the document and what the control did; one taps the chevron three times with a draw in each and expects collapsed, expanded, collapsed). "Exactly one" is asserted with a sentinel, not a wait: a second gesture of the same kind on an element of the test's own, and a timer once its click has arrived (`clicksOfPressDrawRelease`; `tests/CONVENTIONS.md`). `bridge/gutter.spec.js` "a press that is not a mouse button": a tap on the header, the re-stamped control, the zone and the card, with and without a draw, one click each and every one the browser's; the same with the bridge's press handler removed (the browser's rule for a touch, on which the bridge's rule rests); a pen, one click each, the bridge's on the header; a mouse press whose `pointerdown` was default-prevented, the bridge's click. Seven unit tests in `pierre-codeview-bridge.test.js` ("only for a press the browser handles as a mouse press") and one in `header-click-repair.test.js`. With `_isMousePress` made to answer yes always, seven of the ten touch tests of the E2E block fail on `codeview` (the other three got the browser's click in the release's task in that run, which is why a doubled tap was not seen on every tap) and four unit tests fail (`s5/mutation-always-mouse-e2e-codeview.log`, `-unit.log`). The verifier's own round-2 probes on the fixed tree: `touch-repair-2` 16 of 16, `touch-repair-3` 10 of 10, `click-repair` 8 of 8, `touch-repair` 16 of 16, `click-repair-2` 8 of 8 (`s5/verifier-*-codeview.log`). *(Phase 7: two exceptions to "acts once" that the verifier of phase 6 measured are closed, and that statement corrected.)* (A) A press that opens the context menu: with Control held, a primary-button press is the context-menu gesture on macOS; the browser sends `contextmenu` and no click, and the bridge, seeing a mouse press, a draw and no click, clicked the control. The pane's root now listens for `contextmenu` and drops the press in progress. (B) Two presses with no pause between them, the file drawn in each: the click the bridge made up for the first press arrived while the second was in progress and was counted as the second's own, which then went without. `_onRootClick` counts only clicks the browser sent (`isTrusted`). Both are in the E2E block, with a mouse, in both modes and on both engines ("two presses on the chevron with no pause between them ...": two clicks, the file as it was; "a press that opens the context menu acts as it does without a draw ...": Control with the primary button gives what it gives without a draw on that platform, the secondary button nothing), and in three unit tests; with the two lines taken out again three of the four E2E tests fail on `codeview` (the fourth needs the second press to arrive before a 0 ms timer and did not get it in that run). So: a press on a control around a draw of its file acts as the same press does without the draw, for a mouse, a pen and a touch, the context-menu gesture and a second press included. Still as said before: the bridge's click carries no modifier keys. *(H174 fix, session 2, 2026-11-27: the repair seen from Playwright's `check()`.)* The bridge's click is made in a timer task set at the release, one task after a click the browser sends. `locator.check()` reads the box as soon as its release has been handled and throws "Clicking the checkbox did not change its state" when that is before the timer, on a page that then ticks the box and collapses the file. `collapse-scroll-anchor.spec.js` "marking a file viewed keeps its collapsed header where it was stuck" failed that way once in a repeat stage (PR mode; the failure's screenshot shows the file viewed and collapsed). Measured with a draw forced into the press: no click of the browser's and one of the bridge's, the box ticked and the file collapsed in 680 of 680 runs; `check()` threw in 29 of 240 with the H174 fix and 34 of 200 without it (`h174/session2/probe-d/`). The timer is left as it is: that no click is coming is known only when the release's task is over. Every test sets a "Viewed" box through `setViewedBox` in `tests/e2e/diff-dom.js` (a click, then a wait for the box). Section 11, "H174 fix", failure D.
- H141. **A height that is changed back before the second frame was a mismatch "still there two frames later".** This is the one report session 2 left unexplained (`delta: 99.5` in `file-comments-edit-delete.spec.js` "should cancel edit and restore original text", PR, once in six full development-bundle runs under the first rule). Measured in session 3 (`s3/probe-995-1.log`, the test's own steps with the pane sampled in every frame): the comment form opening is +231.5 px, the saved card replacing it −133.91 px, **the card's edit form opening +99.5 px**, and its cancel −99.5 px; the report's `actualStickyHeight − computedStickyHeight` is the edit form's height to the hundredth, and that test is the one that undoes a growth at once. Reproduced on purpose, ten of ten (`s3/probe-995-b-1.log`): the edit form opens and a render is forced in that task (the viewer reports 99.5); at the first frame's callbacks the pane is off by 99.5 and the viewer follows in that frame; the edit is cancelled in a task before the second frame; at the second frame's callbacks the pane is off by −99.5 and the viewer follows in that frame; exact from the third. The first rule compared at the second frame and failed; the rule of H139 (two frames counted from when the pane's box last changed) follows it, because the drawn files' height at the second frame is not what it was at the first. Under load Playwright's `fill` and click fit between two frames; alone they take three (`probe-995-1.log`: growth seen in frame 37, shrink in frame 40), which is why 240 executions alone did not show it. Not known: what forced a render in the task of the edit form in that one run (any publish does; one of three plain executions had a report at the comment form's opening). Pinned as a fixture self-test of the height half of the rule (`fixture-isolation.spec.js` "a frame in which a drawn file's height changed does not count either ...", `layoutValidator.reportThenChangeHeightBetweenFrames`: the layout reads 50 px short for two frames and a form opens between them; 120 of 120, `s3/fixture-rule-height-2.log`; with the rule's `moved` test switched off it fails 6 of 6, `s3/fixture-rule-height-mutation.log`). A first version of that self-test drove the real mechanism alone and failed 1 of 50: the viewer had measured the file before the first frame (the same non-determinism session 1 met), so the self-test holds the viewer's number itself, as `reportWithTheLayoutOff` does.
- H142. **`scrollWidth - clientWidth` of a file's code block reads 1 on `codeview`, and it is not a scroll.** Phase 5's list has "a very long line wraps: nothing scrolls sideways" (`diff-render.spec.js`), whose measure (`wrapInfo`, `diff-dom.js`) took the largest `scrollWidth - clientWidth` of the `pre`, the file's element, the pane's scroller and every code column. With the second session's `ZONE_CSS` it read 1 on `codeview` in both modes and the test failed. The phase's own list does not contain that spec, so three sessions' sequences never ran it; the verifier's did (`verify/e2e-codeview-phase5-list.log`: 280 passed, 2 failed). What the 1 is (`s4/probe-wrap-chain-codeview.log` and `-filediff.log`; the verifier's `probe-wrap-overflow-*.log`): on `codeview` a file's frame is the `pre`'s own 1 px border (on main it is the border of `.pierre-diff-body`, an element around the host, and the `pre` has none); the zone's row is drawn over that border, as the zone is over the frame on main (H125); and for that the unified code column is 1 px wider on each side (`margin-inline: -1px`). The column's box is exactly the `pre`'s border box (277 to 1263 px; the `pre`'s padding box is 278 to 1262), and `scrollWidth` counts from the padding box. Nothing scrolls: `scrollLeft` moves by 0 on the `pre`, the host, the viewer's two wrappers, the scroller and every column, and every ancestor of the `pre` reads 0. **Decided: the CSS stays and the measure asks each box the question that fits it.** A box that scrolls (`overflow-x` `auto`, `scroll` or `hidden`): how far it can be scrolled. A box that does not: how far something sticks out past its border (its own right border width is taken off `scrollWidth - clientWidth`). On main no measured box that does not scroll has a border, so every number there is the one it was; on `codeview` the only number that changes is the `pre`'s, by its border. *Not done: measuring scroll containers only.* With wrapping off and the columns made not to scroll or clip, the long line leaves the `pre` by 16,308 px and the pane's scroller still reads 0 on `codeview`: the viewer's wrapper around the items has layout containment, and an overflow does not pass it. Only the `pre` and the host report that case (on main `.pierre-diff-body` scrolls and would). So the boxes that do not scroll stay in the measure. *Not done either: CSS without the overhang.* It needs the frame's side borders to be something other than the `pre`'s border (a positioned pseudo-element over a padded `pre`, or a border on another box), a second look check of every configuration, for a number no reader can see; and it would not make the measure sound, because the lone column of an entirely added or removed file in split layout carries a stretched annotation row past its own box by design on both engines (494 px for a deleted file's zone row on `codeview`; any card in such a file on main). The measure has its own test next to its user, in both modes and on both engines ("the measure of that test sees a line that does not wrap, in a column that scrolls and in one that does not"): 0 as drawn; more than 5,000 in the vendor's scrolling layout; more than 5,000 with nothing scrolling or clipping; 0 again (`setLineLayout`, `diff-dom.js`). Rule for the phases that follow: a `codeview` sequence runs the lists of the earlier phases too (`accept-list.sh` of the phase-6 evidence does from this session on; `tests/CONVENTIONS.md`).
- H143. **A click on the viewer's expand control is lost when the file is drawn between the press and the release, on both engines, and is left that way.** The vendor handles its expand controls with a `click` listener on the `pre` (`InteractionManager.syncPointerListeners`), every draw of a file re-creates its rows and separators (H43), and a browser sends no click when the pressed element left the document. Measured in the application, both modes: a press on the first expand control of `src/utils.js`, a comment reload that draws a card under the file's last row, the release. Default engine: `pointerdown`, `pointerup`, no `click`, 25 line-number cells before and after; without the reload a `click` and 45 cells (`s4/probe-expand-click-2-filediff.log`). `codeview`, with the file published between the two: the same events and nothing expanded; without, a `click` and the gap's rows (`s4/probe-expand-click-codeview.log`). So this is main's behaviour, not the engine's, and it stays (parity first). The bridge's repair of H140 does not reach into the viewer's rows; H140's sentence that they "work on pointer events" holds for lines and line numbers and not for the expand controls, and the comment in `_handleRootPointerDown` says so now. A repair would be a fix of main (the pressed control would have to be found again among the new rows by its hunk index and direction): a candidate for the defects issue (#638), not part of the migration. How it was found: `pr-page.spec.js` "should expand context and show correct line numbers with offset" (phase 5's list; a test of main's that clicked as soon as the expand controls existed) failed once on `codeview` in the fourth session's whole-suite run (PR mode, nothing expanded; `final-s4/e2e-codeview-whole.log`) and, repeated under load, once in 120 executions on the default engine (the cells did not grow; `s4/repeat60-default-pr-page-141.log`); on `codeview` 120 of 120 in the same repeat and 30 of 30 alone. It was not in any earlier run of either engine's logs. The test now waits for the file's upgraded diff and for the diff to settle before it counts and clicks, which is what `tests/CONVENTIONS.md` asks of a pointer on a row: 240 of 240 on each engine (`s4/repeat60-*-pr-page-141-settled-*.log`). A draw that is not part of the load (a comment reload a test starts itself) can still fall into a click on an expand control; no spec does that. *Phase 9, session 6:* a file is drawn once more after it reports settled. A changed file's rows are replaced 19 to 54 ms after `waitForContentUpgrade` and `waitForDiffSettled` return (30 to 74 ms on main at `3223ecce`; 12 of 12 loads on each tree, both modes), and a context file is drawn again 128 to 142 ms after a range was added to it, with nothing changed. A press that straddles such a draw lost its click in two whole-suite runs (`context-files.spec.js:246` in the verifier's development-bundle run; `pr-page.spec.js:141` in the second whole E2E run of session 6's first proof run) and, with a recorder, in 1 of 1,200 runs of the second test's flow alone on main (press on the control, rows replaced 6 ms later, release 4 ms after that, no click). Nothing a test can wait for announces that draw, so the E2E helpers for expand controls read from the page whether a click arrived and make it again when none did (`clickOnce`, `clickDelivered` in `tests/e2e/diff-dom.js`; `tests/CONVENTIONS.md`). What asks for either draw was not traced. Evidence: `evidence/phase-9/s6/` (`expand-quiet-{final,main}.log`, `expand-probe-main-x600.log`, `probe-gapdraw-{dev,shipped}.log`, `probe-retry-{with,without}-click-read.log`).

**Added by phase 7**

- H144. **One key press starts two navigations.** The review panel (`AIPanel`, its own `keydown` listener) and the suggestion navigator (through `KeyboardShortcuts`) both answer `j` and `k`, each with a cursor of its own, so a press usually asks for two different cards (measured on both engines: the first `j` asked for the suggestion in the last file and then for the one in the first). On main both run to their end and the one whose scroll is issued last wins, which is the navigator's, because its listener runs second and the panel's target is resolved at once. On the virtualized viewer step 3 of `revealInDiff` moves the pane, and microtasks run between two listeners of one event: the panel's navigation had already sent the viewer to the far file when the navigator, started a moment later, found its own card still in the document and scrolled to it natively; a frame later the pane was at the far file and the navigator's card was gone (N11 "the navigator keys walk suggestions in files far apart", both modes). Two rules in `revealInDiff`, on `codeview` only: the navigation that started last wins (`_revealGen`; an older one stops at its next check and reports itself cancelled), and before the pane is moved for a target that is not mounted, every handler of the input event is let start (`_afterInputHandlers`, a 0 ms timer). After the first `j` the pane is at 181 px on both engines, after the second at the last file. The two cursors are main's and stay.
- H145. **Collapsing the file the reader is inside is the bridge's to land.** `setCollapsed` asks, before it publishes, whether the pane's top edge is inside the file (`_isReaderInside`: mounted, starting above the pane and ending below its top) and, if so, sends an instant item target after the publish: the collapsed file's header is flush under the toolbar and the files after it follow. A file whose top is in view, a file that is not mounted and an expand are not touched. On main nothing scrolls and the header is lost above the viewport (H48); the difference is deliberate (`COLLAPSE_INSIDE_FILE`, H51) and is in the list for the user. The golden comparison reports it as a landing difference in every dump, by design. **Corrected in phase 10 (merge of main `6f06bbb1`, v5.4.3): this is main's contract now, and it is main's to define.** Main's `760fe822` "Keep a collapsed file's header where it was stuck" gives the old application the same behaviour, and states it more exactly than this entry did: the header stays at the viewport position it had (which is flush under the toolbar while it is stuck, and partly above the toolbar's edge when the last rows of its file have pushed it up), the next file is directly below it, a header that was not stuck does not move, and an expand is not anchored. `setCollapsed` was changed to that (H172): `_isReaderInside` is `_stuckHeaderOffset`, the item target carries the header's measured offset, the publish and the target are one flush, and an expand leaves the scroll position alone. The difference is gone from the list for the owner (section 14, row 9) and from the golden comparison, which now treats this landing like any other.
- H146. **The expansion map belongs to the diff it was made on, and the viewer draws two diffs of a file for a while.** After a fold the record's metadata has the expanded lines as ranges and the map must not be applied to its hunks; the viewer goes on drawing the old diff, which needs the map (F5, F28). Three paths derive a mounted record again: `addContextRanges`, `upgradeFileContents`, and `reconcile` for a context record whose stored ranges or contents changed (`_applyContextEntry`: a chat link or a tour stop to another part of a context file, a range pushed by the server). Each calls `_retireExpansions` after the derive and before the publish. It asks the row renderer which diff the next draw will be (`getDiffForNextRender`, F28). *The diff on screen:* the map is kept and the diff it is for remembered (`record._expansionsOf`); while that is set, the bridge's own readers get no map (`_expansionMap` answers null: the oracle and a second fold read the record's ranges). `_watchForNextDiff` takes over the row renderer's `onRenderUpdate` and empties the map in the call that stages the new diff's result; `_dropRetiredExpansions`, from `_onPostRender`, does the same one draw later when that callback was not called (the new diff is then drawn once with the old map, and again without it, in one task: nothing is painted in between, but the development validator reports that pass). *That callback is not called for a file that leaves the mounted window during the wait:* the viewer does not stage a highlight result for an item that is not mounted, and on the file's return it draws the record's metadata at once. Until phase 8 the retired map was still there then, so the fallback ran on every such return and the development validator reported `sticky container height does not match computed layout ... delta: 40` (found by phase 7's verification; nothing wrong was painted).
