Since phase 8 the `unmount` branch of `_onPostRender` drops a retired map (`_dropExpansionsOfUnmounted`: the watch is given back, the map emptied, the item's cached height reset), the rule `_retireExpansions` already had for a file that is not on screen, and the return is one draw of the new diff (`bridge/contents.spec.js` "a file that leaves the mounted window while a reveal awaits display ..."; with the call taken out the test fails on the state while the file is away, `evidence/phase-8/mutation-unmount-expansions.log`). *The new diff* (plain text, a result the viewer already holds, a present diff that is not highlighted yet): the map is emptied and the item's cached height reset before the publish, which draws the new diff in the same task; nothing is taken over. Before the second session this case went through the fallback and the validator reported it (`delta: 80` for a reveal in a plain-text file). The renderer gets its own callback back wherever the watch ends (`_unwatchNextDiff`: the hook itself, the fallback, a list write, a discarded record). A file that is not on screen loses the map at once; a list write replaces the instances and the map with them. What goes wrong in that window, on the phase-6 bridge as well (measured in phase 7's verification, `evidence/phase-7/verify/r2/probe-expand-while-pending-*.log`): an expand control clicked in the fraction of a second between the fold and the new diff's display is not lost, it is applied to another gap. The control belongs to the diff on screen and the viewer records the click by hunk index, which the new diff counts differently (clicked: the "up" control of the gap at lines 1500-2484; drawn: lines 539-558, right after the newly revealed line 538). Not fixed here: the window is one worker round trip, and a fix has to translate a hunk index of one diff into the other inside the vendor's click path. A candidate for the defects issue (#638).
- H147. **What the re-diff brings with it from main** (decision D5a). (a) The changed rows after the upgrade are the vendor's pairing of the two contents, which differs from git's in some files (H118): a line the patch shows as deleted can be drawn as context afterwards. Annotations are addressed by line and side and stay on their lines; diff positions and hunk-summary anchors come from the patch and are not affected. (b) The patch's own lines stay on screen through the parity ranges, which cover the new side: a hunk that only deletes has none. (c) Read in the code, not run: with "Hide whitespace" on, the patch comes from `git diff -w` and the contents do not, so the diff of the contents shows whitespace-only changes again in a file once its contents have loaded. That is main's behaviour today on the per-file renderer (`instance.render({ oldFile, newFile })` with no diff options) and is copied with the rest; it is a candidate for the defects issue (#638), where the fix would be the vendor's `parseDiffOptions` on both engines.
- H148. **A flake of main's suite explained: a reload right after the first `goto` logs `Error loading PR: TypeError: Failed to fetch`.** `panel-resize.spec.js` opens the pull request, clears two `localStorage` keys and reloads at once; when the reload comes before the first answer, the browser aborts the page's first request and the top-level loader (`PRManager.loadPR`; in Local mode `LocalManager.loadLocalReview`, "Error loading local review") logs the rejection. The console guard named the loaders those two call (`Error loading files`, `Error loading local diff`, ...) and not the two themselves, so the line failed a test about once in several hundred runs (first seen in the phase-6 verification, `panel-resize.spec.js:115`). Reproduced on demand in both modes by holding the first request until the reload (`fixture-isolation.spec.js` "A page reloaded while its first request is in flight": failed with exactly those two console lines before the change, `wip-guard-before.log`). Settled where the cause is: both prefixes are on the guard's list of loaders a navigation can interrupt, with that test as their proof; `panel-resize.spec.js` is as on main. *(Phase 10: the browser of Playwright 1.65, Chromium 158, delivers no console line for a request that a reload cuts short. Measured in both modes, with the held request aborted after the reload and with it left hanging: no `Error loading` line in either; a request that fails without a reload still logs it (`evidence/phase-10/reload-test/probe.jsonl`). On Chromium 147 the line came in every run. The test's last assertion waited for that line and failed 2 of 2 on the merged tree. It now proves the two halves apart: the reload did interrupt the first request, which was never answered, and anything a loader logged about it has the known form; and the line itself, written as the loader writes it, is on the guard's list (the test fails at its end when the entry is taken out). The entries stay: an older browser and a request that fails for another reason both still produce the line.)*
- H149. **A wait and a read in two evaluations is a race when the page's data can move between them.** `contextFileState` of the `codeview` adapter waited until a context file's rows were the ones last published and then read rows and data in a second evaluation; a range added in between moved the data at once and the rows a worker round trip later, and the read's own consistency check threw inside an `expect.poll` (once in this phase's runs, `chat-file-links.spec.js` "a link to a line outside the shown range ...", Local mode). The wait and the read are one evaluation now.
- H150. **`ensureContextFile`'s visibility check is narrow on purpose.** It answers without a request only for a line that no stored range holds and that the context item shows all the same (revealed by hand, or in a gap of five lines or fewer). A range is asked line by line (`_contextItemShowsLines`, phase 8; at most the 500 lines a stored range may have): until then only its two ends were asked, and a range whose ends were shown by two separate expansions with hidden lines between them was answered without the new range that main adds. A line inside a stored range goes through the range logic as on main, so a tour stop or a link near the edge of a range still extends the range to the window around the line; widening the check to "any visible line" would stop that.
- H151. **When a file's contents arrive under the reader, the viewer keeps the line and moves the pane; the per-file renderer keeps the pane and moves the line.** The diff of the two contents has more rows than the patch above some lines (a fourth line of context before a hunk). On the virtualized viewer the first line in view stays where it is and the pane's scroll position grows by the rows added above it (17.39 px per row; `bridge/contents.spec.js` "the line at the top of the pane stays there ..."); on main the scroll position stays and the line moves down. A tool that puts a file's top edge at the pane's top and then reads it can therefore find the file a row higher than it put it, when the contents arrive in between. That happened to the golden dump under processor load (unit tests run while it ran): `D.dumpPart` of the `codeview` adapter decided "this window starts at the file's top" from the file's position and left out a first separator that was in the DOM, 17 px under the pinned header (two dumps of 32, `corpus-many/d01/file01.ts`; reproduced with eight busy processes, and shown not to happen on the default engine under the same load). The adapter now asks the viewer where the drawn rows start (`instance.renderRange.startingLine`); three loaded dumps of the same configuration are identical to the default engine's. Nothing may run next to a dump, a look check or an E2E run.
- H152. **"Centre" is not the same place on the two engines.** A tour scrolls to its stop with `block: 'center'`, which centres it in the scroll container. On main the toolbar and the tour bar are pinned inside that container, so its centre is half their height above the middle of the diff a reader sees; on the virtualized viewer they are above the container. Measured by the look check's new landing: the first stop 256.64 px below the pinned bars on main and 313.64 px on `codeview`, 57 px lower. `pr.css` gives a tour stop that much `scroll-margin-bottom` on `codeview` (the sum of the two heights, read on `.diff-view`, where they are still the page's), which puts it at 256.64 px. A later `block: 'center'` navigation to anything else would need the same.
- H153. **A navigation can land a fraction of a pixel apart on the two engines.** A scroll that a navigation asks for ends on a whole pixel of the scroll offset. The target is at a fraction of a pixel when wrapped rows above it have heights that are not whole pixels, and the two engines scroll two different containers (main's holds the toolbar and its own padding above the files), so the same target is rounded from two different fractions. Measured by the look check's second page in the `edge` corpus, a click on a finding: the card 449.95 px below the pinned bars on main and 449.27 px on `codeview` (unified), 397.70 and 397.02 to 397.14 (split); every other landing of the 24 configurations is equal to the hundredth, and where the pane is put by an exact scroll position every number is equal. In unified layout and the light theme the first row of pixels under the pinned file header then shows the next slice of a text line (2 pixels of one glyph outside the tolerance). `visual.mjs` accepts a landing number within 1 px, which is the golden comparison's rule for landings, and those four screenshot pairs with at most 4 pixels outside the tolerance; nothing else of a landing is accepted.
- H154. **"Connected" is not "drawn": a card of a mounted file can be in the document with no place on screen.** The first session of phase 7 decided step 3 of `revealInDiff` ("bring the target into the mounted window") from `element.isConnected`, in `pr.js`, in `scrollToAnnotation` and in `whenAnnotationRendered`. For a file of fewer than 5,000 rows that is the same thing, and the corpus's large file has 4,000. For a longer file it is not (F29): with the reader at the top of an added file of 9,000 lines, a click on a finding on line 8,000, the navigator key and a click on a comment on line 8,200 moved nothing, with no toast and no error, where the default engine scrolls to each (the phase's verifier, `evidence/phase-7/verify/probe-huge-window-*.log`). "Drawn" is now asked of the slot the card is shown through (`PierreCodeViewBridge.isElementDrawn`), which is also right for a card whose row the viewer dropped (H119), for one of a line the diff on screen does not have yet, and for a card that minimize mode hides (a slot, no box). `whenFileMounted` is replaced by `whenElementDrawn`: a card in the comment zone of a mounted file whose first block of rows is not drawn (the reader beyond row 10,000) is in the same position. Measured after the fix on a 12,000-line file, both modes: every one of those navigations arrives, and the card lands 176.5 px from the top of the page on both engines at every step (`evidence/phase-7/s2/probe-landing-trace-*.log`); on `codeview` in about 0.3 s, on the default engine in about 1.5 s of smooth scrolling. *One state keeps "attached" on purpose:* a card whose line was revealed a moment ago is attached while its file still draws the old diff (F5), and the display that is on its way draws it where the pane is. Main's scroll helper is written for that (`scrollIntoViewStable` waits up to two seconds for the card's box and then corrects), and `inline-feedback-context.spec.js` "suggestions spanning a visible hunk and hidden endpoint render and navigate" holds the worker's result back and requires the helper to have started meanwhile. So for a mounted file whose new diff awaits display the bridge sends nothing and hands the card over at once, as the first session did (`_displayWillDraw`), with one exception: a file drawn in row windows, whose display draws the rows of the window the pane is in; there the pane is taken to the line first and the card is waited for (reachable only in a coloured file the reader has expanded to 5,000 rows or more: a changed file of that size is drawn as plain text, which is displayed at once). The first form of this fix asked "drawn" there too, waited four seconds for a display the test holds back, and failed that test in both modes in all three whole-suite runs of the sequence's first start (`s2/final-aborted-1/`); the sequence was stopped and started again on the corrected tree. A test that proves a navigation arrived asserts `toBeInViewport()`, never attachment (`tests/CONVENTIONS.md`); the E2E corpus for it needs more than 5,000 rows, and more than 10,000 for the zone (N11, "Navigation to a card far down a file longer than the rows the viewer draws").
- H155. **A card on a line that a reveal has just added to a long file: three things have to hold together, and the first form of this entry had one of them, proven on a recording stand-in only.** *What phase 8 first built (rejected by its verifier).* `_displayWillDraw` hands a card over at once when its file is on screen with a new diff awaiting display and the file is drawn whole, because that display draws the card where the pane is. "Drawn whole" was read from the rows on screen; a reveal can take a file from fewer rows than a row window to more, so the rows of the pending diff are counted too (`PierreBridge.drawnRowCount`) and at 5,000 or more the line target is sent. That was tested against the recording viewer only, with the remark that the contract workload has no file that crosses the limit. *What the real viewer does* (the verifier's probes, `evidence/phase-8/verify/probe-h155*.log`, `probe-crossing-diagnosis*.log`; repeated and explained in `evidence/phase-8/r2/probe-1.log`): a context file of 7,000 lines showing lines 1 to 4,995, a card on line 6,500, lines 6,495 to 6,505 revealed (5,006 rows). The line target was sent and the pane went to the end of the file; the new diff was displayed with 5,000 rows, ending at line 6,499; the card was never drawn and the wait ran out after four seconds; with files after the long one the validator reported the sticky container 160 px short three times. With the count of the pending rows switched off the card was handed over at once and was not drawn either. The cause is neither the navigation nor the publish: it is the viewer's choice of which rows to draw, F30. *What is built now.* (1) `PierreBridge.coverPaneWithRowWindow` (F30, H162): the rows after a block boundary are drawn as soon as the pane's window reaches them, so a pane that is at the card's line has the card's row. (2) `drawnRowCount` in `_displayWillDraw`, as before: without it a card of a pending diff of 5,000 rows or more is handed over with the reader anywhere in the file and no target sent. (3) `whenAnnotationRendered` takes the pane to the line once more when the diff that awaited display is displayed and the card is still not drawn. A target sent while the old diff is on screen is laid out with the new diff but cannot go beyond the pane's present height; where the long file ends the pane and the reveal adds thousands of rows (3,600 lines revealed at once in the only file, or in the last of thirty-one), the first target stops at the old diff's end. With files after the long one the pane is high enough and nothing is sent twice. *Proof.* `bridge/annotations.spec.js` "navigating to a card on a line that was just revealed in a long file", eight cases on the production bridge and the real development bundle: the file first, in the middle, last and alone in a pane of thirty-one; a file that was drawn in windows before; the large reveal alone, last and in the middle. Each asserts what `_revealSteps` needs: the card is handed over drawn, after the display, the browser's scroll puts it inside the pane, the layout audit is clean and the validator is silent; and which targets were sent when. Mutation runs (`evidence/phase-8/r2/mutations.txt`): without (1) five of the eight fail, with the walk and the two tail tests of `view.spec.js` and the E2E test; without (2) seven of the eight; without (3) the two cases that need the second target. The verifier's own probe specs pass on the fixed tree (`r2/verifier-probes-on-fixed-tree.log`: I1, I3 and I4 resolve in 1.3 s with the card inside the pane, audit clean, 0 validator reports; its variants A to H draw every row). Unit tests in `pierre-bridge.test.js` hold the arithmetic and the wiring only.
- H156. **The page's card resolvers have no document fallback, and tests must not need one.** `findCommentCard`, `findSuggestionCard`, the review panel's two card navigations and the suggestion navigator answer from the bridge (by annotation id or by data) and from the file-comment zone registry; `document.querySelector` for a card is gone with the table renderer, whose rows were always in the document. A card of a file that is not mounted exists (the bridge builds it on demand) and is not attached. What is still looked up in the document, through `revealInDiff`'s `findElement` as a last resort: an external thread that was filed in a zone or a file's wrapper (`ExternalCommentManager.findThreadCard`), and the suggestion navigator's own lookup by suggestion id, both unchanged from main. Unit tests that need cards give the page manager a bridge: the production one over the recording viewer (`createBridgeHarness`), with a stand-in that puts the bridge's own card elements into the document after each annotation change where a test reads what a reader would see (`submitted-comment-ui.test.js`, `external-comment-manager.test.js`), or `cardBridge` of `tests/utils/page-manager.js` where only `revealInDiff`'s card questions matter.
- H157. **`.gitignore` has `test-*.js`.** A new file named `test-<anything>.js` anywhere in the tree is ignored without a word (`tests/e2e/test-server.js` is tracked because it was added by force). Phase 8 wrote `tests/contract/harness/test-extras.js`, saw that `git status` did not list it, and renamed it `vendor-extras.js`. Check `git status --short --ignored` after adding a file.
- H158. **`hydratePartialDiff` in the contract suite comes from a second copy of the library.** The shipped bundle exports only what production reads (correction 25). The vendor contract tests still build "a second diff of the same file, carrying its contents" with the vendor's own `hydratePartialDiff` (about twenty call sites; the facts about the pending swap, the row window and expansion were measured on its result), so the suite's global setup bundles that one function from the installed package into `tests/.cache/pierre-diffs-test-extras.js` and the fixture loads it next to the vendor bundle as `window.VendorTestExtras`. It is a pure function of plain objects; anything that needs the viewer's own module state (the worker pool, the highlighter cache) must not be added there.
- H159. **The reference of the parity checks is the per-file renderer at the phase-7 commit, not main itself.** Main has no parity scripts and no `diff-dom.js`, so `reference.mjs` cannot run in a checkout of `3223ecce`. The saved set (`evidence/phase-8/reference/`, `REFERENCE.json` names commit `3d862040`, clean) was produced by that commit's default engine, whose golden dump is identical to the one phase 7 stored for the same engine (`before/reference-golden-vs-phase7-filediff.txt`), and every phase from 2 on showed its default-engine dump identical to the previous phase's. What changed on that engine between main and phase 7 is in the ledger: the two defects of main removed in phase 3 (H51, H60) and `mergeContextRanges`' line starts (F23, proven invisible there). The scratch directory that holds the set is cleaned by the operating system (files not read for three days are deleted): phase 9 must copy what it needs or refresh access times.
- H160. **Taking a class out of a selector takes weight out of it.** The viewer's rules in `pr.css` were written under `.diff-view.codeview-engine`, and some of them override a general rule for the same element. Un-scoped to `.diff-view` they were one class lighter, and one override lost: `.diff-view diffs-container .file-comments-zone { background: none; border-bottom: 0 }` (two classes and a type) against the general `.file-comments-zone:has(.file-comment-card:not(.posted-comment-hidden))` (three classes: `:has()` weighs as much as its heaviest argument). Every file with a file-level comment then had the zone's background and a second 1 px line under it, and was a pixel taller. No test saw it: the E2E suite passed three times, the contract gates passed (the viewer measures what is there), the golden dump reads rows. The look check failed on it at once (113 number groups and 48 of 480 screenshot pairs, all on files with a filled zone; `evidence/phase-8/final-run1-look-check-failed/`). Fixed by giving every selector of the block the weight it had: each starts with `.main-layout .diff-view` (the pane is inside `.main-layout` on both pages), so the cascade among the rules that remain is what it was with the switch on. Pinned by `tests/e2e/file-comments.spec.js` "the zone draws no line of its own under a file comment ..." in both modes (computed border and background of the zone and of its container; with the selector weakened the test fails, `evidence/phase-8/mutation-zone-specificity.log`). Any later simplification of those selectors has to pass the look check, not only the suites.
- H161. **The comment button is the button of the row under the pointer at the click, and rows move under a resting pointer.** `openLineCommentForm` (the E2E helper) hovered a line's number and clicked the comment button. When a card above the line had just been attached, the rows below it moved a frame or two later, the browser reported the row that was then under the pointer, the button became that row's, and the form opened on another line (seen on a neighbouring deleted line, and on a line of the hunk above). The test that saves from that form then failed on the line of the saved comment: `offscreen-mutations.spec.js` "a comment saved while a comment reload is in flight is kept ...", Local mode, once in the second run of phase 8's final sequence, once in 80 executions repeated here, and once in 160 at the phase-7 commit with the switch on (`evidence/phase-8/flake-offscreen-457-*.log`): not new in phase 8, and not seen in phase 7's whole-suite runs. The application does what a reader would see (the button is drawn on the row under the pointer). The helper now reads which lines the form it opened is on (its first and last line and its side), closes a form that does not hold the line it aimed at and repeats the gesture; a button that keeps opening the wrong line still fails, after the helper's timeout. A form on a selected range that holds the line is the right form: the first version of the check compared the form's first line with the line aimed at and closed the range form of `comment-forms.spec.js` "a range dragged on the additions side in split layout ..." (both modes failed in the next whole-suite run, `evidence/phase-8/final-e2e-run1-helper-too-strict/`). 300 of 300 executions of the flaky test after the corrected change.
- H162. **The bridge replaces a method of a vendor class.** `PierreBridge.coverPaneWithRowWindow` (F30) assigns `VirtualizedFileDiff.prototype.computeRenderRangeFromWindow`, once per page (the function it installs carries a mark, so a second bridge or a second evaluation of the script wraps nothing twice). This is a larger step than reading an internal: every diff item of every viewer on the page goes through it, for the rows it draws (`render`) and for its part of the pane's layout (`getAdvancedStickySpecs(windowSpecs)`), which is why both agree. What it assumes of the vendor, all pinned: the method's name, its arguments `(fileDiff, fileTop, { top, bottom })` and its answer `{ startingLine, totalLines, bufferBefore, bufferAfter }`; `instance.height` as the item's layout height; `bufferAfter` as the height of what is left out after the drawn rows, measured from the item's end less its bottom padding; that the answer for a window centred on a row is that row's block and the one before. And of the configuration: a block of `ROWS_PER_ITEM_WINDOW` rows is far taller than the pane's window (5,000 rows of 17.4 px against a window of about 1,300 px), so the block of the first row left out and the one before it hold the whole window. With a class that has no such method the bridge warns once at construction and the viewer keeps the vendor's row window; a first answer that needs no correction is returned untouched, and an answer that draws nothing is never preferred to one that draws rows. **At a vendor bump:** the two F30 tests of `row-window.spec.js` fail when the vendor covers the pane itself (then delete the wrapper, the export and this entry); the bridge tests of F30 fail when the wrapper stopped working. Alternatives measured and not taken: a row window larger than every file (F31: the viewer's height measurement fails beyond 2^18 px); a row window smaller than the pane, which the vendor's formula does cover (F26: the trim at every scroll step); sending the scroll after the display or publishing again (the rows are not drawn at any scroll position: F30). Candidate for the upstream report with F26 and F31 (the user files those; #638 lists them).
- H163. **The end of the pane is an estimate until the files there are drawn, and a reader who scrolls to it is kept at it.** *(Widened and fixed in phase 9; the entry's first form spoke of a card's height only.)* The pane's height is rows times line height for everything the viewer has not drawn. Two things are taller than their estimate: the card of a line that has no row yet (the first form of this entry: a test that set the scroll position to the scroll height once and then expected the file's last line in the viewport passed 24 of 30 times, `evidence/phase-8/r2/repeat15-e2e-tail.log`), and every wrapping line. Phase 8's verifier measured the second on a 12,000-line modified file with a wrapping line in every 40, both modes: the pane grew from 226,799 to 229,846 px after the first scroll to the end, which was therefore 3,047 px short; a second scroll reached the end.
  - *Reachable on ordinary reviews* (phase 9; files of fewer than 5,000 rows, drawn whole). Eight untracked files of 600 lines with a wrapping line in every ten, the real server on a throwaway repository, Local mode, 1440x900. A position written by script (`scrollTop = scrollHeight`): 2,095 px short on this branch (86,466 px estimated, 88,561 px measured), 398 px short on main at `3223ecce` (100,238 and 100,636). The End key, which the browser scrolls smoothly so that files are measured on the way: 12,569 px short on this branch without the fix below (the E2E test with the fix taken out), 1,911 px short on main after the first press in two runs and 777 px in a third, and at the end after the second press. So main has the defect in kind (its lazily rendered files have estimated heights too; #638 "File positions are estimates until each file renders") and this branch had it larger.
  - *Fixed for the reader's own scroll toward the end.* `PierreBridge._followEndAfterReaderScroll`: a scroll toward the end that the reader started (a wheel step down, a touch dragged up, a key that scrolls down pressed outside a text field; `_noteReaderScrollInput`, within `READER_SCROLL_MS`) and that reaches the end of the pane, as it is now or as it was when they started, is followed: for as long as the pane goes on growing and nothing else has moved it, it is put at its end again (`_startEndFollow`: until the height has not grown for `END_FOLLOW_STILL_FRAMES` frames, `END_FOLLOW_MAX_MS` at most). The input is used up by the follow it starts. Everything else the reader does ends a follow and forgets the input: a wheel step up, a touch dragged down, any other key (one that scrolls up, one typed into a form), a press anywhere on the pane. So does a scroll target or a view restore of the application's (`_scrollTo`, `_restoreView`): a navigation that starts right after is not pulled back. Measured after the fix, same repository: End key 0 px short, wheel 0 px short, one gesture each, in three runs.
  - *Round 2 of phase 9: the first form of the follow undid the reader's next scroll.* It kept the end of the pane as it was at the reader's input for 1.5 s and took every position beyond that (old, smaller) end for "the end reached": a Page Up or an Arrow Up pressed within 1.5 s of End was put back at the end (found by the phase's verifier, 6 of 6 in both modes; `evidence/phase-9/verify/probe-endfollow-*.log`), and while the scrollbar was held the 1.5 s did not apply at all. Fixed as described above: the input is used up, and every other input forgets it. The verifier's probe after the fix: Page Up 188, 616, 1004 and 1306 ms after End leaves the reader 557 px above the end, Arrow Up 40 px, both modes (`evidence/phase-9/r2/verifier-probes-after-fix.log`).
  - *The scrollbar.* Nothing is written while the reader holds the pane's own scrollbar (a press of the primary button whose target is the pane itself, at the pane's right edge, until its release: `_pressOnScrollbar`; what makes such a press a held scrollbar is under "A press that holds no scrollbar" below). A held thumb decides the position: the browser moves it by as much as the pointer moves, at every pointer move, so a position written during a drag is put back at the next move. Measured by the verifier with a real scrollbar (headless Chromium started without `--hide-scrollbars`, a 16 px classic scrollbar on the pane): with the first form of the follow 5 and 6 follows started during one slow drag to the bottom, and the pane jumped about 4,000 px to the end and back each time; with no follow, none. Now: 0 follows and 0 frames in which the position goes back during the drag, and a thumb dragged up from the bottom stays where the reader put it (`scrollbar-drag.spec.js`, both modes).
  - *The scrollbar, measured against main (session 3 of phase 9).* Round 2 wrote here that a thumb held at the bottom of its track was short "by where the browser maps that point of the track to, not growth", without having dragged main's. That was wrong. The same real-thumb drags on main at `3223ecce` and on this branch, the eight-file review, PR and Local alike (`evidence/phase-9/s3/probe-scrollbar-compare.log`): a slow drag (40 moves, a frame each) to 3 px above the pane's bottom edge leaves main at its end (0 px short, its pane having grown from 97,571 to 101,015 px on the way) and this branch 3,784 px short, held and let go (its pane grows from 85,695 to 100,956 px: the estimates are off by four times as much). The mechanism is the browser's: a thumb moves by the pointer's movement from where it is, a pane that grows slides the thumb back up under the pointer, and the pointer is at the bottom of the track before the thumb is. What the pointer is ahead by, the browser gives back when the pane is laid out again (one move to 2 px past the end of the thumb's travel: main 159 px short, this branch 1,891 px, held).
  - *The release (session 3).* `_followEndAfterScrollbarRelease(press, event)` puts the pane at its end, and keeps it there while the files at the end are measured, when the scrollbar is let go after it was taken to the end. That is one of: the pane is at its end at the release (round 2's rule); the thumb was at the end of its track during the press and was not taken back up, and the pane has grown since (`_noteScrollbarScroll`, called from the pane's scroll listener: a scroll that leaves the pane at its end sets it, a scroll that goes up clears it, a scroll down that leaves it short, which is the browser giving back what the pointer is ahead by, changes nothing); or the pointer was pulled down the scrollbar to the pane's bottom edge or below it (`_pulledToTrackEnd`: the pointer travelled at least 8 px down, the pane is below where it was at the press, and the press was let go at the bottom edge; session 3 asked instead that the press began within 16 px of the pane's edge, which session 6 found to be too little, below). A press is forgotten at the next pointer move the page is told of (`_handlePointerMove`): with no button down its release never arrived, and the release of a later press elsewhere is not taken for it; with a button down it holds no scrollbar (session 6). After the fix, same probe: slow drag to the bottom edge, held 2,883 px short, let go 0; one move to the end of the track, held 1,891 px short, let go 0 (main 159).
  - *A press that holds no scrollbar (session 6 of phase 9; the verifier's second rejection).* Session 3's rule took every press whose target is the pane itself for a press on the scrollbar, and for the pull to the bottom edge asked only that it began within `max(scrollbar width, 16)` px of the pane's right edge. Nothing asked whether a thumb was held. Where the scrollbar has no width of its own that strip is the pane's right padding (`padding: 0 17px`): a press there, dragged down and let go at the pane's bottom edge, sent the reader to the end of the review. The verifier, default headless Chromium, both modes: 3,122 to 44,843 px on the 60-file review, the same with a text selection dragged into the code, and 0 to 87,790 px on the eight-file review (`evidence/phase-9/verify/r2/probe-strip-{1,2}.log`). It was reachable in an ordinary Chrome too: the application's style sheet gives the pane a scrollbar 8 px wide (`.main-layout ::-webkit-scrollbar`), so 8 of the strip's 16 px were padding beside it (3,000 to 87,790 px with scrollbars shown and the fix taken out, `evidence/phase-9/s6/fixes/thumb-evidence-mutation-e2e.log`).
    - *Measured: what tells a held scrollbar from a press on the padding* (Chromium; `s6/probe-events-1.log`, `probe-events-overlay.log`, `plain-overlay-thumb.log`). While a thumb is held the page is told of no `pointermove` between the `pointerdown` and the `pointerup` (0 in drags of 8 moves of a 16 px scrollbar styled by a test, of the application's own 8 px one, and of a scrollbar with no width of its own on a page without the application), of no `selectstart` and of no `click`; it sees the pane's `scroll` events only. Of a press on the padding it is told every move (8 of 8, `buttons` 1), a `selectstart` at the press and a `click` at the release.
    - *The rule now.* A press is a candidate when it is of the primary button, its target is the pane itself, and it is at the pane's right edge: within the scrollbar's own width where it has one, within 16 px where it has none (`_pressOnScrollbar`). The candidate is forgotten at the first pointer move the page is told of (`_handlePointerMove`). At the release nothing is done unless the pane's position changed under the press (`moved`, set by `_noteScrollbarScroll`, and the position at the release against the last one seen), and the pull to the bottom edge also needs the pane to be below where it was at the press (`_pulledToTrackEnd`). This narrows session 3's rule; it adds no behaviour.
    - *After the fix.* The verifier's two probes: 14 of 14 presses stay where they were, 0 follows started (`s6/verifier-strip-probes-after-fix.log`). A selection dragged 80 px below the pane and held 1.5 s, one move to the bottom edge, a double click, the wheel turned while the press is held, the padding beside the application's 8 px scrollbar: 0 follows; the application's own 8 px thumb pulled to the bottom edge: 1 follow, 0 moves told, the pane at its end (`s6/probe-more-1.log`).
    - *Tests.* Unit `pierre-bridge.test.js` "the pane's scrollbar, held and let go" (four cases, one of them the finding). Contract `bridge/view.spec.js` "a press on the pane's edge that holds no scrollbar ..." (a real mouse on a padded pane). E2E `scroll-landing.spec.js` "A press on the pane's edge that holds no scrollbar" (two tests, both modes, the default headless browser, which draws no scrollbar) and `scrollbar-drag.spec.js` "Beside the pane's scrollbar" (both modes, the application's 8 px scrollbar shown). With the fix taken out, 6 E2E tests, 1 contract test and 3 unit cases fail (`s6/fixes/thumb-evidence-mutation-{e2e,contract,unit}.log`); with each of its five pieces taken out alone, the unit file fails (`s6/fixes/thumb-evidence-single-mutations-unit.log`).
    - *Not measured.* A scrollbar with no width of its own held inside the application: in Chromium the style sheet always gives the pane one of 8 px, so that case needs a browser that ignores `::-webkit-scrollbar`, and none was run. Any browser but Chromium. Where a browser tells the page of pointer moves while its scrollbar is held, a release is not followed there: the reader is where the thumb left them (the limitation of section 15, row 13), never sent anywhere.
  - *What is left of the scrollbar.* While the thumb is held the reader is short of the end by what the pane grew (1,891 to 3,784 px measured; main 0 to 159). A slow drag let go with the pointer above the pane's bottom edge and the thumb never at the end of its track stays where the thumb is (3,784 px short; main 0); a second drag reaches the end. Both are the height estimates (rows times line height, wrapping lines not counted); estimates that count them would remove the cause and are work inside the vendor's layout (`computeEstimatedDiffHeights`, the per-line `heightDeltas`), not attempted. Section 15, row 13; accepted for this change by a ruling of the orchestrator on 2026-11-20, made under the owner's delegation of this work; it is not the owner's own decision and is put before the owner at hand-over.
  - *Not followed, on purpose:* a position written by code. No application path writes "the end"; a test that does scrolls again until the pane rests there (`tests/CONVENTIONS.md`).
  - *Callers.* `_handleRootScroll` (the pane's one `scroll` listener) calls `_followEndAfterReaderScroll` before its own-scroll guard; `_handleRootScroll` also calls `_noteScrollbarScroll` first; `_handlePointerUp` (the document's `pointerup` and `pointercancel`) calls `_followEndAfterScrollbarRelease(press, event)` for a press that began on the scrollbar (one caller) and forgets the press; `_handlePointerMove` (the document's `pointermove`) forgets it at any move the page is told of; `_noteReaderScrollInput` asks `_pressOnScrollbar` (one caller) whether a press is a candidate; both followers start `_startEndFollow`. `_noteReaderScrollInput` is bound to `wheel`, `touchmove` and `pointerdown` on the pane (capture) and to `keydown` on the document, and unbound in `destroy`. The gutter hide and hover re-derivation of the same listener (H129) are unchanged: a position the follow writes is a scroll like any other.
  - *A scroll key pressed while the scroll of the key before it is finishing does nothing: Chromium's, on any page; the moment is a few frames longer here after an End key* (session 3 of phase 9; round 2 had "in Chromium, with or without the bridge" from a probe that switched the follow off and never ran on main). On a page with nothing of the application (one tall scrolling box; `evidence/phase-9/s3/plain-key-at-scrollend.log`): a Page Up pressed in the frame in which the End key's smooth scroll arrives at the end moved the box 2 times in 30; pressed as soon as the box's `scrollend` event had been dispatched, 30 times in 30; the event came 20 to 45 ms after that frame. A script that grows the box or writes its position in that frame changes nothing (`plain-end-then-pageup.log`), and a script write followed at once by a key, in the middle of a key's scroll or at rest, loses nothing (`plain-key-after-write.log`, 30 of 30 in each case). Through the applications, the same probe under load (`probe-pageup-during-end-{main,final}.log`): on main 80 of 80 Page Ups moved the pane (the probe's own round trips take longer than the browser's moment), on this branch 72 of 80. The 8 came 2 to 4 ms after a write of the follow, which keeps the browser's scroll alive for the frames in which the pane grows; 100 ms and more after the arrival, 60 of 60 moved (`probe-pageup-delay-*-final.log`: 7 of 20 dropped at no delay). *The viewer's own position writes cost a key no distance:* 45 Page Ups from the end of the pane up into files not yet measured took the reader a full page each, in both modes, 2 of the 45 with a write of the viewer's (`applyScrollFix`) during the key's scroll, and on main likewise without one (`probe-walk-{final,main}.log`). The three failures of round 2's stress stage (a second Page Up that did nothing) fit this and were not caught again (0 of 440 with a recorder in the page). *For tests:* "the position has not changed between two looks" does not say a key's scroll is over; wait until the pane's last `scrollend` is not older than its last `scroll` (`watchPaneScrolls` in `scroll-landing.spec.js`; `tests/CONVENTIONS.md`). *For a reader:* a second key in those few frames is not something a hand does on purpose; it is listed as a difference from main (section 14, row 36).
  - *Tests.* Bridge on the real viewer, `bridge/view.spec.js`: "a position the page writes as "the end" is short of the end ..." (the viewer's behaviour, pinned), "a scroll of the reader's that reaches the end of the pane stays at the end ..." (wheel, End key), "a Page Up pressed after the End key is not undone" (while the pane is still being followed; after the follow has ended), "nothing is followed while the pane's scrollbar is held; let go with the pane at its end, the end is kept ...", "a navigation that starts right after the reader scrolled to the end is not pulled back to the end". E2E, both modes: `scroll-landing.spec.js` "The end of the pane" (wheel, End key, Page Up after End at two moments) and `scrollbar-drag.spec.js` (a real thumb: never back during the drag down; dragged up, it stays up). Unit: the input rules (`pierre-bridge.test.js`, "what counts as the reader scrolling toward the end of the pane", 8 cases). With the follow disabled the two first bridge tests and the four first E2E tests fail (`evidence/phase-9/fixes/h163-mutation-*.log`: 2,095 px and 12,569 px short); with the first form of the follow in place of the second, the Page Up and scrollbar tests fail at every level (`evidence/phase-9/r2/fixes/endfollow-mutation-{unit,contract,e2e}.log`: 6 unit, 3 contract, 6 E2E). *Session 3:* unit, three more cases (the thumb at the end of its track under which the pane grew; the pull to the bottom edge with its negatives; a press whose release never arrived); `bridge/view.spec.js` "a scrollbar taken to the end of its track is put at the end of the pane when it is let go, however much the pane grew under it"; `scrollbar-drag.spec.js`, a real thumb, both modes: "the thumb taken to the bottom of its track in one pull and let go ends at the end of the pane" and "the thumb dragged down while the pane grows under it, the pointer pulled to the pane's bottom edge and let go, ends at the end of the pane". With either release rule taken out its tests fail at every level (`evidence/phase-9/s3/fixes/scrollbar-release-mutation-{no-at-end-memory,no-pull}.log`: 1 unit, 1 contract, 2 E2E each; 1,891 and 2,703 to 2,883 px short).
  - *What is left:* a position written by code is not followed (above); the scrollbar while it is held, and after a slow drag let go above the pane's bottom edge (above); the few frames after an End key in which a scroll key does nothing (above).

**Found in phase 9**

- H164. **Two oracles for "which lines does this file show" must give one answer.** `isLineVisible` counts a record's hunks plus what `_expandedRanges` reports (lines the reader expanded with the viewer's controls, and gaps of 5 lines or fewer that the viewer paints in full). `getCodeFromLines`, which fills a suggestion block, walked the hunks alone. On a context file a comment form opens on any line that is on screen, so the suggestion button gave an empty block on a line of a small gap or an expanded line, and a block with lines missing for a range that ran across one (found by the integration review; reproduced on the real viewer and in both modes). Main read a context file's lines from its table rows and had neither. Fixed: a context record answers through `_contextCodeFromLines`, which uses the same ranges as the oracle and reads the lines by number from the record's full contents, without their line breaks (main's context-file block had no empty line; the block of a changed file still has main's, #638). A changed file is not changed: a line expanded by hand in a changed file gives an empty block as on main, and is on the #638 update. **Superseded by H181 (review fixes of PR #646):** one loop for both kinds (`_contextCodeFromLines` is gone); a changed file's block has no empty lines and holds its lines expanded by hand and its small gaps drawn in full, read from the contents; a range with a line the diff does not show gets no block (a warning) instead of the lines that are shown. Tests: `bridge/context-records.spec.js` "getCodeFromLines answers the text of every line a context file shows ...", `context-files.spec.js` "the suggestion button on a context line ..." (both modes), `pierre-bridge.test.js`.
- H165. **A tolerant path lookup must try the exact path of every kind first.** `FileCommentManager.getZone` tried the exact key for a changed file only and then matched by path suffix, so `vendor/src/utils.js` (a context file) got the zone of `src/utils.js`. `_ensureFileCommentsZone` read "another file's zone" as "no zone yet" and created a new element on every call: the header's button, the entry builder and the annotation renderer each held another element, and a form opened from the header was in one the viewer did not draw. Fixed in `getZone` (exact key of a changed file, exact key of a context file, then the tolerant match). The same shape is worth checking wherever a path resolver falls back to `DiffRenderer.pathsMatch` (`findFileElement`, `_canonicalFilePath`): those resolve to an id of the pane and were not found wrong, but a context file and a changed file whose paths end alike are one suffix apart. Tests: `context-files.spec.js` "a context file whose path ends like a changed file's ..." (both modes), `file-comments-zone-registry.test.js`.
- H166. **A sweep over the document misses what the viewer has unmounted; the registry does not.** `CommentMinimizer.setMinimized(false)` removed `file-comments-expanded` with a document query. A file's comment zone is in the document only while its file is mounted, so a zone expanded and then scrolled away kept the class through minimize off and on, and came back open with its indicator saying closed. Fixed with a second sweep through `prManager.forEachFileCommentsZone`. The line-level twin (`comment-expanded` on cards of unmounted files) corrects itself on the refresh after a remount (`_injectLineIndicator` calls `_applyExpanded`), measured by the existing N11 test. Rule for later changes: state that is cleared for "every file" is cleared in data or through a registry, never by `document.querySelectorAll` on something the viewer mounts. Tests: `offscreen-mutations.spec.js` "minimize switched off and on while a far file had its file comments open ..." (both modes), `comment-minimizer.test.js`.
- H167. **A context file was drawn by another renderer on main, and that renderer said things the shared cards did not.** Main's table renderer built its own comment button, form and card for a context file's lines (`DiffRenderer`, `CommentManager.showCommentForm` and `displayUserComment`; the duplicated markup of H4). Three things existed only there, because a line of a file outside the diff has no position in it and its comment is submitted to GitHub as a file-level comment: the button's title ("Add comment (expanded context - may not submit to GitHub)"), a warning in the form ("Warning: Expanded context line - may not submit to GitHub") and an icon on the card with the title "This expanded context comment will be posted to GitHub as a file-level comment". Phase 8 deleted the row-based code and, with it, these three and their CSS; nothing recorded them (the D2 table of functions on context lines, section 12, does not have them), the golden dump compares cards by position, and the look check does not compare context files by pixel. Found in phase 9 by looking at the live drive's paired screenshots, then by listing every class and every user-facing string that main's scripts wrote and no script writes now (50 classes, 8 strings; these were the ones that are not the table's look). Restored on context files: `PierreAnnotations.renderComment` (collaborator `isContextFile`), `renderForm` (`data.outsideDiff`, set by `PRManager.showCommentForm`), `PierreGutter.createGutterButtons` (`commentTitle`, given by the bridge for a context record), and the two CSS blocks from main. Changed files are as on main: none of the three, also on a hand-expanded line. Tests: `context-files.spec.js` "a comment on a context line: the button, the form and the card say that it may not be posted on its line; in a changed file none of them does" (both modes), `pierre-shared-modules.test.js`. **The rule for any later deletion of a duplicated renderer:** diff what the two copies say (classes and strings), not only what they do.
- H168. **The chat counted suggestion cards in the document.** `ChatPanel._ensureAnalysisContext` takes the latest analysis as context when `document.querySelectorAll('.ai-suggestion[data-suggestion-id]')` finds a card, and shows that number ("N suggestions loaded"). On main every suggestion's card is in the document once the suggestions are displayed. On the virtualized viewer only the cards of mounted files are: with three suggestions in files far from the reader the card said 2, and with none mounted the chat took no analysis context at all. Found in the live drive (main: "2 suggestions loaded", this branch: 1). Fixed: `PRManager.countDisplayedSuggestions` counts the suggestion annotations of every file and the suggestion cards of every comment zone of the registry, and the chat asks it (`_countDisplayedSuggestions`; the document only when the page has no manager). This is the class of H104 and H135 ("a count over the document is a count over the mounted window") in a component the phases did not read for it. A search of the other components for queries into the pane's DOM found no further count. Test: `offscreen-mutations.spec.js` "the chat is told how many suggestions the analysis has, also when their files are far away" (both modes), `file-comments-zone-registry.test.js`.
- H169. **A stylesheet rule that asks `:has()` about what a file's code block holds makes every change inside a file a style pass over the whole file, and every update of one file a style pass over every mounted file.** *(Found, measured and fixed in round 2 of phase 9. The entry's first form said "scrolling inside a long file costs more per frame than on main", took it for the viewer's row measuring (F31) without a profile, and left it for the owner. The phase's verifier rejected that. The cause was the bridge's own stylesheet.)*
  - *The rules.* `PierreStyles` had `pre[data-diff]:has([data-line-annotation]):not(:has([data-line]))` (three rules, one of them for every descendant of the block, `... *`) and `[data-code]:has(...)` for a file that shows a card and no line (binary, mode-only, pure rename, empty: vendor fact F10). Main has no such rule. With them the browser decides the block's style again whenever an element is added to the block or taken out of it, and the descendant rule makes that the style of every row.
  - *Who does that all the time.* (1) The viewer's hover marks a row by moving its gutter button into it, and the bridge re-derives the hover when the pane has come to rest after a scroll (H129). In a file with 10,000 drawn rows each rest was 21 ms of style and a layout of up to 92 ms; a wheel scroll in steps (the measuring script's, and a notched wheel's) rests between steps: 3.2 s of style in 50 steps, 111 long tasks in 420. (2) Every publish ends in one pass of the viewer over all mounted files, which touches each code block: one revealed line restyled 19,867 elements in 46 ms (61 elements without the rules), and a comment form opened in a 31-row file restyled 40,885 elements with a 4,000-row file mounted below it (169 without). That second one is the "reveal of a hidden line takes 40 ms against 10" that the phases had carried since the cut-over.
  - *How it was found.* A CPU profile of the scroll showed no script time worth the name (the time was the browser's own); the browser's counters by stretch of the scroll showed style; a trace with invalidation tracking named `Affected by :has()` on `PRE` and `CODE`; taking the rules out removed it. Scripts and outputs: `evidence/phase-9/r2/{profile,trace}-long-file.mjs`, `reveal-{breakdown,profile,trace}.mjs`.
  - *Fixed.* The condition is a class of the file's host, `pierre-rowless`, set by `PierreStyles.markRowless(host)` from what was just drawn: `_onPostRender` calls it at every draw of every item, which is before the viewer measures the item, and takes it off when the host goes back to the pool. `ROWLESS_FIX` and `FRAME_CSS` key on `:host(.pierre-rowless)`, with the specificity the old selectors had. The contract specs that run the viewer without the bridge install the same call as their post-render hook (`harness/constants.js`: `ROWLESS_SCRIPTS`, `MARK_ROWLESS`).
  - *And when the pane counts as at rest.* Marking a row is still a layout of the file's rows (the browser's, for the button that moved: 7 ms at 5,000 drawn rows, 16 ms at 10,000). With a file that shows 2,000 rows or more mounted, the pane is at rest after eight frames and 120 ms without a move, not after two frames (`_watchScrollRest`, `_hasLongFileMounted`, `COSTLY_HOVER_ROWS`). Not for every file: the buttons are hidden until the rest, and with the longer wait everywhere the hover-then-click gestures of the E2E adapters stopped working (a Playwright click on a control that is not shown yet scrolls it to another place on its retries, which takes the line from under the pointer; the adapters now wait for the button before they click, `tests/CONVENTIONS.md`).
  - *Measured after both* (`perf-long-file.mjs` in the evidence package; main at `3223ecce` in a scratch checkout, same machine, same sitting, medians of 3, PR / Local): *12,000 lines:* frame interval p50 10.1 / 10.2 ms and p95 17.5 / 18.1 ms here, 10.3 / 10.2 and 18.2 / 18.4 on main; long tasks during the scroll 2 / 2 here (the longest 133 / 133 ms), 0 / 0 on main; long tasks from the navigation to the end of the scroll 3 / 3 with 380 / 377 ms in all here, 3 / 4 with 546 / 613 ms on main; the file's rows on screen after 325 / 306 ms here, 1166 / 1074 ms on main; a jump to the middle line 195 / 196 ms here, 50 / 53 ms on main. *4,000 lines:* p50 10.4 / 10.1 and p95 18.5 / 18.3 ms here, 10.3 / 10.2 and 26.1 / 18.1 on main; long tasks during the scroll 0 / 0 and 0 / 0; a jump to the middle line 32 / 32 ms and 30 / 30 ms. *The 60-file corpus* (`perf.mjs`, medians of 5): reveal of a hidden line 7.3 / 4.1 ms here, 8.3 / 12.1 on main; frame p95 17.7 / 16.8 and 16.7 / 18.1; first row 180 / 176 and 312 / 305 ms.
  - *What is left, in files of more than 5,000 rows only.* The viewer draws a block of 5,000 rows in one task when the window moves to it: two tasks of about 0.13 s in a scroll through 12,000 lines, and a jump to a line in a block that is not drawn takes about 0.2 s where main, which has every row in the document from its first render, takes 0.05 s. Main pays for those rows before the file is first shown. That is the price of the row window (F26, F30, F31), not of this defect; it is on the list of limitations (section 15).
  - *The rule for any later stylesheet change:* never `:has()` with a descendant argument on `pre`, `code`, `[data-diff]` or `[data-code]`. A child combinator on the block (`:has(> [data-additions])`) asks about the block's own children, which change at a draw, and is fine.
  - *Tests.* `pierre-shared-modules.test.js`: the rule above, on the stylesheet text; `markRowless`. `gutter-buttons.spec.js` "The cost of a change in one file" (both modes): a trace of one comment form opened, the elements restyled added up, fewer than the mounted rows. `bridge/gutter.spec.js`: "with a long file mounted, the hover is not re-derived between the steps of a scroll, and once when the pane has come to rest", "with no long file mounted, the pane is at rest after two frames". The layout of row-less files is pinned as before (`vendor/layout.spec.js` F10; gates G9, G11, G13; the look check). Each new test fails with its fix taken out: `evidence/phase-9/r2/fixes/h169-mutation-*.log` (the old rules: 4 unit tests, the E2E test in both modes with 40,885 and 40,871 elements; two frames everywhere: the bridge test).
- H170. **For one frame after a jump of the scroll position that brings a file back into the viewer's window, the files below it are drawn above their places.** *(Phase 9, session 5; measured, not changed.)* Found behind a lost click (H171). With the pane at rest at its end and the first file of a three-file review out of the window, a position written in one step 364 px up (a script's `scrollTop`, or `scrollIntoView`) leaves the two drawn files 39 to 45 px above where they belong until the viewer has drawn the first file again, which is in the next frame or the one after; the scroll position and the pane's height do not change meanwhile (`evidence/phase-9/s4/siv-probe-2.log`, `shift-probe.log`, PR and Local alike). A reader's wheel (100 px steps) and Page Up bring the file back before it is needed: 0 frames off in 137 and 116 frames. What can do a jump of that size in one step is a drag of the scrollbar's thumb, a click in its track, and code. Main draws every file at all times and has no such frame. Not fixed: the frame is the viewer's (it places the window it has drawn and draws the newcomer in its next pass), and no gesture of a reader presses a control within one frame of the reader's own jump. Section 14, row 38; section 15, row 15.
- H171. **A Playwright `click()` on a control that is still moving scrolls the pane itself, and then presses at once.** *(Phase 9, session 5; tests.)* `context-files.spec.js` "a context file whose path ends like a changed file's ..." failed once in session 3's proof (Local mode) with no form in the file. With a recorder the same flow failed 10 times in 3,798 runs, PR and Local alike, every time the same way (`evidence/phase-9/s4/zone-fails.txt`, `zone2-fails.txt`): the pane at rest at its end; mousedown on the header's comment button; 10 to 20 ms later the pane 364 px higher and the first file drawn again; mouseup on the pane's background; the click sent to no control. No script of the page had written the position (every write of more than 60 px was recorded with its stack: none). The writer is Playwright. `scrollFileIntoView` starts the application's animated scroll and returns when the file shows; the click then finds the button "not stable" five times, and its sixth attempt, like every attempt after the first, scrolls the control itself before it presses: `scrollIntoView` with block `end` (then `center`, then `start`; `playwright-core/lib/server/dom.js`, `_retryPointerAction`; the attempts are in `zone-probe-pwapi.log`). That is the jump of H170, and a press in its one frame is released after the files have moved. A press held for 150 to 300 ms on the same button with no such scroll before it: 40 of 40 opened the form. *Fix, in the test:* it waits for the pane to be at its end, where the scroll to the last file ends, and then clicks once; the fixed test passed 1,999 of 1,999 times (`combined-x1000.log`; a two-thousandth run was sent to a port another program on the machine holds). `tests/CONVENTIONS.md`: "A control is clicked when the scroll that brought it has ended". The second failure of that proof is the same kind and is main's: `external-comments.spec.js` "clicking the right chevron increases scrollLeft of the segment row" calls `aiPanel.expand()`, narrows the panel at once and clicks a chevron that moves with the panel's edge for the 0.2 s of its width transition. Recorded on main at `3223ecce`: mousedown on the chevron, mouseup 13 ms later on the row beside it, no handler run, nothing scrolled (1 of 999 runs alone; this branch 0 of 999 alone, and 1 of the 267 executions in the logs saved on this branch, whole runs and stress runs together; `chevron-main-x999.log`). A press held for 50 ms right after the narrowing scrolls the row 0 times in 5 on both trees, and after the panel's transitions 5 times in 5 (`chevron-held-{main,final}.log`). The spec also gives its 2 s limit to `waitForFunction` as the function's argument, so the wait ran to the test's 30 s. *Fix, in the test:* it waits for the panel's own transitions before the click (the misplaced limit is left as it is and is on the #638 update). *(H174 fix, 2026-11-25: the same class in the review panel. `aiPanel.expand()` opens the panel with a width transition of 0.2 s and its controls travel about 300 px; a click on the "User" segment button made in the next statement did not reach the button in two whole runs of 15 (`comment-crud-advanced.spec.js:452`, `comment-filter-menu.spec.js:102`, both PR mode). A press begun two frames after `expand()` and held for three: 0 of 20 clicks; begun after the panel's transitions: 20 of 20; the same without the fix. Every spec now opens the panel through `expandReviewPanel` in `tests/e2e/helpers.js`. Section 11, "H174 fix".)* *(H174 fix, session 2, 2026-11-25: the same class in the diff options popover. `DiffOptionsDropdown._show` sets `left` to the gear's centre and the transform to `translateX(-50%) translateY(-4px)`; the popover is built with `translateY(-4px)` alone and a transition of 0.15 s on its transform, so the first time it opens on a page it travels half its width, 160 px, to the left ("Split" is 43 px wide), and from the second opening on it rises 4 px and no more. `setDiffView` clicked the gear and then the option. `scroll-landing.spec.js:118` (Local mode) failed that way once in 40 executions of a repeat stage, and the phase-9 entry `split-view.spec.js:130` is the same state: "Split" focused, not pressed, the pane unified, no error on the console. Measured (`h174/session2/probe-b/`): a press on "Split" begun two frames after the gear's click and held for three frames switched the layout 1 time in 20, and in 2 of the other 19 left the failed run's state exactly (mousedown on "Split", mouseup on the file under the popover's old place, the click sent to `body`); begun once the popover's transitions were over, 20 times in 20. With the two production files at `bed90ca1`: 6 in 20 and 20 in 20. With twenty workers, 40 of 300 first openings had two animation frames in a row with the button still where it starts (`travel-under-load.jsonl`), which is all Playwright asks before it presses. Playwright's own click right after the gear's did not fail in 400 tries with twenty workers (`b2-own-click-20-workers.jsonl`), so the last link, its press landing inside the travel, is inferred from the state it leaves and was not recorded. Every opening of that popover in the suite goes through `openDiffOptions` in `tests/e2e/helpers.js` (the gear, then the popover's transitions): `setDiffView`, `setMinimize`, and four specs that clicked the gear themselves. `PanelGroup` (the layout picker) and `NotificationDropdown` open the same way by their code; that was not measured, and their tests have not failed. The travel is main's and is left as it is. Section 11, "H174 fix".)*
- H172. **One place moves the pane on a collapse, and it is the viewer's side, not the page's.** *(Phase 10.)* Main's `HeaderAnchor.keepHeaderInPlace(header, change)` measures the header's top, runs the change and scrolls `.diff-view` by the shift. That cannot be the mechanism over the virtualized viewer, measured on the merged tree with the bridge's own scroll taken out (`evidence/phase-10/mechanism/results-table.txt`): the helper as main has it scrolls an element that does not scroll here (the scroll container is `#diff-container`), and the collapsed file is not even mounted afterwards (three long files, half way into the first: scroll position 1791 before and after, no element); made to scroll the right element it reads the header's position after the viewer's own redraw, which is no measure of the collapse (shift -123 px where -1774 px is right; in the 60-file review the header ends 176.6 px above the pane). `PierreBridge.setCollapsed` is the mechanism: before the publish it reads where the stuck header is (`_stuckHeaderOffset`: 0 while it is stuck at the pane's top, negative when the file's last rows have pushed it partly above, null when the header is not stuck or the file is not mounted), and it sends the viewer an item target with that offset in the same flush as the publish (`batch`), so the header is at its place when the click handler returns and in every frame after it (without the batch the file had no element in the task after the click and was back within two frames). `header-anchor.js` and its two script tags are not in the merged tree: nothing would call it, and two things that both scroll on a collapse are a defect. **Its callers**, each with what the change does to it: `PRManager.setFileCollapsed` is the only caller of `PierreBridge.setCollapsed`; it is called by `toggleFileCollapse` (the header's chevron through `onToggleCollapse`, `toggleGeneratedFile`, `TourRenderer`'s expand of a collapsed file before a stop and its re-collapse of the files it opened when the tour ends), by `toggleFileViewed` (the header's "Viewed" box: collapse when checked, expand when unchecked), by `revealInDiff` (expand only, before a navigation), and by `toggleContextFileCollapse` and `toggleContextFileViewed` (the same two controls of a context file). All of them collapse through the one function, so all of them keep a stuck header in place, a partly covered one too; none of them collapses inside an open `batch` (the three `batch` calls outside the bridge publish annotations), so the flush is always in the caller's task. `public/js/local.js` patches none of these functions: Local mode runs the same code. Neither side of the merge keeps main's wrapper-reading versions of the four functions (they read a `collapsed` class of an element a file need not have).
- H173. **The viewer moves the pane by a few pixels when the file that expands is at the pane's top edge.** *(Phase 10.)* Main's contract is that an expand is not anchored: the scroll position is the number it was, and a collapsed header that was partly under the toolbar drops to the toolbar's edge. Left to itself the viewer added 8 px to the scroll position on such an expand, in the same flush (`evidence/phase-10/mechanism/debug-frames.jsonl`: 25982 before, 25990 after; main 26435 and 26435), and did the same for a collapsed header that sat a fraction of a pixel above the pane's top. `setCollapsed` notes the scroll position before an expand of a file that is mounted and ends below the pane's top edge (`_reachesIntoPane`) and writes it back when the flush changed it. A file that ends above the pane is left to the viewer, which keeps the reader's place under a file that grows above them, as the browser's scroll anchoring does on main.
- H174. **A file above a collapsed header gets its contents after the collapse and grows; the pane is held at its end through that.** *(Found and measured in phase 10 and left as a limitation by the orchestrator's ruling of 2026-11-20; the owner decided on 2026-11-25 that it is fixed. Fixed in the "H174 fix" part of section 11. The ruling "no further end-of-pane behaviour" is lifted for this defect only: the two other limits, rows 12 and 13 of section 15, are as they were.)*
  - *The defect, measured again on the tree before the fix* (`bed90ca1`; evidence directory `h174/probe/before-fix/`, 8 of 8 runs: PR and Local mode, chevron and "Viewed", pointer taken away and pointer left where the click was; the same numbers in all eight). The 60-file corpus, a wheel scroll to the end, the pane at rest, the last file collapsed from its stuck header. Before the collapse the file above has no element and has not been given its contents. In the frame after the collapse the pane is at its end (0 px short), the collapsed header 502.84 px below the pane's top edge in a pane of 597 px, the file above 936.41 px tall, and its content upgrade is queued (the viewer mounted it in the collapse's own flush). Its contents are applied 32 ms after the collapse with the pointer away and 10.1 s after it with the pointer resting on that file (`PRManager.PIERRE_POINTER_UPGRADE_DELAY_MS`; after a click on a header that was stuck at the pane's top the pointer is over the file that came up under it). The file is then 1,080.75 px tall (144 px more), the viewer has kept the line at the pane's top where it is (H151: the scroll position grew by 52 px, the rows added above that line), the pane is 92 px short of its end and the header at 595.19 px: out of view but for 2 px. With the pointer resting, the upgraded diff is displayed one frame after the page's upgrade queue has gone idle (a highlighted file keeps its old diff on screen until the worker answers, F5).
  - *On main* (`6f06bbb1`, phase 10's measurement and its verifier's): the file above has its element and its contents before the collapse, the header rests at 503 px and the pane at its end.
  - *Two mechanisms, and why one.* (B) "A file that a collapse brings into view has its contents before it is shown": measured by giving the five files above their contents before the collapse (`_ensurePierreContentUpgrade`; `h174/probe/candidate-b/`, 4 of 4): records that are not mounted take them, the pane's scroll position grows by 682 px with the stuck header where it was, and the collapse lands at 502.56 px and stays there with the pane at its end. So it works when the contents are there first. It cannot be made to hold in general: the files a collapse brings into view are whatever fits into a pane above the header, their contents are a request each, and a collapse has to land in the task of the click (H172: the header's place is read in that task and in every frame after it), so a collapse made before the answers are there (a reader who arrives at the end and collapses at once; contents kept back by the test) shows the files from their patches all the same. It would also fetch the contents of files the reader never looked at, which the rule "a file is upgraded when the viewer mounts it" exists to avoid on a large diff. (A) "The pane is held at its end while the files above the collapsed header receive their contents" covers every order of events by construction, needs no request that is not made today, and is the rule the pane already has for a reader at its end (H163). (A) is what is built, alone.
  - *What is built.* `PierreBridge.setCollapsed` reads where the file's header is before it publishes (`_stuckHeaderOffset` as before, or `_headerTop` for a header that is in view and not stuck), and after its flush calls `_holdEndAfterCollapse(record, headerPlace)`. A hold begins only when the collapse is drawn on return (not inside a `batch`, a viewer callback or with a list write waiting), the pane is at its end, and the header is more than 1 px below the place it had: the pane's end kept the collapse from putting the header where it was. A header that is where it was means the pane is long enough, and nothing is held (a collapse in the middle of a review; a short review that never scrolled). While it holds, the bridge looks at the pane once per frame and after every draw of a file (`_queueEndHoldLook`, from `_onPostRender`, in a microtask: when the viewer's pass is over and before the frame is painted). A look writes a position only when the pane's end has moved away since the hold last saw the pane at its end, and then `min(end, position + how far the header is below its place)`: the header is never taken above the place it had, and a pane that has become long enough for that place ends the hold there.
  - *What ends a hold* (each a unit test, and the first three the "reader's own scroll wins" rules of H163, which hold unchanged): any input of the reader's (a wheel step either way, any key, a touch move, a press on the pane, its scrollbar included: `_noteReaderScrollInput`; a scroll toward the end ends it too, and what that scroll does at the end is `_followEndAfterReaderScroll`'s as before); a scroll target or a view restore of the application's (`_scrollTo`, `_restoreView`); the next collapse or expand of any file; a position above the one the hold last saw in a pane that is not at its end (something else scrolled; a pane that got shorter and is still at its end is not that); the header back at its place; the collapsed file's record no longer the pane's; nothing awaited and the pane unchanged for `END_FOLLOW_STILL_FRAMES` (20) frames; `END_HOLD_MAX_MS` (15 s).
  - *What is waited for, named and bounded.* `_awaitsContents()`: the page says that a drawn file is still to be given its contents (hook `contentsAwaited`, answered by `PRManager._isContentUpgradeAwaited`: the catch-up sweep for the files drawn first has not run, a file is queued or its upgrade is in flight, which includes the ten seconds for a resting pointer, or an upgrade a navigation asked for is in flight; false once that render has been replaced), or a drawn file shows an older diff than the one last published (the bridge's own records). The longest wait the application makes on purpose is the ten-second pointer delay; a wait that has no limit of its own (the reader typing in a comment form of that file) is ended by the key that types, and the 15 s end everything else. No timer is slept on: the hold is a frame callback that reads the pane, and it stops scheduling itself when it ends.
  - *Measured after the fix* (`h174/probe/after-fix-1/`, 8 of 8, the same eight runs): header 502.84 px after the collapse and 503.19 px once the file above has its contents (0.35 px: the fraction the pane's end moved by), pane 0 px short of its end in both. *One frame:* with the look of each frame alone, the pane was painted 92 px short for one frame, because the viewer draws a file's new diff and moves the pane for it in a frame callback of its own (`applyScrollFix`, called from the viewer's scheduler), after the hold's callback of that frame (`h174/probe/after-fix-2/phases.log`: the viewer's write in frame 20969.1, the hold's in frame 20985.8). With the look after a draw, both writes are in the same frame, 0.2 to 0.3 ms apart, before it is painted (`after-fix-3/phases.log`, 4 of 4), and `collapse-at-end-contents.spec.js` reads the header's place after every frame drawn on the way and finds it nowhere else.
  - *What is left.* (1) A reader who does something on the pane between the collapse and the arrival (a press, a key, a wheel step) has the pane as the viewer leaves it: the line at its top is kept, and the collapsed header can move down by what the file above grows by. That is the rule asked for (the reader's input wins) and H151's. With the pointer away the window is the 30 to 100 ms of the request; with the pointer resting on the file above it is the ten seconds. (2) The file above still grows on screen, upward now: its rows move up by 144 px in the measured review where on main they were there before. (3) A collapse that is not drawn when `setCollapsed` returns (made inside a `batch` or a viewer callback) starts no hold; no caller does that (H172).
  - *Callers of every function changed, and what the change does to each.*
    - `PierreBridge.setCollapsed`: one caller, `PRManager.setFileCollapsed`, reached from `toggleFileCollapse` (the header's chevron through `onToggleCollapse`, `toggleGeneratedFile`, `TourRenderer`'s expand of a collapsed file before a stop and its re-collapse of the files it opened when the tour ends), `toggleFileViewed` (the header's "Viewed" box), `revealInDiff` (expand only), `toggleContextFileCollapse` and `toggleContextFileViewed` (H172's list, checked again; `public/js/local.js` patches none of them). For all of them: a collapse or an expand that changes the file's state now ends a follow of the pane's end that is running (before, a collapse from a stuck header ended it through `_scrollTo` and the others left it running; a follow started by the reader's scroll is ended by the press that makes the click in any case). A collapse of a mounted file whose header the pane's end moved down begins a hold; an expand, a collapse of a file that is not mounted, and a collapse that leaves the header where it was begin none, and their scroll positions are the numbers they were (the collapse and expand tests of `collapse-landing.spec.js`, `collapse-scroll-anchor.spec.js` and `scroll-landing.spec.js` pass unchanged). The tour's re-collapse at its end can begin a hold when it leaves the pane at its end with that file's header moved down; the next navigation ends it.
    - `PierreBridge._noteReaderScrollInput`: bound to `wheel`, `touchmove` and `pointerdown` on the pane and to `keydown` on the document (H163). One line added: an input toward the end ends a hold. A follow of H163 (`_endFollow` without `hold`) is treated as before in every branch.
    - `PierreBridge._onPostRender`: the viewer's callback, for every draw of every item. One call added, `_queueEndHoldLook`, which returns at once unless a hold is running. Nothing else of the callback changed.
    - `PRManager._createBridge`: one caller, the `PRManager` constructor (the same object serves both modes; `local.js` does not patch it). The bridge is given one more hook.
    - `PRManager._upgradeFilesWithContents`: two callers, `loadAndDisplayFiles` (PR mode) and `LocalManager`'s diff load (`local.js`), which both call it right after `renderDiff`. It now notes the render whose catch-up sweep is pending (`_pierreUpgradeSweepSignal`) and clears the note when the sweep's callback runs; what it queues and when is unchanged for both.
    - `PRManager._abortContentUpgrades`: one caller, `renderDiff`. It clears the note too.
    - New, with one caller each: `PierreBridge._headerTop`, `_holdEndAfterCollapse`, `_queueEndHoldLook`, `_awaitsContents`; `PRManager._isContentUpgradeAwaited` (the hook).
  - *Duplication, flagged.* `_startEndFollow` (H163) and the hold are two loops that keep the pane at its end, with different rules for when to write and when to stop (the follow writes whenever the pane is short of its end and lasts 2 s; the hold writes only after growth, is capped by the header's place and lasts while something is awaited). They share the slot (`_endFollow`), so everything that ends one ends the other, and are not merged: H163's rules were to stay as they are. A later change to how the reader's input ends either must be made with both in view.
  - *Tests.* E2E `tests/e2e/collapse-at-end-contents.spec.js` (18 tests, 9 per mode; every scenario keeps the contents back until the collapse is on screen and waits for the file above to show them): the last changed file by chevron and by "Viewed", with the pointer taken away and with the pointer left where the click was (the real ten seconds); a context file as the last item by both controls; the reader scrolls up after the collapse by the wheel and by Page Up; a collapse that does not leave the pane at its end. `tests/e2e/scrollbar-drag.spec.js` "the thumb taken up after the collapse stays where the reader left it ..." (a real thumb, both modes).
