Unit: `pierre-bridge.test.js` "the pane's end after a collapse that its end cut short (H174)" (15 cases), `codeview-render-diff.test.js` (the page's answer; the hook's wiring). `collapse-landing.spec.js` and `collapse-scroll-anchor.spec.js` pass with their assertions unchanged (one comment of the first, which said that contents arriving above a collapsed header move what is below, now names this entry; and since session 2 its `readFile`, like the one test of the new spec that collapses the thirty-first file, gives the thirty files above their contents before it measures, so that the half pixel a collapse lands within is not left to which of them the navigation drew: section 11, "The three failed executions"; and since 2026-11-27 both specs, like the new one, set a "Viewed" box through `setViewedBox` and not through Playwright's `check()`: section 11, failure D). Each part of the fix taken out fails tests (`h174/mutations/SUMMARY.txt`): without the hold, 11 unit cases and the 12 positive E2E tests; without the look after a draw, 1 unit case and the same 12 E2E tests, on the one frame; the other parts in the "H174 fix" part of section 11.
- H175. **`playwright install` deletes the browsers no installation links to.** *(Phase 10.)* Installing the browser of Playwright 1.65 into the private browsers directory removed the Chromium of 1.59.1 (`chromium-1217`, `chromium_headless_shell-1217`, `ffmpeg-1011`): `pnpm install` had replaced the 1.59.1 package, so nothing linked to them any more (`evidence/phase-10/playwright-install.log`, its first three lines). They were put back by installing `playwright-core@1.59.1` in a scratch directory and running its installer with `PLAYWRIGHT_SKIP_BROWSER_GC=1`. Any later `playwright install` into a directory that holds more than one version's browsers needs that variable.
- H176. **A test helper that repeats a click the browser did not send hides how often that happens.** *(Phase 10; item of the phase-9 verifier.)* `clickOnce` reads from the page whether a click arrived, and `clickDelivered`, `clickExpandControl` and the context-gap helper make the click again when none did (H143). A test passes however many clicks were lost. `clickOnce` now counts each one and reports it: a line `[diff-dom] lost click (n in this worker): ...` in the run's output with the test's title, and a `lost-click` annotation on the test. A self-test in `fixture-isolation.spec.js` loses one click per mode on purpose and checks the count, the annotation and that a click that arrives is not counted. The number per whole run is in the proof's `lost-clicks.txt`; a run's count minus the self-test's two is the number of clicks the browser withheld.
- H177. **With `prefers-reduced-motion`, the pane was drawn one frame behind the viewer: fixed in the review fixes of PR #646 (section 11, "Review fixes").** *(Found on 2026-11-25 in session 2 of the H174 fix; fixed and proved on 2026-11-27.)* `pr.css` answers the setting with a blanket rule, main's since `a28afae5`: `*, *::before, *::after { animation-duration: 0.01ms !important; animation-iteration-count: 1 !important; transition-duration: 0.01ms !important; scroll-behavior: auto !important }`. The initial `transition-property` is `all`, so with the setting on every style change of an element that has no transition of its own became a transition 0.01 ms long, and the first frame of a transition is drawn with the value it starts from (layout reads in that task see it too). The viewer places what it draws with the `height` of its list (the one element child of the scroll container, `#diff-container > div`) and the `top`, `bottom` and `height` of the list's sticky child that holds the drawn files (`#diff-container > div > div`), written in the task that writes the scroll position: in a collapse's task `document.getAnimations()` listed exactly those four, and none without the setting (`stage-b/tree-rm-without-fix-and-drag-first-try.jsonl`). So each time the viewer moved its window, one frame showed the files where the window had been. Measured before the fix in both modes (`h174/session2/probe-rm/`): the collapse of `collapse-landing.spec.js`'s scenario put the header 656.34 px below the toolbar in that task and in the frame drawn next (6 of 6); a wheel of 60 steps of 400 px down the sixty-file review drew 14 or 15 of about 210 frames with a hole in the pane (0 of about 208 without the setting); the same at `bed90ca1`.
  - *Fixed at its cause.* Inside the media block of `pr.css`: `.main-layout .diff-view > #diff-container > div, .main-layout .diff-view > #diff-container > div > div { transition: none !important; }` (specificity (1,2,1) and (1,2,2) over the blanket's (0,0,0); both `!important`, one origin). Those two elements have no transition without the setting, so with it they are now exactly as without it. Nothing else changes: real transitions and animations everywhere else are still cut to 0.01 ms.
  - *What else the blanket rule touches, and why that is unaffected.* (1) Every transition and animation the page's stylesheets declare (157 `transition` declarations in `pr.css` alone; e.g. the review panel's `width, opacity` of 0.2 s): cut to 0.01 ms, which is the rule's purpose; the E2E test reads the panel's computed duration (`1e-05s`). (2) The spinners the block itself exempts. (3) Every element without a transition of its own: a style change keeps its old value for one frame. That shows only where a script writes geometry in step with the scroll position. Measured with the fix and the setting on: a collapse's task lists no animation at all; a wheel of 40 steps down the sixty files lists one property in all, the `visibility` of the gutter buttons' container (`.pierre-gutter-buttons`, which the bridge hides while the pane moves, H129; 16 frame samples), i.e. a button hidden one frame later, on its own row, placing nothing (`stage-b/tree-rm-fixed.jsonl`). The viewer's rows, file headers and separators are in shadow trees, which a document rule's `*` does not reach; the toolbar and the tour bar are sticky by CSS, not by script. Main has the rule and places nothing with those properties: under the setting, 0 animations in a collapse's task, the header within 0.5 px in every frame, 0 frames with a hole of 199 and 200 (`stage-b/on-main.md`).
  - *Tests.* `tests/e2e/reduced-motion.spec.js` (6, 3 per mode, `page.emulateMedia({ reducedMotion: 'reduce' })`): the collapse of the thirty-first of sixty files from 300 px inside it keeps its header within 0.5 px of its place in every frame (without the fix it fails before the click: the place read by the setup is itself mid-transition, `stuck` false); a wheel of 60 × 400 px draws no frame with a hole (`watchPaneHoles` in `diff-dom.js`; without the fix 15 in each mode); the review panel's transition is still 0.01 ms (passes either way: it guards that the exemption stays narrow). Red and green: `stage-b/red/reduced-motion.txt`, `stage-b/green/reduced-motion.txt`. Section 14 row 40, section 15 row 17.
- H178. **Two questions about a line: "drawn now" and "part of the file's diff".** *(Review fixes of PR #646, findings 1 and 3.)* `PierreBridge.isLineVisible` answers "drawn now": false for every line of a collapsed file. `PierreBridge.isLineInDiff` (new, data only) answers "part of the file's diff": the same `_showsLine` test over the record's metadata and its expanded ranges, without the collapse. Whatever belongs on a line (an external thread, a tour stop, the reveal in `ensureLinesVisible`) asks `isLineInDiff`; the bridge hides a collapsed file's annotations and shows them when it is opened, as it always did for user comments and suggestions. Before, a GitHub thread on a changed line of a viewed or generated file became a fallback card in the file's comment zone (the styling of a thread whose line is gone) and stayed there after the file was opened, and every tour stop in a collapsed file was skipped. And the reveal asked again for the contents of a collapsed file whose first request was still on its way: the pane fetches the contents of every file it draws at load, collapsed or not, and the reveal for a line in a hunk of a collapsed file requested them a second time (measured with the contents held: `gen/data.lock` requested twice with the H174 commit's files, once with the fix, both modes). `isLineVisible` callers that rely on false-while-collapsed and stay on it (re-grepped): `whenLineDisplayed`, `scrollToLine` and its flash loop, `_isAnnotationLineShown`, `PRManager._revealLines`, `_contextItemShowsLines`, `ChatPanel._handleFileLinkClick` (ChatPanel.js:5886). `isLineInDiff` callers: `PRManager.ensureLinesVisible` (whose callers are `loadUserComments`, `_renderAdoptedUserComment`, `revealInDiff`, the `review:expand_hunk` handler, `SuggestionManager`'s display, `TourRenderer.prepareStop`, `ExternalCommentManager._renderThreadPierre`; for an expanded file nothing changes, for a collapsed file a line in its hunks is no longer revealed and its contents are not asked for again; a line in a gap is revealed as before), `ExternalCommentManager._isPierreLineInDiff`, `TourRenderer._mountStopPierre`. *Main, measured* (`stage-b/on-main.md`): the thread on a collapsed file is a fallback card in the zone and stays there after the file is opened, the stop in a collapsed changed file is skipped, the reveal requests the contents twice: all older than the migration; a stop in a collapsed context file was mounted on main (its table renderer), so that skip was the migration's. *Tests:* unit `collapsed-file-lines.test.js`, `external-comment-manager.test.js`, `ensure-lines-visible.test.js`; E2E `collapsed-file-annotations.spec.js` (threads, PR mode only: Local mode loads none; the user comment's reveal, both modes).
- H179. **The tour decides a file's collapse from data and writes it through `setFileCollapsed` only.** *(Review fixes of PR #646, finding 3.)* `TourRenderer._isFileCollapsed(path)` asks `PRManager.isFileCollapsed` (falling back to the bridge record's `collapsed`); a collapsed file is opened with `setFileCollapsed(path, false)` before the stop is mounted, and on exit (`unmountAll`) every file the tour opened that is still expanded is collapsed again with `setFileCollapsed(path, true)`. It never reads an element: under virtualization a file the tour opened is usually unmounted by the time the reader leaves the tour (E2E: four stops, the last one 1,450 lines further down; both opened files have no element at exit and are collapsed again). A file the reader closes again during the tour is left alone. Before, the anchor scan asked `isLineVisible` before the expand branch, so the expand branch (which read the wrapper's class) was unreachable, and `unmountAll` re-collapsed only files whose wrapper it found. Callers of `setFileCollapsed` (re-grepped): `toggleFileCollapse` (header chevron and click, `toggleGeneratedFile`), `toggleFileViewed`, `revealInDiff`, `toggleContextFileCollapse`, `toggleContextFileViewed`, `TourRenderer.mountStop` and `unmountAll`; the tour no longer goes through `toggleFileCollapse` (no toggle race with a reader's click between the read and the write). Measured (question (d) of the unit stage): with the collapsed file's header 20 px under the toolbar, a tour stop in it opens the file, the header lands flush (0 px) and the stop card settles 168.7 px below the bar within the tour's own scroll of 40 px, and does not move afterwards (both modes). *Tests:* unit `tour-collapsed-files.test.js`, `tour-renderer.test.js` (repaired: it collapses through `setFileCollapsed`, no longer by stamping a class on a hand-built wrapper); E2E `collapsed-file-annotations.spec.js` (both modes: a generated file and a viewed file, the reader closing one again, a collapsed context file as the only stop).
- H180. **`PRManager._canonicalFilePath` answers from data only.** *(Review fixes of PR #646, finding 5.)* Order: the exact path (changed files, then context files from `_contextItemIds` and `contextFiles`), then normalized equality (`DiffRenderer.normalizeFilePath` on both sides), then a suffix match (`DiffRenderer.pathsMatch`); a changed file before a context file at each step; among several suffix matches the first in pane order. No element lookup: the old order asked `findFileElement` (a suffix match over the mounted wrappers) before the data, so with `packages/x/src/index.js` mounted and `src/index.js` scrolled away, `./src/index.js` named the mounted file, and a context file's alias named nothing once the file was scrolled away. Callers (re-grepped): the bridge's `resolvePath` hook (every `_record` miss), `isFileCollapsed`, `setFileCollapsed`, `_revealLines`, `scrollToFile`, `scrollToContextFile` (and through them the sidebar, the review panel's navigation and ChatPanel's file links). An ambiguous bare suffix (`index.js` with two such files) still resolves to the first in pane order. The function does not exist on main; main had every file's element and `findFileElement('./src/index.js')` answered `src/index.js` (measured). Observed and left alone: a finding whose path is in another form is drawn in no file, here and on main (suggestions are drawn by exact path); its navigation now opens the file it names. *Tests:* unit `canonical-file-path.test.js`, `set-file-collapsed.test.js`; E2E `path-aliases.spec.js` (both modes: a click in the review panel on a finding on `./src/index.js` opens the collapsed `src/index.js` and leaves `packages/x/src/index.js` alone; the same for a collapsed context file scrolled far away). Corpus `sharedSuffixCorpus`.
- H181. **A suggestion block is every line of the range, one line each, or nothing.** *(Review fixes of PR #646, finding 2.)* Vendor fact F32: the lines of a parse keep their line ending (`"one\n"`, `"one\r\n"`). `PierreBridge.getCodeFromLines` takes each line of the range from its hunk, or, when the metadata is not partial and `_expandedRanges` says the line is drawn (lines expanded by hand, a gap of 5 lines or fewer drawn in full), from the full contents by number; strips each line's ending; joins with `"\n"`; and answers null when any line of the range has no text (or for a backwards or non-integer range). Context files go through the same loop; `_contextCodeFromLines` is gone. `CommentManager.getCodeFromLines` passes the null on, and `insertSuggestionBlock` then shows the warning "Cannot insert a suggestion for lines A-B: the diff does not show the text of every line of the range. Expand the hidden lines first." and leaves the textarea and the button as they were. Before: a block of three lines had an empty line after each (main too: measured `"```suggestion\nconst alpha11 = 11 * 2;\n\nconst alpha12 = 12 * 2;\n\n..."`, `stage-b/on-main.md`); a range across lines outside the hunks lost them; a range across hidden lines got the lines of the hunks only, a block that, applied on GitHub, deletes the others. That last one is reachable here and not on main: on this branch a drag in the gutter crosses a hunk separator (15 down to 79 selects 15-79, 65 lines of which 59 are hidden, and the form posts that range), where on main a drag across a separator selects nothing and one upward stops at the separator (`stage-b/on-main.md`, the follow-up question). Callers: the bridge's `getCodeFromLines` <- `CommentManager.getCodeFromLines` <- `CommentManager.insertSuggestionBlock` <- the line form's suggestion button (`onSuggestionInsert`, changed and context files) and `PRManager.insertSuggestionBlock` <- the suggestion button of a user comment's edit form (`editUserComment`). `FileCommentManager.insertSuggestionBlock` (file-level comments) is unrelated and unchanged. Supersedes H164's note "the block of a changed file still has main's [empty line]" and H52. Found and left alone: Save stays disabled after the button fills an empty form until the reader types (the insertion fires no `input` event, and only `input` enables Save; main's form and insertion are the same at source level, `pierre-bridge.js:2177-2239` and `comment-manager.js:372` at `6f06bbb1`, not measured there). *Tests:* contract `vendor/lines.spec.js` (F32) and `bridge/context-records.spec.js` "getCodeFromLines answers the text of every line a context file shows ..." (its last assertion, a selection running past the shown lines, changed from "the shown lines" to null); unit `suggestion-block-lines.test.js`, `pierre-bridge.test.js`, `comment-manager-getcodefromlines.test.js`; E2E `suggestion-block.spec.js` (both modes: three lines of a hunk, saved as is; a range into lines expanded by hand; a drag across a separator and a comment saved over a gap refused; a context file's small drawn gap and its hidden gap) and `comment-forms.spec.js` through `SUGGESTION_BLOCK_HAS_EMPTY_LINE`, flipped to `false`.
- H182. **A comment form's identity is its file, first line, last line and side.** *(Review fixes of PR #646, finding 6.)* The annotation id is `form-<file>-<start>-<end>-<side>`; the bridge caches the form element by id (`PierreAnnotations.renderForm`), and its Save and Cancel act on the data of the first open, so two ranges must never share an id. `showCommentForm` and `editAndAdoptSuggestion` (`edit-suggestion-<id>`) return early when the id is open: `PRManager._focusOpenForm` (asked of `bridge.hasAnnotation`, data) puts the caret in the open form's textarea with `preventScroll`, and adds nothing. Measured (question (d)): with the edit form mounted and outside the pane and its suggestion's Edit button at the pane's bottom, a second Edit (clicked from the page) leaves the scroll position as it was, both modes (Playwright's own click moved it 8 px to bring the button fully into view, which is Playwright's). Before (and on main for changed files, measured): + on line 11, then a range 11-13 and +, gave one form, the 11-11 form, and Save posted 11-11; + twice on a line gave two annotations sharing one element; Edit twice, the same. Main's context files closed an open form first (its table renderer, `CommentManager.showCommentForm` calls `hideCommentForm()`; what was typed was lost; read at source level, not measured); here neither kind closes another form, so a second + on a context file no longer closes the first (section 14, row 45). Callers: `showCommentForm` <- `onCommentClick` (`PRManager._bridgeInteractionOptions`: the gutter's + and a range selection); `editAndAdoptSuggestion` <- the suggestion card's Edit button; `_focusOpenForm` <- those two. `tests/parity/visual.mjs` removed a form by the old id; it uses `form-<f>-<l>-<l>-RIGHT` now and waits for the form to be gone. *Tests:* unit `comment-form-lifecycle.test.js`; E2E `comment-form-identity.spec.js` (both modes).
- H183. **The Save guard of a comment form holds only while the save's promise is pending.** *(Review fixes of PR #646, finding 4.)* `PierreAnnotations.renderForm` disables Save and marks it saving while it awaits the page's `onCommentFormSubmit`; that hook (`PRManager._bridgeInteractionOptions`) must return the save's promise. It was a braced arrow without `return`: the guard awaited `undefined`, and a double click or Cmd/Ctrl+Enter twice posted twice (measured on main, both gestures and both modes: two POSTs, two rows, two cards). The guard's `finally` now resets the button whether or not it is in the document: a form whose file the viewer unmounted is kept by the bridge and comes back with the file, and with the old `isConnected` test a save that failed while the file was scrolled away left Save disabled for good (shown with the return fixed and only the old `finally`: `stage-b/red/comment-form-identity-guard-finally-only.txt`, 2 of 2 fail). The Edit-suggestion form's adopt request is awaited the same way (one adopt for a double click). Main forms are never unmounted, so the stuck Save is the migration's; the double post is older. Callers: `onCommentFormSubmit` <- the Save listener of `renderForm` (Cmd/Ctrl+Enter reaches it through `saveBtn.click()`); `_handleCommentFormSubmit` <- the hook; `_bridgeInteractionOptions` <- the constructor (and `tests/utils/page-bridge.js`). A refused save shows nothing to the reader (the browser logs the status), here and on main. *Tests:* unit `comment-form-lifecycle.test.js`; E2E `comment-form-identity.spec.js` (both modes: a double click, Cmd/Ctrl+Enter twice and an edit form's double click with the request held, counted with a sentinel request; a save refused while the file has no element, then saved).
- H184. **A line selection never holds a line the diff does not show.** *(Follow-up to the review fixes of PR #646: section 14 row 46.)* On the branch a drag in the gutter crossed a hunk separator in both directions and the form posted the hidden lines (15 down to 79 posted 15-79, 59 of them hidden; main: no selection downward, a stop at the first line below the separator upward). Every range the viewer reports is now cut back to the run of shown lines that holds its first line (`range.start`, the gesture's anchor): `_shownRuns` (hunks plus `_expandedRanges`, merged where they touch; `_showsLine` asks it too, so the oracle and the selection are one definition), `_cutToShownRun`, `_cutForList` (against the item the viewer holds, H103 rule 5). The bridge cuts in all four selection callbacks (`onLineSelectionStart` for a shift-click, `onLineSelectionChange` on every row, `onLineSelectionEnd`, `onLineSelected`) and corrects the viewer at once with `setSelectedLines(cut, {notify: false})` (`_cutViewerSelection`): the viewer draws a selection that changed with the pointer in the next animation frame and one it is given at once, so the uncut range is never drawn, and the next row is cut again from the untouched anchor. Also cut: `getLineSelection` (the gutter's + and chat button read it), `selectLines` (from its `start`; the gutter's button drag now passes the line it started on first), the selection `_restoreView` puts back, and the selection the viewer re-applies after `_publish` (`syncSelection`, no callback). An end in the other column (`endSide`) is cut at that column's run of the same index (hidden lines are unchanged lines, the same on both sides); runs that do not pair up (hunks without context lines, which no source of the app produces) keep the start line. Downward this is better than main, upward the same. The suggestion button's refusal (H181) stays for comments saved over a gap before. *Against the diff that is drawn, too (browser stage).* A mounted file goes on drawing its present diff until the new one has been highlighted (F28): after a content upgrade, and after a reveal that loads the contents first. The item then shows lines the screen does not. Cut against the item alone, a drag from 15 to 79 in the window of a reveal of 18-76 was not cut, and the viewer, which maps line numbers to row indexes of the diff it draws (`getLineIndex` -> `getRenderedDiff`), highlighted rows 15, 16, 78 and 79 on both sides of the separator still on screen; in the window of a content upgrade the cut end was 17, a row the patch's diff does not draw, and the + disappeared (the vendor places it on the selection's last row, `placeUtilityFromSelection`, and hides it when that row is not drawn). `_cutForList` now also cuts against `getRenderedDiff()` of a mounted instance when that is not the item's diff, with the expansion map that belongs to the drawn diff (`_drawnExpansionMap`: the map, unless `_retireExpansions` retired it for another diff); `_cutToShownRun`, `_shownRuns` and `_expandedRanges` take that map as an optional last argument whose default is what they used before. Both windows then give 15-16 with the + on 16; the new diff, once drawn, shows both cuts' lines, so the selection stays right. *Where the cut end is named (round 2, after the independent verification of 2026-11-28 rejected round 1).* A cut end is the first or last row of the run, normally an unchanged line, which has a number on each side. Round 1 left it on the side the range ended on, and two defects followed. (1) In unified layout a drag or shift-click let go on a deleted row past a separator (15 to the deleted 81; 8 to 81; the deleted 12 to 81; 14-15 then a shift-click on 81) gave `{start: 15, side: 'additions', end: 17, endSide: 'deletions'}` or `{12, deletions, 17}`: the highlight was right (the viewer finds rows by index, `getIndexesFromSelection`), but unified layout draws an unchanged line once, as an additions row, and the viewer places the gutter's buttons on the selection's bottom row only by that row's own side (`InteractionManager.placeUtilityFromSelection` -> `targetForSelectionPoint` skips a row whose side is not the point's, then `hideUtility()`; with a selection, `placeUtility()` never falls back to the hovered row): the file had no + and no chat button at all while the selection lasted, 10 of 10 in each mode (`drag-2026-11-27/verify/probes/plus.jsonl`). (2) Wherever old and new numbers differ, an end left in the other side's numbers named other lines, because `getLineSelection` answers `start` and `end` under the start's side. Measured in the verifier's probe (`verify/probes/clamp.jsonl`, `src/shift.js`: three lines added after old 11, old 60 changed): unified, new 12 down to the deleted old 60 highlighted R12-R18 and answered `RIGHT 12-15`; split, R64 up to the left column's 10 answered `RIGHT 56-64` (three hidden lines; the form said "Lines 56-64"); unified, the deleted L60 up to R12, and split, L61 to R12, opened "Lines 59-60" and "Lines 59-61" for highlighted L56-L60 and L56-L61. Now `_cutToShownRun` hands a cut end to `_addressCutEnd`, which names the boundary row on the side the range started on (its number there from `PierreBridge._unchangedTwin`, which reads the hunks: context blocks, and the lines between hunks), so that one side's numbers name the whole range; except in unified layout for a range that started on a deleted row and is cut at its bottom, where the row is named on the additions side (`endSide: 'additions'`): what the viewer reports for a release on that row, and the only address the viewer can put the + on. A boundary row that is not an unchanged line keeps the side it was cut on. After (`stage-b/round2/probes/`, the verifier's two probes run on the fixed files): 15, 8 or 14-15 to the deleted 81 give `RIGHT 15-17`, `8-17`, `14-17`, the + on 17, the form "Lines a-17"; the deleted 12 to 81 gives `LEFT 12-17` with the + on the right 17 and that row's one-line form (as a release on 17 gives, and as on main inside one hunk, question 5); new 12 to the deleted old 60 gives `RIGHT 12-18`, the + on 18; L60 up to R12 `LEFT 56-60`; split R64 to L10 `RIGHT 59-64`, L10 to R64 `LEFT 10-15`, L61 to R12 `LEFT 56-61`, and L15 to R79 `LEFT 15-17` with the + on the left 17 and "Lines 15-17" (round 1: the + on the right 17 and a one-line form there). Upward from a deleted row the top end keeps its old number (the bottom row, which carries the +, is the deleted start). Unit: `line-selection-cut.test.js` (28: the unit stage's 20, four of the drawn diff, four of the cut end's address; one test of the unit stage changed, the upward cross-column end in `src/o.js`, which asserted the old-number end), `line-selection-comment-range.test.js` (4); mutations M1-M9 in `drag-2026-11-27/`, M10 (no cut against the drawn diff: 3 fail) and M11 (the drawn diff without its map: 1 fails) in `drag-2026-11-27/stage-b/mutations/`, M12 (no `_addressCutEnd`: 4 fail), M13 (no unified exception: 3 fail) and M14 (the exception in split layout too: 1 fails) in `drag-2026-11-27/stage-b/round2/mutations/`. Browser: `line-selection-cut.spec.js` (22 tests per mode: unified and split, both columns, across columns both ways, from a deleted row to 79 and to the deleted 81, a drag and a shift-click that end on the deleted 81, a file whose old and new numbers differ in both layouts, lines expanded by hand, shift-click, the + button's drag, the whitespace toggle, a context file, the window of a reveal that loads the contents first; each with the highlighted rows recorded at every microtask checkpoint and frame, `watchSelectedRows`), `suggestion-block.spec.js` "a range dragged across a separator stops at it ..."; section 11, "Drag clamp", has the answers to the browser questions and the proof. Callers of every changed function: section 11, "Drag clamp".

## 9. Parity matrix

How parity with main is proven for every item of the regression checklist, in both modes. A cell names the tests that cover the behaviour; phase 9 does not close while a cell lacks a passing test. Specs named `N1`–`N13` are written in phase 2 (N13 in phase 5); "+L" and "+PR" mean an existing spec is parameterized to the other mode in phase 2. Nothing in this matrix is changed by phase 1.

1. **Same spec, two engines.** Specs touch the diff only through `diff-dom.js`, so each scenario runs unchanged on FileDiff (phase 2) and CodeView (phases 5–9). A scenario that cannot exist on FileDiff (eviction) asserts the user-visible outcome of "scroll far away and back".
2. **The matrix below.** A cell may not rest on a stubbed unit test alone. Phase 9 does not close while a cell lacks a passing test.
3. **Golden comparison.** Pre-upgrade dumps must be identical between engines. Post-upgrade dumps must have identical changed rows; context rows may differ only where D5 applies (main's re-diff adds context next to a hunk), and each such file is listed. Context files are compared in the dump's context stage (added in phase 5): order in the pane, ranges, line texts, hidden-line counts and card positions must be identical, except for F25 (one line fewer for a file ending in a newline) and the small-gap rule (a hidden gap of 5 lines or fewer is drawn), each listed per file; how a context file is drawn (number columns, separator markup and label, token colours) is D2 and is not compared. Anything else is a regression.
4. **Known non-parities are recorded, not hidden:** D1–D8, Q2, the pre-existing Local gaps (`renamed`/`binary` never set by `local.js` [OLD§c7]), the Local base-branch change not re-anchoring on main.

Codes: plain name = existing spec; `+L` = parameterized to Local in phase 2; `+PR` = parameterized to PR; N*n* = new spec; `G` = golden; `C1`/`C2` = contract suite L1/L2; `U` = unit test (supporting only); `M` = live drive; `X(Dn)` = intentional change. "—" = the feature does not exist in that mode.

## 11. Phase log

### Phase 0: dependency bump to 1.6.4, alone (`de5d96e6`)

`package.json` pins `@pierre/diffs` to exactly `1.6.4`; lockfile has `@pierre/diffs@1.6.4` and `@pierre/highlights@0.4.0`; Shiki stays at 4.5.0. Unit 11316 / 11316. E2E 491 passed, 2 skipped, 1 failed (`gutter-buttons.spec.js:90`, the known flake), identical to the baseline on untouched main.

### Phase 1: test infrastructure, vendor contract suite, remaining gates

What was added:

- **Vendor bundle build.** `scripts/pierre-diffs-entry.mjs` exports `CodeView` and `hydratePartialDiff` in addition to the existing names. `scripts/build-pierre-diffs.mjs` stamps every bundle with `/*! @pierre/diffs@<version> <mode> */` and has a `--dev` flavour (`NODE_ENV=development`, unminified) written to `tests/.cache/pierre-diffs.dev.js` (gitignored). The development build is the only one in which the vendor's height validator exists.
- **L0.** `tests/unit/vendor-bundle-fresh.test.js` (the served bundle's stamp equals the installed version; the served worker is byte-identical to the installed one), `tests/unit/pierre-diffs-entry-exports.test.js` (the export list is pinned; every name production code and the contract suite read is exported), `tests/unit/e2e-console-guard.test.js`.
- **L1.** `playwright.contract.config.js`, `pnpm run test:contract`, `tests/contract/harness/` (a page and helpers ported from the probe harness; requests to a virtual origin are fulfilled from disk, so no port is bound; any undeclared `console.error` fails the test), `tests/contract/fixtures/workload.js` (a deterministic pure-JavaScript generator of a 59-file `git diff`-shaped patch with full old and new contents; checked once against real `git apply`: the 58 non-binary sections apply and reproduce the new contents exactly), 13 spec files under `tests/contract/vendor/` and 5 under `tests/contract/gates/`: 107 tests.
- **E2E infrastructure.** `tests/e2e/fixtures.js`: fallback to a kernel-assigned port on `EADDRINUSE`, awaited `server.close`, the console guard attached to every browser context, the `PR_ENGINE` init script, also on the context (inert until phase 5; an unknown value is an error), and the removal of the animation-disabling init script, which never worked (deviation below, hazard H41). `tests/e2e/console-guard.js`: the guard and its allow-list. `tests/e2e/test-server.js`: the database is closed when `listen` fails. `tests/e2e/helpers.js`: `expectResponse`, `expectRequest`, `cleanupComments`, `cleanupAllComments`.
- **`tests/CONVENTIONS.md`**: a Playwright E2E section and a vendor contract suite section, including the rule that a unit test may assert what we send to the vendor and never what the vendor does.

Results:

- `pnpm test`: 336 files, 11340 tests, all passing (11316 before, plus 24 new).
- `pnpm run test:contract`: 107 passed.
- `pnpm run test:e2e`, console guard on. Three runs on the final tree (after the animation init script was deleted): 491 passed / 2 skipped / 1 failed with `gutter-buttons:90` as the one failure, which is exactly phase 0 and the baseline, then 490 / 2 / 2 twice. Three runs before that deletion: 491 / 2 / 1, then 490 / 2 / 2 twice. Every failed spec passes when re-run alone, and the failure classes are ones the phase-0 tree shows as well; no run had a console-guard failure of its own (each run saw about 4,850 console errors, all allow-listed, apart from the one 500 that belongs to an already failing test). Details below.

Deviations from the plan in this phase, with the proof:

- **Spec files were touched.** The plan lists only the infrastructure files. The 41 `page.waitForResponse` / `page.waitForRequest` calls in 14 spec files, every one of them of the register-then-await shape, were converted to `expectResponse` / `expectRequest`. Phase 0's first run lost a worker to exactly this (`comment-crud.spec.js:553`, "worker process exited unexpectedly"), and helpers nobody calls would not have removed the cause. The change is mechanical and does not alter what any test asserts.
- **The fixture's animation-disabling init script was deleted; E2E runs with transitions on, as it always has.** Main's `fixtures.js` registered an init script that appended a `transition-duration: 0s !important` style to `document.head || document.documentElement`. An init script runs before the parser has created `<html>`, so both are null on every navigation, not only on `about:blank`: the script threw `TypeError: Cannot read properties of null (reading 'appendChild')` once per document and styled nothing, unseen until the guard existed. Proof: (1) the script instrumented to report what it sees, over one full run: 714 documents (490 `/pr`, 114 `/local`, 69 `/`, 17 `/settings`, 24 `about:blank`), every one with `readyState: "loading"` and no element to append to, 0 styled; (2) a probe on the phase-0 tree and on this one: 0 injected style elements and a computed `transition-duration` of `3s` for an element with `transition: opacity 3s`, in PR mode, after a reload and in Local mode. Deleting the script therefore changes no test's behaviour, and that is why it was deleted instead of repaired: the phase's bar is "E2E equal to phase 0", every baseline number was measured with transitions on, and production runs with them on. A working injection (a `MutationObserver` waiting for `<html>`; the probe then shows the style present and `0s`) was run twice over the full suite as an experiment and not adopted: 488 / 2 / 4 and 491 / 2 / 1, 1.7 minutes instead of 1.9 to 2.0. It broke nothing outright (no `transitionend` listener exists in `public/js`; the four `animationend` listeners still fire at zero duration), but the first run's failures included `edited-posted-comments:95`, which failed in no other run of this phase and was not investigated. Hazard H41 records what later phases must assume; question Q4 hands the choice to the user.
  - *Correction of the first report.* The first version of this phase kept the script with an early return when there was nothing to append to, and recorded that the error came from the initial `about:blank` document and that "real pages are styled as before". Both statements were wrong, and the early return turned a loud no-op into a silent one. Independent verification caught it; the paragraph above replaces it.
- **The `PR_ENGINE` init script is registered on the context, not the page** (the plan says `page.addInitScript`). Hazard H42 has the reason and the measurement.
- **The Playwright section of `tests/CONVENTIONS.md` is adapted, not verbatim.** The old branch's text refers to `whenAnnotationsSlotted`, which is not ported.
- **Corrections 1 to 9 in section 6**, each of which changes a later phase.
- **The workload is 59 files, not about 60**, and is generated in JavaScript instead of by git (as the plan requires). Tests refer to files by role, never by path.

Console guard: what main logs. One full run in record mode (`E2E_CONSOLE_GUARD=record`) collected 5,563 errors in 494 tests:

| Count | Message | Disposition |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 3,878 | Vendor parser reports on the shared fixture diff (`parsePatchContent: hunk line count mismatch`, `processFile: invalid rawLine`, `parseLineType: Invalid firstChar`) and on the generated diff in `lazy-diff.spec.js` | Allow-listed by exact message; hazard H36 |
| 720 | `WebSocket connection to 'ws://localhost:<port>/ws' failed` | Allow-listed: the E2E server mounts no WebSocket endpoint |
| 714 | `TypeError: Cannot read properties of null (reading 'appendChild')` from the fixture's animation-disabling init script, once per document loaded (not once per test) | Script deleted: it had never styled a document (hazard H41) |
| 206 | `... TypeError: Failed to fetch` (comments, context files, file contents, viewed state, files, recent reviews) | Allow-listed: an in-flight fetch aborted by a navigation or by the page closing |
| 20 | `POST /api/local/2/refresh` 500 and its frontend log line | Allow-listed: the Local fixture review points at a worktree that does not exist |
| 23 | 404s and their frontend log lines in tests that provoke them (non-existent PR, mock analysis ids, missing stored config, a failed update) | Allow-listed, each by URL |
| 2 | `DiffHunksRenderer.processDiffResult: deletionLine and additionLine are null, something is wrong {file: q.sql}` | Allow-listed; hazard H37 |

With that script gone and the allow-list in place the guard reported nothing in any later full run, except twice on a test that was already failing for its own reason (`split-view.spec.js:746`: its `cleanupAISuggestions` call gets a 500 from `/api/reviews/1/ai-suggestions` and throws; the guard then reports the same 500).

*Correction (phase 2).* That 500 was not load. It was deterministic and depended on test order: the E2E-only `DELETE /api/reviews/:id/ai-suggestions` route deleted `source='ai'` rows while a user comment adopted from one of them still pointed at it through `comments.parent_id` (a foreign key with no `ON DELETE` action; the user-comment DELETE route only soft-deletes, so the row stays). Once any earlier test in the worker had adopted a suggestion on the review, the route answered `FOREIGN KEY constraint failed` for the rest of that worker's life. The spec passed alone only because nothing had adopted anything yet. Fixed in phase 2 (section 11).

Runs 1 to 5 were made with the animation init script still present (throwing in the survey run, returning early afterwards). The census proves the script styled no document in either form, so those runs and the round-2 runs on the final tree measure the same page behaviour.

The failure set changes from run to run, which is the definition of flake used here, and every failed spec was re-run alone:

- `comment-crud`, `comments-review`, `external-comments`, `split-view`: pass alone (`--workers=1`), `comment-crud` three times, and its multi-line test 12 of 12 with `--repeat-each=12`. Their failures under load are the fallback gutter intercepting the click (30 s timeouts) and, for `comment-crud:439`, a selection set through `fileState.instance` being lost when the idle content upgrade re-renders the file.
- `inline-feedback-context` does **not** always pass alone, and that was checked against the phase-0 tree instead of being assumed. With `--workers=1 --repeat-each=10` (80 executions per run, machine load 2 to 4): the phase-0 tree (`git archive HEAD`, its own vendor bundle) failed 0, 3, 2, 1 and 2 of 80; this tree failed 6, 2, 1, 1, 1 and 0 of 80 (the last four with the phase-0 bundle or with the guard switched off, to bisect). That is 8 of 400 against 11 of 480, about 2% each. The failing test is `:123` "suggestions spanning a visible hunk and hidden endpoint render and navigate", which holds worker messages back and asserts a card stays hidden; `:81` fails the same way under load (5 of 144 on the phase-0 tree, 2 of 144 on this one, 8 workers). It is a flake that main already has and that the list of known flakes did not contain. Phase 2 rewrites this spec's vendor access behind helpers and should remove the race.
- Under an 8-worker stress of `inline-feedback-context` plus `comment-crud` (140 executions per run) the phase-0 tree failed 2 and 2, this tree 4 and 1.
- Round 2, final tree: `comment-crud` (20 of 20), `split-view` (20 of 20), `comments-review` (16 of 16) and `gutter-buttons` (6 of 6, then 60 of 60 with `--repeat-each=10`) pass alone. `comment-crud:41`, `comment-crud:76` and `comments-review:42` are the fallback gutter intercepting the click again; `gutter-buttons:98` timed out in its setup (`setDiffView` waiting 5 s for the split layout) at a load average of 13, in the spec that already holds the known flake `:90`.
- `comment-crud:553` in run 5 is the test whose bare `waitForResponse` crashed a whole worker in phase 0's first run. With `expectResponse` the same timeout under load now fails that one test and nothing else.

Open items carried forward:

- *Q1 (decision D2) and Q2 were open when this phase ended; both were answered by the user on 2026-11-14 (section 3).*
- **For the user: Q4, transitions in E2E.** The suite runs with CSS transitions and animations on (hazard H41); the fixture's attempt to switch them off never worked and was removed. Later phases write their specs for transitions on. Switching them off for real is possible (measured: nothing breaks outright, about 10% faster) but re-times all 494 tests and is not part of this migration unless asked for.
- **The contract suite is not run by CI.** `.github/workflows/test.yml` runs `pnpm test` and `pnpm run test:e2e`. Adding `pnpm run test:contract` to the E2E job (it needs the same Chromium install, about one minute) is what makes "the contract suite gates every bump" true for a bump made outside this migration. Not changed here because the workflow file is outside this phase's scope.
- **The shared fixture diff is malformed** (hazard H36). Phase 2 decides whether to fix it, and must check `hydratePartialDiff` against it either way.
- **`inline-feedback-context.spec.js` is flaky on main at about 2% per execution**, alone and at one worker. Phase 2 moves its vendor access behind helpers; the race should be removed there, and until then a failure of `:81` or `:123` in a full run is re-run alone before it is read as a regression.
- **Corrections 1, 2, 4, 5 and 9 change the bridge's design** (two item metrics, no vertical padding on the root, the row-less CSS, the displayed-diff signal, the line-anchored restore). Phase 4 implements them as written in section 6; the plan document's sections 3.2, 3.4, 3.7, 3.10 and its fact table are superseded on those points.

### Phase 2: characterization on the current engine

Phase 2 took three sessions. The first is described directly below, then the second and the third. Each session's "results" paragraph is that session's; the numbers that hold for the finished phase are at the end of session 3. Tests only: nothing under `public/` or `src/` was touched in any of them.

What was added or changed (tests only; nothing under `public/` or `src/`):

- **Test-infrastructure root causes.**
  - `tests/e2e/test-server.js`: the E2E-only `DELETE /api/reviews/:id/ai-suggestions` route detaches adopted children (`parent_id`) and deletes in one transaction. It can no longer answer 500 (see the correction in the phase 1 log).
  - `tests/e2e/helpers.js`: `cleanupAISuggestions` and `cleanupAllComments` check every response (they took `fetch(...).catch()` for error handling; `fetch` resolves on a 500). The six per-spec copies of these helpers were removed.
  - `tests/e2e/console-guard.js`: six entries removed (four for the malformed fixture, one for a generated diff in `lazy-diff.spec.js`, the `processDiffResult` entry), and the unanchored `/TypeError: Failed to fetch/` replaced by one entry that names each loader a navigation can interrupt and applies to `console.error` only. An uncaught "Failed to fetch", a failed fetch of the bundle or worker, and any loader nobody has looked at now fail the test. Console errors seen in a full run: about 1,100 (WebSocket handshakes and interrupted loaders), down from about 4,850; none unexpected in three runs.
  - What the tightened guard caught, fixed at the source: `comment-crud.spec.js` "should load existing comments" navigated away while its own save was in flight (it waited for any comment row, not its own); `diff-header-lookalike-stats.spec.js` answered `''` as every file's contents; `lazy-diff.spec.js` generated a diff whose header declared a line it did not have. One loader was added to the named list after it showed up once: Local mode's load-time refresh (`Error refreshing diff`).
  - `tests/e2e/fixtures.js`: Chromium-unsafe ports are skipped (H44).
- **The shared fixture and the corpora** (`tests/e2e/fixtures/diffs.js`): `defaultFixture()` (well-formed, H36), `evictionDiff()`, `manyFilesDiff()` (60 files), `edgeFilesDiff()` (added, binary, CRLF, deleted, empty added, generated, long line, mode-only, no newline at end of file, quoted path, rename with change, pure rename, non-ASCII path), `customCorpus()` for verbatim git output, `routeDiff(page, corpus)` for both modes (with and without `?w=1`), and the contents registry the test server answers from. The corpora are built with the contract suite's patch writers (`createPatchBuilder`, extracted from `tests/contract/fixtures/workload.js`; `build()` output is byte-identical before and after, by SHA-256). Responses are shaped with the server's own helpers (`buildChangedFileEntries`, `mergeChangedFilesWithDiff`, the hunk-hash helpers).
- **`tests/e2e/diff-dom.js`**, the only E2E file that knows vendor DOM, and `tests/unit/e2e-vendor-dom-boundary.test.js`, which fails when any other file under `tests/e2e` names a vendor element, attribute, the bridge or the bundle global (comments included). Every such reference was moved: `helpers.js`, `pr-page`, `lazy-diff`, `split-view`, `comment-minimize`, `comment-crud`, `gutter-buttons`, `hunk-summaries`, `quoted-git-paths`, `inline-feedback-context`.
  - `gutter-buttons.spec.js` asserted an implementation detail of the engine being replaced (`data-fallback-positioned`). It now asserts what a user sees: after the pointer leaves, a scroll, or a layout toggle, no gutter button is showing anywhere except on the row under the pointer.
  - `comment-crud.spec.js` set a selection through the vendor instance. It now makes one with a real pointer drag (`selectLineRange`).
  - `lazy-diff.spec.js` asserted the engine's bookkeeping for a collapsed file (a body element with no host, no bridge record). It now asserts that a collapsed file shows no rows until expanded, and that a deferred file shows the placeholder and no rows until loaded.
  - `inline-feedback-context.spec.js`: the vendor-instance calls are behind `expandHunkBy`, `hunkContaining`, `redisplayBaseDiff`, `holdWorkerResults`. Its flaky test (about 2% alone on main, phase 1 log) asserted that a card stays hidden for as long as worker results are held back. That is not something the application promises: in 13 of 119 executions at four workers the card was displayed without any held result being delivered. The test now states the invariant it was written for, navigation does not report completion while the card it navigates to has no box, with a per-frame watcher, and holds the worker to open the window when there is one. 80 of 80 alone at one worker, 119 of 119 at four (the 120th was H44).
- **Both modes.** Newly running in Local mode as well as PR mode: the `pr-page` diff display and hunk expansion tests, `lazy-diff` (collapsed-on-load through Local mode's localStorage viewed state; the deferred placeholder; an overlay materializing a deferred diff), every `split-view` test except AI suggestions (full-width cards, prose width, one-sided files, the paint probe). `diff-header-lookalike-stats` now also runs in PR mode.
- **N5, `tests/e2e/context-files.spec.js`**: 23 scenarios in each mode. Rendering (header, badge, lines, gaps), default ranges and the end-of-file shift, position among the changed files and sidebar order, stored ranges on load, two ranges in one wrapper with a separator, an overlapping request extending the stored range, a covered request adding nothing, gap expansion by step and in full, a comment on a context line (saved against file and line, placed under the line, back after a reload), a comment arriving with a pushed range (the auto-context path), a file-level comment, the gutter chat control and the header chat button, collapse without touching changed files, viewed (collapses, persists under its own `context:` key, survives a reload), dismiss (every range, pane and sidebar), a range removed elsewhere, "the diff wins", sidebar click landing, no movement of the file being read when context files are added above it, and an AI suggestion on a context line. Nothing in the spec names how a context file is drawn; `expectedPaneOrder` in `diff-dom.js` holds the one expectation decision D2 may change (`CONTEXT_FILE_PLACEMENT`, `'interleaved'` today).
- **Golden dump** (`tests/parity/dump.mjs`, `compare.mjs`): 32 dumps (4 corpora x PR and Local x unified and split x light and dark), each with a pre-upgrade stage (file-contents requests held) and a post-upgrade stage, plus two landings (sidebar jump, collapse while scrolled inside a file). 0 files unsettled, 0 console errors. A second dump of three corpora compares equal to the first under `compare.mjs` (24 dumps, "no regression"), so the dump is deterministic.

Results at the end of session 1:

- `pnpm test`: 337 files, 11344 tests, all passing (11340 after phase 1; the boundary test and the reworked console-guard tests are the difference).
- `pnpm run test:contract`: 107 passed.
- `pnpm run test:e2e`, three consecutive runs, load average 18 to 21 by the end (shared machine): 559 passed / 2 skipped / 1 failed each. Failures: `gutter-buttons.spec.js:83` "clear when the pointer leaves the diff (PR mode)" in runs 1 and 3, `comments-review.spec.js:62` in run 2. The first is the baseline's known failure (it was `gutter-buttons.spec.js:90` before the rewrite moved it: same test, same symptom, a button left visible after the pointer went away; main's own run has it); the second is the fallback gutter intercepting a click, the class the phase 1 log describes. Alone: `gutter-buttons` 60 of 60 (`--repeat-each=10`), `comments-review` 16 of 16. Both are in the fallback positioner that phase 8 deletes. The suite went from 494 tests to 562.
- `grep -rn "diffs-container\|pierre-diff-body\|shadowRoot" tests/e2e/*.spec.js`: nothing.

Deviations from the plan in this phase, with the proof:

- **The eviction corpus's big file is 4,000 lines, not 20,100.** The application defers a patch over 20,000 lines behind "Load diff" (`PRManager.PIERRE_AUTO_RENDER_MAX_PATCH_LINES`), and decision D4 keeps that. A deferred file is a placeholder a few lines high; it pushes nothing out of reach. 4,000 lines is about 70,000 px of rows and above the per-file highlight cap.
- **Corpus contents are served from a registry in the test server, and by `routeDiff` for a hand-written diff**, not only "a per-path registry"; and the helper answers `file-content` (context files) from the same registry. The default fixture's `src/utils.js` special case in the two context-content mocks became unreachable and was removed.
- **`selectLineRange` drags down the line-number column, not from the gutter button, and repeats the gesture when the rows were replaced under it** (H45, H43). The assertion that must hold is the selection the application reports after the pointer is released.
- **`waitForDiffToRender` and the other moved helpers are re-exported from `helpers.js`**, so the 41 specs that import them were not touched.
- **The context-files API is mocked in N5** (H46).
- **The sidebar label of a binary file differs by mode** and is characterized as it is (H47).
- **`tests/CONVENTIONS.md`**: the two bullets describing the malformed fixture and the any-path file-contents mock were replaced by the rules that now hold (the vendor-DOM boundary, a served diff comes with its contents, upgraded is not displayed, strict cleanup).

#### Session 2

What was added or changed (tests only; nothing under `public/` or `src/`):

- **Both modes.** `comment-crud`, `comment-crud-advanced`, `file-comments`, `file-comments-edit-delete`, `comment-minimize`, `ai-analysis-actions`, `ai-analysis-sidebar`, `chat-lines` and the AI-suggestion test of `split-view` now run in PR mode and Local mode (`MODES` in `tests/e2e/helpers.js`). For that the test server mocks Local mode's analyses endpoint with the same rows as the PR mock (`recordMockAnalysis`), and `seedAISuggestions` takes the review to seed.
- **New specs, each in both modes**: N1 `diff-render` (order, every row checked against the file contents the patch was generated from, separators, wrap, binary, mode-only, empty added, pure rename, added, deleted, generated, CRLF, no newline at end of file, non-ASCII, quoted path, the per-file plain-text cap, the empty diff), N2 `diff-theme-highlight` (tokens, theme switch and reload, worker script blocked), N3 `file-controls` (collapse, Viewed and its persistence, sidebar click and active entry, no scroll-spy, header chat button), N4 `scroll-landing` (sidebar jump far, twice in a row, in split layout, to a collapsed file; positions stable between scroll passes; AI-panel click on a far finding and on a finding in a collapsed file; the layout toggle; collapsing the file being read), N6 `comment-forms` (Cancel, Escape, two open forms keeping their text across re-renders and a layout toggle, the suggestion block, a dragged range in split layout, a dragged range on the deletion side), N7 `edit-and-adopt` (save, cancel, the unreachable "show original" toggle), N8 `chat-file-links` (visible line, hidden line, range, file only, a file outside the diff becoming a context file, a second range of it, a far file after one read-through, the same in split layout, a file never rendered), N9 `rerender-overlays` (whitespace on and off, two toggles at once, and in Local mode a refresh and a scope change: every file and every card there once afterwards), N10 `text-selection` (double-click and drag on code, drag on a comment, copy, no line selection), N11 `offscreen-mutations` (below), N12 `expansion` (no control before the upgrade, expansion by step and to the end, a stretched card in split layout after an expansion, the gutter button under a parked pointer across a re-render, the upgrade not landing under a parked pointer, a draft surviving the upgrade).
- **N11, changes while the file is several viewports away** (eviction corpus): comments saved, edited, deleted and restored while away and what the cards show on return; Clear All; counts from data while away; a file-level comment and the header icon; adopt, dismiss and restore from the review panel; a panel click on a far comment; the navigator keys across files 70,000 px apart; a comment on a hidden line saved while away; deleting and then revealing lines in the same file; the minimizer's indicator on return; file-level comments on a deleted and on an added file.
- **Helpers.** `tests/e2e/diff-dom.js`: `waitForFileSettled`, `waitForDiffSettled`, `openLineCommentForm`, `headerLanding` (distance below the pinned toolbar, the same on either layout of the scroll container), `firstVisibleRow`, `rowBox`, `selectedText`, `flashedLines`, `syntaxTokens`, `wrapInfo`, `clickExpandControl`, `parkPointerOnLine`, `scrollThroughDiff`, and the six named expectations of hazard H51. `tests/e2e/helpers.js`: `seedSuggestions` and `seedComment` (real rows on any file and line), `reloadComments`, `enableChat`, `mockViewedStateApi`, `mockContextFilesApi`, `setMinimize`, `cleanupContextFiles`. `tests/e2e/fixtures/diffs.js`: `changedLines`, `checkRows`. `tests/e2e/test-server.js`: `POST /test/seed-suggestions`. `tests/e2e/fixtures.js`: a per-test `expectedConsoleErrors` option, used once (the vendor's report of a worker that could not start).
- **Golden dump**: a third stage, `annotated` (a line comment, a file-level comment and a suggestion on one file, with their slots), and a third landing (the suggestion's card after a click in the review panel: 52 to 53 px below the toolbar in every dump). 32 dumps stored, 0 unsettled files, 0 console errors. Two dumps of the default and edge corpora compare equal (16 dumps, "no regression"), as do two of edge and eviction; see H55 for what that took.
- **Performance baseline** (`tests/parity/perf.mjs`, medians of 5 runs, 1440 x 900, load average 7 to 10):

  | Measure | PR mode | Local mode |
  | --- | --- | --- |
  | Navigation to first code row, 60-file corpus | 321 ms | 319 ms |
  | Diff hosts in the DOM at rest / after scrolling through | 3 / 60 | 3 / 60 |
  | Wheel scroll through 52,800 px: long tasks | 0 | 0 |
  | Frame interval p50 / p95 / max | 10.0 / 17.7 / 27.3 ms | 9.7 / 16.7 / 27.3 ms |

Three consecutive runs at the end of the session, load average 13 to 17: 813 passed / 2 skipped / 1 failed (`gutter-buttons.spec.js:83`), 808 / 2 / 6, 813 / 2 / 1 (`chat-lines.spec.js:179`, Local mode). The second run's six: `gutter-buttons:83`, `chat-tabs:85`, `comments-review:267`, `comment-forms:50` (PR mode), and `inline-feedback-context:110` in both modes. All five specs pass alone (`--workers=1 --repeat-each=3`, 138 of 138). `comment-forms:50` and `chat-lines:179` were then fixed at their cause (below) and pass at eight workers (150 of 150 with three other specs). Known flakes after this session, with what is known about each:

- `gutter-buttons.spec.js:83` "clear when the pointer leaves the diff (PR mode)": the baseline's failure; a button left visible by the fallback positioner that phase 8 deletes. In 3 of the last 6 full runs.
- `inline-feedback-context.spec.js:110` "suggestions spanning a visible hunk and hidden endpoint": in 2 of the last 6 full runs (once in Local mode, once in both modes), never alone (24 of 24 today, 80 of 80 in session 1). The failing step is the precondition after `redisplayBaseDiff`: line 150 is still reported visible ten seconds later, so something re-applies the revealed ranges after the test has put the base diff back. Not understood yet; it started with the suite growing from 562 to 816 tests, which is load, not proof. *(Resolved in session 3: hazard H59.)*
- `chat-tabs.spec.js:85` and `comments-review.spec.js:267`: neither touches the diff; once each; pass alone. `chat-tabs:85` was already on the list of known flakes.
- The fallback gutter taking a click meant for the hovered row (H54): once per full run or less after `waitForDiffSettled`, and only in tests that click the button themselves; `openLineCommentForm` makes the gesture again.

Deviations from the plan in session 2, with the proof:

- **Six behaviours are asserted through named expectations in `diff-dom.js`** (H51), four of them defects or limits of main that the plan did not know: the layout toggle keeps the pixel offset and not the line; an upgrade under an open form takes the focus; a deleted comment is drawn again by the next re-render; a chat link to a never-rendered file does not arrive and a revealed line's flash lasts frames. The plan's N4, N8, N11 and N12 wording assumed the opposite in each case. They are characterized as they are and not fixed (application code is outside this phase).
- **Test infrastructure was changed at three more roots**: the Local staleness check is mocked like the PR one (H57); comments on files outside the stored diff no longer leave context files behind (H53); a pointer gesture waits for the diff to settle, and a gutter click that another file's stale button took is made again (H54). None of these loosens an assertion; `gutter-buttons.spec.js` still asserts the stale-button defect directly and still fails on it at the baseline's rate.
- **Three existing assertions that read a value once now poll for the settled value** (`split-view.spec.js`, two card-width checks; `pr-page.spec.js`, the expansion click now waits for the displayed upgrade and uses `clickExpandControl`). Same expected values.
- **`rowCount` counts rows that have a box** (H56).
- **N8 produces its link with the chat panel's own linkifier** from text placed in the message list, because E2E has no chat provider; the click runs the panel's real handler.
- **N9's scope change calls `localManager._handleScopeChange`**, the function the scope selector calls, with the scope endpoint answered by the test; the selector's own UI is not driven.
- **N11's "while away" changes go through the functions the cards' buttons call** (`prManager.deleteUserComment`, `restoreUserComment`), the comments API followed by the reload every change triggers, the review panel's quick actions, the navigator keys and Clear All. A card that is several viewports away cannot be clicked.
- **The golden's sidebar-jump target is the middle file and its landings wait for 40 identical frames** (H55).
- **`ai-analysis-actions` and `ai-analysis-sidebar` clean both fixture reviews after each test.** They seeded suggestions and adopted some without ever cleaning up.

Left after session 2 (all of it done in session 3): the N9 scenarios for the Local base-branch change, external threads and hunk summaries; the N11 scenarios for the review dialog, a save during a reload, the posted filter, publication, summaries, tour stops, external threads and the review history; a context file created by a tour stop; the cause of the `inline-feedback-context.spec.js:110` flake; the matrix cells naming those; three full runs on one tree.

#### Session 3

What was added or changed (tests only):

- **The `inline-feedback-context.spec.js:110` flake: cause found and removed** (H59). The file under test was rebuilt for the worker pool after the test had put its base diff back, and the rebuild applied the revealed range again. `diff-dom.js` got `engineSettled` and every settled-state wait requires it. A full run before the fix with twelve traced copies of the scenario did not hit the race (825 passed, 2 skipped, 1 failed: `gutter-buttons:83`), so the proof is the deterministic one: the worker script held back (`ifc-flake-proof.txt`).
- **N9 completed** (`rerender-overlays.spec.js`, 13 tests):
  - PR mode: a refresh through the toolbar button puts every card back (the Local refresh test's twin; the refresh endpoint is answered with the pull request as the page has it).
  - Local mode: the base-branch change. The selector exists for a branch-scope review of a stacked branch, so the review's metadata is answered as one. Choosing `feature-a` fetches `?base=feature-a` once and re-renders both files; the cards are not put back (`BASE_CHANGE_KEEPS_CARDS`, H51) and return with the next reload; choosing the review's own base drops the override.
  - PR mode, external threads: hiding and showing whitespace puts each thread back on its line once, without a second sync; a pull-request refresh syncs once more and does the same. The order is pinned in each step (thread list answered after the rebuilt diff has settled and with no thread on it), because left to chance main can leave a thread at file level (H60).
  - Both modes, hunk summaries: every summary is on its hunk once with whitespace hidden and again when shown; and a `?w=1` answer with one hash too few for a file leaves that file without any summary while the other file keeps its own (fails closed), until the hashes line up again.
- **N11 completed** (`offscreen-mutations.spec.js`, 38 tests). Added, with the target file about 70,000 px away unless the gesture needs it on screen:
  - Both modes: a comment saved through its form while a comment reload is in flight, the stale answer delivered after the save: both comments once, counted as two, still so after leaving and returning.
  - PR mode: the review dialog refuses "Request changes" with nothing to submit and accepts it with one comment on the far file ("1 comment will be submitted with this review", from data); the review is submitted through the test server's GitHub stand-in and the card says "Posted comment" on return. Hiding and showing posted comments from the comment filter while away is what the far file shows on return, with the pending comment untouched. A posted comment edited while away shows the edit and "Edited since posted" when its card is reached from the review history.
  - Local mode: the preview opened from far away lists the comment and its file; "Show dismissed" while away lists the dismissed comment in the panel and never draws it on the diff.
  - Both modes, hunk summaries: a summary loaded for a file that has never rendered is on its hunk when the file is reached; one pushed while the file is away is there on return; leaving and returning adds none.
  - Both modes, tour: three stops across files 70,000 px apart, each shown on its line and in the viewport, forward with the button and back with the keyboard, no "could not show" toast; leaving the tour removes every stop, also in the file that is away.
  - PR mode, external threads: a thread on a never-rendered far file is on its line when the file is reached; a refresh while away adds the new upstream thread and duplicates nothing; a click on the far thread in the review panel brings its row into view.
- **N5** (`context-files.spec.js`, 25 scenarios per mode): a tour stop outside the diff adds its file as a context file with the stop's range, where a context file goes in the pane and the sidebar, the stop drawn under its last line and in view, and leaving the tour removes stop, file and stored row; a context file that was there before the tour is used by the stop without any write and stays when the tour ends.
- **Helpers** (`tests/e2e/helpers.js`): `mockAppConfig` (the server's own `/api/config` answer with chosen keys replaced), `mockHunkSummaries`, `pushHunkSummaries`, `mockTour`, `mockExternalComments` (with `holdLists`), `externalThread`. `tests/e2e/fixtures/diffs.js` exports `hunkHashes`. `diff-dom.js`: `engineSettled`, `BASE_CHANGE_KEEPS_CARDS`.

Measured in session 3 and recorded as hazards: H59 (the worker-pool rebuild), H60 (an external thread demoted to file level; its trigger was first recorded wrongly and is corrected in the fix round), H61 (what the review panel's counts count).

Also measured, and as the plan expects: on main a summary, a tour stop and an external thread each reach a file that has never rendered and land on their line (the application renders the lazy body first); the comment-load generation and retry guard holds against a stale answer; the review dialog, the preview and the review history read data, not cards.

Context files, for decision D2 (still the user's): every way a context file comes to exist on this branch is characterized in both modes: a chat link (N8 and N5), a tour stop (N5), a comment saved on a file outside the diff (N5, through the server's own row and the push), rows stored for the review on load, and a range added or removed elsewhere and pushed. Unchanged-file findings (#602) are not on this branch (H50, re-checked: the only callers of `ensureContextFile` are `ChatPanel` and `TourRenderer`, the only server creator is `ensureContextFileForComment`); what is characterized of it is the display side, a suggestion on a context line. Position and order are asserted through `expectedPaneOrder` only.

"One tree" is checked, not assumed: a SHA-1 over `git diff -- tests` plus the checksums of the untracked test files was taken before and after every run. Run 1's is `4689ba55…` at both ends; `tests/CONVENTIONS.md` (a document) was extended during run 2, and with its earlier text put back the same `4689ba55…` comes out again; the checksum without that document is `e9f42664…` before and after run 3. - **The golden dump was taken again, and it replaces session 2's.** Two things were wrong with the stored one. `dump.mjs` no longer ran: the cleanup helpers gained a `page.request` call with a relative URL at the end of session 2 (H53) and the script's browser context had no base URL; fixed by giving it one. And the stored Local-mode dumps of the eviction corpus carried an artefact of the very leak H53 describes: the "collapse while 300 px inside the file" landing was -300 px (the scroll offset kept) because a context file left on the Local review by an earlier combination of the same dump made the pane longer; on a clean review it is 394 px (unified) and 412 px (split), the same as PR mode, which is what H48 says a short diff does. Rows, order, headers and annotations were not affected (the dump lists the corpus's files only). A third fault showed in the first re-dump: the post-upgrade stage waited for "upgraded, or nothing queued or in flight", which is also true of a file the background queue has not started on, and one file of one dump was recorded as it was before its upgrade (25 rows where the next dump had 31). The stage now asks for each file's upgrade and waits until it is displayed. Final state: 32 dumps in `golden/`, 0 unsettled files, 0 console errors; a second full dump of all four corpora compares equal with nothing listed (`golden-determinism.txt`: "compared 32 dump(s)", "no regression"). Session 2's dumps are kept beside them as `scratch/golden-s2-superseded` and must not be used as a reference.

Deviations from the plan in session 3, with the proof:

- **`BASE_CHANGE_KEEPS_CARDS` is a seventh named expectation** (H51). The plan asks for the base-branch change as a characterization; it is one, stated through a constant because the new engine's data-first annotations will most likely change the outcome without anyone touching that path.
- **The external-thread scenario of N9 pins the order of two asynchronous answers** (`holdLists`, then `waitForDiffSettled`). Without the pin the scenario fails on main about one time in six for the reason in H60. *(Corrected in the fix round: this paragraph went on to say that the defect needs a second re-render and that no deterministic form of it had been found. Both were wrong. One page load is enough, the deterministic form is now a test, and the N11 scenario, which was left unpinned on the strength of that sentence, is pinned too. See H60 and the fix round below.)*
- **Every "settled" wait in `diff-dom.js` now also waits for the worker pool** (H59). No assertion changed.
- **N11's PR-mode posting scenarios submit real reviews through the test server's GitHub stand-in** and leave posted comments, review records and history entries in the worker's database, dismissed by the cleanup like any comment. `edited-posted-comments` and `comment-publication` already do the same and read counts relative to what is there.
- **Matrix rows 95 and 96 have no Local cell.** The plan's matrix names N11 for Local mode there; Local mode has no posted comments and no review history, and `edited-posted-comments.spec.js` asserts both absences. Row 57's Local cell is the "Show dismissed" flip, the only comment filter Local mode has.

#### Fix round after verification round 1

An independent verification of the tree session 3 left rejected the phase with four blocking findings (`verify/BLOCKING-round1.md` in the phase-2 evidence). A first fix session edited the specs for all four and probed H60 (`round2/`) and ended without recording anything; a second one reviewed that work, finished it and wrote this section (`round3/`). Tests only, as before: nothing under `public/` or `src/`.

The four findings, each with its cause and what was done:

1. **`pr-page.spec.js:239` failed in two of the verifier's three full runs.** Cause: the test asserted the wrong element and passed only while Playwright's first poll beat the scroll (H65). It now asserts the clicked file's landing. 12 of 12 at eight workers with the other fixed tests (`round3/stress-fixed-tests.log`).
2. **`offscreen-mutations.spec.js` "the navigator keys walk suggestions in files far apart" clicked a point of a 78,000 px container** (H64). The keyboard focus is now taken with a click on the toolbar. 24 of 24 (both modes) in the same stress run.
3. **The N11 external-thread scenario left a known race to chance, and H60 described its trigger wrongly.** H60 is rewritten with the single-load measurement, the two conditions and the vendor mechanism. The N11 scenario holds the thread list until the diff has settled, at the first load and at the panel's refresh. The window itself is now a test: `rerender-overlays.spec.js` "a thread list answered while a file's upgraded diff awaits display" holds file contents and worker results to keep it open and asserts the outcome through an eighth named expectation, `THREAD_ADDED_WHILE_DIFF_AWAITS_DISPLAY` (H51). 12 of 12 each in the stress run. `tests/CONVENTIONS.md` says that one page load is already a race of independent answers.
4. **The "one-sided files" describe of `split-view.spec.js` had lost its `afterEach`.** The hook is back (`cleanupAllComments`, both modes); the verifier's own probe, run again, finds both fixture reviews with two files and no context file after the describe (`round3/probe-splitview-leak-fixed.log`). The cause behind the finding is that isolation rested on every describe remembering a hook; a census found many that never had one, and the fixture now resets the two fixture reviews after every test (H62, H63). `tests/e2e/fixture-isolation.spec.js` (4 tests) proves it.

Found while doing that:

- **A second flake with the cause of H59**: `inline-feedback-context.spec.js:77`, the one failure of the first full run of this round. Proven with the worker script held and removed in `expandHunkBy` (H59, addendum).
- **`chat-tabs.spec.js:85` "persists open tabs and focus across a page reload"** (on the known-flake list since the planning survey; the one failure of this round's third full run: expected 2 tabs, received 1). The chat panel writes its tab list 100 ms after the last change. When a step of the test takes longer than that, an entry holding only the seeded session is written first; the test polled for "an entry of the right shape" and then read the tab count once. With 300 ms put between two steps the one-tab entry is what the store holds at the moment every tab has a session id, 4 of 4, and never without the pause, 4 of 4 (`round3/probe-chat-tabs85.log`). The poll now asks for both session ids. 40 of 40 for the spec at four workers. Not a diff spec; fixed because the cause was in plain sight and the acceptance bar is about the whole suite.
- **The baseline's "known flake" is explained** (H66): `gutter-buttons.spec.js:83` was a clean-review test meeting an analysis an earlier spec had left. It stopped failing with the reset; the defect it used to trip over is now asserted by a test of its own.

The verifier's non-blocking items:

- *A spec read the bridge record's `baseMetadata` through `inPage`, unseen by the boundary test.* `contentsApplied`, `framesWithoutUpgrade` and `firstFileBelowToolbar` are helpers of `diff-dom.js` now, and `tests/unit/e2e-vendor-dom-boundary.test.js` forbids `inPage`, `waitInPage` and the record's fields (`baseMetadata`, `fileDiff`, `getRenderedDiff`) in every other E2E source. No spec calls the page-side adapter any more. The two scripts under `tests/parity` still do; they are not specs.
- *The boundary test did not scan `tests/e2e/fixtures/`.* It walks the directory tree now and asserts that it found `fixtures/diffs.js`.
- *`smart-truncate` was not parameterized (plan step 3) and the ledger did not say so.* It runs in both modes now (10 tests, 6 before); matrix rows 10 and 22.
- *Helper names differ from the plan's list.* Recorded here: the plan's `shadowRows`, `lineRow`, `annotationSlot` and `expandControl` are `rows`, `lineNumberCell`, `annotationGroups` (with `annotationCell`) and `clickExpandControl` (with `expandControls`) in `diff-dom.js`. The plan's names describe the current engine's DOM (a shadow root, a slot); the helpers are named for what a spec asks.
- *`openLineCommentForm` and `clickExpandControl` retry their gesture, so the stale-button class of H54 no longer shows in the specs that use them.* Unchanged, and deliberate: `gutter-buttons.spec.js` is the spec that asserts that defect, and it does so without a retry.
- *`hunk-summaries.spec.js` had dropped a term of a wait.* `readFirstHunkHash` waits for the rendered file again (`waitForFileRendered`), then for its anchors.
- *The hand-off file's header and the ledger's results were stale.* Rewritten; the results below are the ones that hold.

Deviations from the plan in the fix round, with the proof:

- **The E2E fixture resets the two fixture reviews after every test** (H62). The plan lists `cleanupComments` / `cleanupAllComments` as helpers for specs to call. A describe that forgets is then a leak into whichever spec the worker runs next, and the census found thirteen describes leaving user comments, five leaving context files and fourteen leaving AI rows. The reset changes no assertion; what it changes is what a test can find at its start, which is now the seeded review. The first full run with the reset in place: 845 passed, 2 skipped, 1 failed (`inline-feedback-context:77`, above), so no test of the suite depended on rows an earlier test left (`round3/e2e-explore1.log`).
- **The test server wraps one application function, `ensureContextFileForComment`, to know when it has finished** (H63). It observes; the call and its result are passed through.
- **Two more named expectations (nine in all)**: `GUTTER_BUTTON_STAYS_WITH_ANALYSIS_SUGGESTIONS` (H66) and `THREAD_ADDED_WHILE_DIFF_AWAITS_DISPLAY`, the latter with a test that asserts a defect of main by holding its window open. The plan's N9 row asks for "external re-anchor after whitespace (PR)"; that scenario is there and pinned, and this one is added because a characterization that only ever takes the lucky order says nothing about the other.
- **`external-comments.spec.js` is not pinned.** It is main's spec and loads its threads on a plain page load, so it stays exposed to H60 (one failure in the phase 1 runs that fits). Pinning it would hide the unpinned scenario phase 3 is asked to make reliable; it is on the known-flake list below with that reason.
