The product loses neither the click nor the form: a press held 150 to 300 ms on the button, 40 of 40. The test waits for the pane to be at its end; 1,999 of 1,999 after |
| The right chevron of the review panel's segment row did not scroll it | The test's flow with a recorder, 999 runs alone on each tree; a press held 50 ms before and after the panel's transition on each tree; a click on a row 0 px wide (scrolls: that guess was wrong) | Main's test, main's code (`AIPanel.scrollSegmentRow` and its CSS are unchanged on this branch): it presses a chevron in a panel that is still opening. On the #638 update with the misplaced `waitForFunction` limit |

**The rulings.** Session 3 put two limits before the owner. On 2026-11-20 the orchestrator ruled on both, under the owner's delegation of this work. They are rulings of the orchestrator, recorded as such; the owner has not decided either and is shown both at hand-over.

1. *Files of more than 5,000 rows* (two tasks of about 0.13 s of the vendor's during a scroll through a 12,000-line file; a far jump of about 0.2 s against main's 0.05 s): accepted for this change. In the follow-up draft with its options: the upgrade to `@pierre/diffs` 1.7 (section 13), a smaller `ROWS_PER_ITEM_WINDOW`.
2. *The scrollbar* (while the thumb is held at the end the pane is short of its end until the release; a slow drag let go with the pointer above the pane's bottom edge stays where the thumb is): accepted as a limitation of a viewer that estimates heights before it measures them. Among the differences a user can see (section 14, row 37) and in the follow-up draft.

The ruling also closed the subject for this phase: no further end-of-pane or scrollbar behaviour was added in session 5, and none of the bridge's follow code was changed.

**The proof of session 5** is the one whose numbers are under "The proof" in "session 3" above (`final-sequence-s5.sh`, 2026-11-20, 07:46 to 09:06, fingerprint identical before and after, `git status` identical: 50 modified files and one new).

Known flakes at the end of session 5: the list after round 2, less the second Page Up (session 3) and the two of this session, which are explained and whose tests no longer race. A stress run of more than about 1,400 repeats is itself a hazard on this machine: the test server of worker *n* listens on port 4000 + *n*, and 5432 and 6000 are not free to it (`ERR_EMPTY_RESPONSE` from another program's port, `ERR_UNSAFE_PORT` from Chromium).

*Shared functions changed, and their callers.* `_handlePointerMove`: the document's `pointermove` listener, one caller; the new line forgets a press that the old line kept while a button was down, and no other reader of `_scrollbarPress` exists outside `_handlePointerUp` and `_noteScrollbarScroll`. `_noteReaderScrollInput`: the four input listeners; only its `pointerdown` branch changed (which presses are remembered). `_followEndAfterScrollbarRelease` and `_pulledToTrackEnd`: one caller each. `clickGapControl`: `expandContextGap` and `expandContextGapFully` (six call sites in `context-files.spec.js`, and the live-drive scripts). `clickExpandControl`: `expansion.spec.js` (three call sites), `pr-page.spec.js`, `scroll-landing.spec.js`. For all of them the normal path is one evaluation before the click and one after; a click that arrived ends the helper as before.

**Deviations added by session 6.**

16. *The release rule of deviation 8 is narrower.* A scrollbar let go is put at the end of the pane only when the press began on the scrollbar's own width (16 px where it has none), the page was told of no pointer move during it, and the pane moved under it. Session 3 followed any press on the pane itself that began within 16 px of its right edge. The "no pointer move" part is measured in Chromium only; in a browser that tells the page of pointer moves while its scrollbar is held, a release is not followed (the limitation of section 15, row 13), and nothing is sent anywhere.
17. *The E2E helpers for expand controls repeat a click the browser never sent* (`clickOnce`, `clickDelivered`; H143). Whether a click arrived is read from the page (a trusted `click` event), not from Playwright, which reports a lost click as made; a click that arrived is never repeated. A test of main's (`pr-page.spec.js:141`) was changed to use it: one call, nothing asserted differently.
18. *A fix of application code was made after the orchestrator's ruling that closed the scrollbar subject.* The ruling excludes further end-of-pane or scrollbar behaviour; this removes a movement and adds none.

Known flakes at the end of session 6: the list after session 5, with two entries added and explained, both H143 under a draw that follows "settled": `context-files.spec.js` "expanding the gap below and the gap above reveals 20 more lines each" (the verifier's development-bundle run; 1 of 400 with its recorder) and `pr-page.spec.js` "should expand context and show correct line numbers with offset" (this session's first proof run; 1 of 43 saved whole-suite logs, 0 of 1,800 alone; on main at `3223ecce` 1 of 1,200 alone, recorded). #### Phase 9, verification round 3 (the independent verifier's note, 2026-11-20)

The verifier ran the proof again on the tree of session 6 (`evidence/phase-9/verify/r3/`, fingerprint identical before the runs, after them and after its probes, and equal to the implementer's): unit `11458 passed`; contract `270 passed`; E2E `948 passed`, `12 skipped`, 0 failed, three runs in a row; development bundle `958 passed`, `2 skipped`, 0 validator or orphan-host lines; golden comparison `no regression` against the saved reference and against main's own dump (both reports byte-identical to the implementer's); look check exit 0 against main's capture and against the saved reference (480 pairs, 0 differ; 1128 number differences, 0 not accepted); performance on the 60-file corpus first row 187 / 177 ms (main 312 / 305), frame p95 16.7 / 17.4 ms (main 16.7 / 18.1), reveal 4.2 / 4.0 ms (main 8.3 / 12.1), and in the 12,000-line file frame p95 17.7 / 18.2 ms with the two tasks of about 0.13 s; pack check OK; stress of the known-flaky specs twenty times over `2480 passed`; live drives 161 steps, 0 failed. No E2E title of phase 8 is gone (926 then, 960 now).

Session 6's fix was probed in both modes (`verify/r3/probe-*.log`). Where the scrollbar has no width: a press on the right padding dragged to the bottom edge in steps, taken below it in one move, held while the wheel turns, and dragged into the code as a selection held 80 px below the pane: 0 follows, the reader stays. On the application's own 8 px scrollbar: the thumb pulled to the bottom edge is 4,570 px short while held (0 follows, 0 backward frames) and at the end after the release (1 follow), and a wheel step up after that holds; the thumb let go mid-track, a click on the track, and a press 1 px beside the scrollbar: 0 follows. End followed by Home or Page Up: the reader stays where the second key put them. With the bridge of before the fix, 3 unit, 6 E2E and 1 contract test fail (`verify/r3/mutation/`).

One more known flake, found by the verifier's stress stage (`scroll-landing`, `scrollbar-drag`, `context-files` and `pr-page` twenty times over at eight workers: 2,719 passed, 1 failed): `scroll-landing.spec.js` "an AI-panel click on a finding that names its file in another form expands the collapsed file completely" (Local mode; a phase-3 test this phase did not change) expected 31 rows and got 45. The test takes its row count after `waitForFileRendered`, which does not wait for the file's contents: the middle file of the 60-file corpus shows 31 rows from its patch and 45 once its contents have arrived (measured in both modes, `verify/r3/probe-rows.log`), and under load the count was taken before they arrived. The spec alone: 32 of 32; the test forty times over in both modes: 80 of 80. Not a defect of the application. The test should wait for the file to be settled (`waitForFileSettled`) before it counts.

### Phase 10: merge of main v5.4.3, and its collapse-header contract

Started from `be28ba89` (phase 9), tree clean, with the local branch `fable-code-view-phase-9` at `be28ba89`. `git merge --no-commit --no-ff main` with main at `6f06bbb1` (`evidence/phase-10/merge.log`). The phase commits nothing: the merge is in progress at hand-over (`MERGE_HEAD` is `6f06bbb1`), and the verifier's commit concludes it with two parents. Added to the plan by the orchestrator on 2026-11-20.

**What was merged.** The 13 commits of main after the branch point `3223ecce`: `06ee81ab` (Playwright 1.65), `2c0d6dd2`, `e5745a9c`, `c717af70`, `9aaada4d`, `989143db`, `44a79c48`, `523b7a96` (provider models and prompts), `c897603f` (dashboard autofocus), `69d1e416` (analysis progress after the modal closes), `760fe822` (keep a collapsed file's header where it was stuck) and the two release commits (v5.4.2, v5.4.3). Git merged everything but two files: `public/js/pr.js` (4 hunks) and `tests/unit/context-entry-collapse.test.js` (2 hunks), both against `760fe822` (the files as git left them: `evidence/phase-10/conflicts/`). `package.json` keeps phase 9's removal of `highlight.js` and takes main's version (5.4.3) and `@playwright/test` `^1.65.0`; `pnpm install --frozen-lockfile` accepted the merged `pnpm-lock.yaml` as it is (`evidence/phase-10/pnpm-install.log`).

**Dependencies and the browser.** `@pierre/diffs` is exactly 1.6.4 and the rebuilt vendor bundle has the hashes phase 9 recorded (`c4514612bee47dead8eb6636fdfd9cb232f9de62`, `06584b4d67a204a3a9e7efd9cdcfc05f1c7729d5`). `@playwright/test` is 1.65.0, whose browser is Chrome Headless Shell 158.0.8127.18 (build v1254); phase 9 ran on 147.0.7727.15 (v1217). The install removed the old browser and it was put back (H175); both are in the private browsers directory.

**How the conflict was resolved: main is the reference, the viewer is the mechanism.** The branch's side of all six hunks stays, because main's side reads the `collapsed` class of a file's element and a file need not have an element here. What main's commit says the application does is taken over whole: a file collapsed from its stuck header keeps the header at the viewport position it had, with the next file directly below it; a header that was not stuck does not move; an expand is not anchored. `PierreBridge.setCollapsed` was changed until the merged tree does that in every case measured (H172, H173); `public/js/utils/header-anchor.js` and its script tags in `pr.html` and `local.html` are not in the merged tree, because nothing would call the helper and a second thing that scrolls on a collapse is a defect (the measurement is in H172).

**The mechanism, decided by measurement** (`evidence/phase-10/mechanism/results-table.txt`, the same in both modes; "header" is its top in px from the pane's top edge, before -> after):

| Scenario | The bridge as phase 9 had it (item target, no offset) | Main's helper around the bridge call, as main has it | The same helper, scrolling the real scroll container | The bridge with the header's measured offset (what is built) |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Three long files, half way into the first, chevron | 0 -> 0, next file 24 px below | the file has no element afterwards; nothing scrolled (1791 -> 1791) | the file has no element afterwards; scrolled by the wrong amount (shift -123, 1791 -> 1668) | 0 -> 0, next file 24 px below |
| Sixty files, 300 px inside the thirty-first | 0 -> 0.39 | no element afterwards | 0 -> -176.61 (above the pane) | 0 -> 0.39 |
| The file ends 30 px below the pane's top: the header is pushed 22.87 px above it | -22.87 -> 0 (moved; main keeps such a header where it was: the test of it passes on main, whose collapsed header reads -22.75) | -22.87 -> -23 | -22.87 -> -23 | -22.87 -> -23 |
| Header 200 px below the pane's top (not stuck) | 200.13 -> 200.13, scroll position unchanged | the same | the same | the same |
| 300 px inside the last file | the pane ends at its new end, header 503 px down | the same | the same | the same |

The publish and the target are one flush (`batch`): with two, the file had no element in the task after the click and was back at its place within two frames; with one it is at its place when the click handler returns (`results-batched-table.txt`), which is what main's synchronous helper gives. Measured in both trees by one probe (`evidence/phase-10/mechanism/zz-facts.spec.js`, `facts.jsonl`; PR mode quoted, Local mode in the file). The margin under a file's element is `0px` on the merged tree, where the viewer keeps 24 px between two files, and `24px` on main. With the reader 300 px inside the thirty-first of sixty files, 2 files have an element on the merged tree (numbers 31, 32 of the pane) and 60 on main. At the pane's end, merged tree: before the collapse the file above has no element and has not been given its contents; right after the collapse it is 936 px tall and the pane is 0 px from its end; 14 s later it is 1081 px tall and the pane is 92 px from its end (the height first changed 114 ms after the collapse). Main: before the collapse the file above has an element and has been given its contents; right after the collapse it is 1081 px tall and the pane is 0 px from its end; 14 s later it is 1081 px tall and the pane is 0 px from its end (the height never changed).

**Main's spec, `tests/e2e/collapse-scroll-anchor.spec.js`.** It passes in both modes, 10 of 10. Its assertions and tolerances are main's; the diff against main's copy is `evidence/phase-10/collapse-scroll-anchor.against-main.diff`. What changed is where it reads: the scroll container (`.diff-view` does not scroll here) through `scrollPaneToEntry` and `scrollState`; "the next file" by position instead of `nextElementSibling` (`gapBelowFile`: the viewer does not keep its files in pane order in the document); the expected space under a file from `spacingBelowFile`, which adds the viewer's gap to the element's margin (the margin is 0 here and the 24 px are the viewer's gap; the number expected is the same 24), with one added assertion that it is more than 0; a file that has no element yet is brought near first (`ensureMounted`), and the wait for the entries asks the pane's data (`filesInPane`). Unmodified, the spec fails 10 of 10 on the merged tree on those reads (`evidence/phase-10/collapse-spec-unmodified-on-merged.log`).

**Beyond main's spec: `tests/e2e/collapse-landing.spec.js`** (20 tests, 10 per mode): sixty files, 300 px inside the thirty-first, by the chevron and by "Viewed", with the header's place read in the page task of the collapse, in every frame drawn after the click and again after the files that came into view are drawn; a header pushed partly above the toolbar; a header that is not stuck; an expand from a stuck place and from a partly covered one; the last file collapsed at the pane's end while the reader's own scroll there is still followed, and after; a context file between two changed files, not stuck and expanded again. **The same file passes 20 of 20 on main at `6f06bbb1`** (`evidence/phase-10/main-direct/collapse-landing-on-main-final.log`; main's application with the test helpers of the phase-7 commit plus the "collapse landings" block of `diff-dom.js`, which uses only the scroll container, the file wrappers and the pinned toolbar), so what it asserts is main's behaviour and not this branch's. On the merged tree it found two differences from main, both removed: the partly covered header (the first table's third row) and the 8 px of an expand (H173). A third was measured and is not a collapse landing: H174.

Main's own whole run at `6f06bbb1` failed `chat-tabs.spec.js:85` ("persists open tabs and focus across a page reload"), which has been on the list since the planning survey and does not touch the diff; the merged tree ran that spec twenty times over in the stress stage without a failure. One test failed for a reason that is not a flake and was changed: the reload test of `fixture-isolation.spec.js`, which the new browser no longer feeds (H148, its phase-10 note).

**Corrected by this phase:** H145, H111, the `COLLAPSE_INSIDE_FILE` row of H51, parity row 99, row 9 of section 14 (the collapse is main's behaviour now, not a difference), the golden comparison (`tests/parity/compare.mjs` no longer lists the collapse landing: a difference there is a regression), and the changeset. New hazards H172 to H176; new row 39 of section 14 and row 16 of section 15 (H174).

**Deviations from the plan's text, each with its reason.** (1) "Keep the old browser directory": the install deleted it and it was downloaded again (H175). (2) The plan expected the 16 listed collapse-landing differences "gone or reduced to what main itself does": they are gone. (3) `collapse-landing.spec.js` and the helper block are new test code beyond "regression tests for what is not covered already", written so that they run on main unchanged; that is what makes main the arbiter of each assertion. (4) One behaviour that differs from main after a collapse was found and left, because it is not the collapse's (H174). (5) `fixture-isolation.spec.js` "A page reloaded while its first request is in flight" failed 2 of 2 on the merged tree and was changed, not skipped: Chromium 158 delivers no console line for a request that a reload cuts short, so the assertion that waited for the loader's line could not hold; the test now proves the interruption and the guard's entry apart (H148, its phase-10 note; `evidence/phase-10/reload-test/`).

### H174 fix: the pane is held at its end after a collapse that its end cut short

Done on 2026-11-25 and 2026-11-27 in a worktree of its own (`code-view-h174`, branch `fable-code-view-h174`, from `bed90ca1`), on the owner's decision of 2026-11-25 that H174 is fixed and not accepted. Two sessions; neither commits anything; an independent verifier commits. Session 1 built the fix, its tests and a proof that was green but for three failed executions of its repeats; session 2 found the cause of each of the three, changed the tests for them and ran the whole proof again (`h174/final2/`, green). It was cut off there on 2026-11-25 and resumed on 2026-11-27: the sequence was run once more on the same tree with three repeat stages (`h174/final3/`), one execution of the third failed (failure D below), its cause was measured and the tests changed for it, and the whole proof was run again on the final tree (`h174/final4/`). During that sequence the orchestrator ruled on how failures of whole runs are treated from then on and made `final4` the proof of record (below). No production file changed after session 1. The session's evidence is in the durable directory `/Users/tim/pair-review-codeview-evidence/h174/` (below: `h174/`); the scratch directory the earlier phases name no longer exists. Hazard H174 has what was measured, the choice of mechanism, what is built, every caller, and what is left; this part has the order of work and the proof's numbers.

**Order of work.** (1) A reference of the tree at `bed90ca1`, clean, before any production change: `tests/parity/dump.mjs` (32 dumps) and `tests/parity/visual.mjs --capture reference` (480 screenshots), run in the worktree itself, because `reference.mjs` refuses a checkout inside its own tree (`h174/reference/`, `REFERENCE.txt`, `capture.sh`). (2) The defect measured in both modes, by both controls, with the pointer away and resting (`h174/probe/before-fix/`): 8 of 8 runs end with the header at 595.19 px and the pane 92 px short of its end. (3) The other mechanism measured (`h174/probe/candidate-b/`). (4) The hold built, and its one frame found and removed (`h174/probe/after-fix-1/` to `after-fix-3/`). (5) Tests, each part of the fix taken out against them, the proof.

The parity scripts do not reach the scenario of H174 (no dump collapses the last file at the pane's end and waits for contents), so "no difference" is what they should say: nothing they cover changed. What the fix changes is stated by the new tests and by the probes. This ledger was edited after the sequence (this part and the status row); the files outside `plans/` are the ones the sequence ran on (`h174/final/tree-outside-ledger.txt`).

**The sequence was started four times; each earlier start is kept with a note.** No production file was edited between them: what changed from one start to the next is test files and `tests/CONVENTIONS.md`, as listed. (The fingerprints of the starts hash the whole tree, not each file, so this rests on the session's record; `tree-outside-ledger.txt` has the two production files' hashes as they are at the end.)

1. `h174/final-run1-before-the-tests-pointer-change/`: complete and green (unit 11535, contract 270, E2E 1006 passed three times, development bundle 1016 with 0 validator lines, 1,800 repeats with 0 failed, golden byte-identical, look check 0 of 480). Superseded because of what the durations of the new tests showed: the tests that begin with a wheel scroll to the end left the pointer resting on the last file while they waited for that file's contents, which the application keeps back ten seconds for a resting pointer; they took 11 to 22 s, the two "reader scrolls up" tests 22 s of a 30 s budget. They now take the pointer off the pane while they wait (`tests/CONVENTIONS.md` has the rule).
2. `h174/final-run2-one-dev-bundle-failure/`: complete; green but for one test of the development-bundle run, `comment-crud-advanced.spec.js:452` "Bulk Deletion via Clear All (PR mode) › should correctly refresh UI state after bulk deletion": the entry of the comment it had just saved was not in the review panel 5 s after its click on the panel's "User" segment button.
3. `h174/final-run3-stopped-one-failure/`: stopped by hand in its stress stage, because its second whole E2E run had one failed test, `comment-filter-menu.spec.js:102` "Comment filter menu (PR mode) › "Show posted" overrides the hide-after-posting preference for this review only": its helper clicked the "User" segment button and the button did not become active in ten seconds.
4. `h174/final/`: the table above. Complete; green but for two executions of its repeat stage, which was then run twice more alone on the same tree (one failed execution, then none).

**The two failures, explained** (`h174/dev-run-failure/EXPLANATION.md` and the files it names). Both are one thing, are in tests main wrote, and are not the diff pane's.
- *Cause.* Both tests call `window.aiPanel.expand()` and click the "User" segment button in the next statement. The panel opens with a width transition of 0.2 s (`pr.css`, `.ai-panel`), and the button travels about 300 px during it: two frames after `expand()` its centre is at x = 1355 in a viewport of 1280 px, at rest at x = 1058. A press that begins while the panel is opening does not reach the button: begun two frames after `expand()` and held for three frames, 0 of 20 presses clicked the button and the panel stayed on its "AI" segment, which lists no user comment; begun after the panel's transitions were over, 20 of 20 (`held-press-with-the-fix.jsonl`, development bundle, both modes). The third run shows the lost click itself (the button's class, read 24 times in ten seconds, never `active`); the second run shows the state it leaves. This is the class of H171 (a click on a control that is still moving).
- *Rate here.* 2 failures in the 15 whole E2E runs of this session before the tests were changed (9 with the shipped bundle, 6 with the development bundle: the three proof runs and three more with a trace kept on failure, `whole-dev-traced.txt`), both in PR mode, both with the machine's load average above 13 (three sequences ran back to back). Alone they did not recur: the describe of the first, 480 of 480 with the development bundle; Playwright's own click right after `expand()`, 800 of 800 (it waits for the button to stop moving, and usually that is enough).
- *Rate without the fix.* The forced press, with the two production files put back to `bed90ca1`: 0 of 20 and 20 of 20, the same (`held-press-without-the-fix.jsonl`). No whole run without the fix was made in this session; the ledger's record for `bed90ca1` is 0 failures in four whole runs (three shipped, one development bundle) on a machine that ran one sequence. Nothing the fix changes is reached by either test: neither collapses a file, and without a hold running the three changed functions of the bridge do what they did.
- *Not proven.* That the second run's failure was this and nothing else: no trace of it exists (the next stage replaced the run's `test-results`), and 480 + 3 × 1016 executions with tracing did not repeat it. The third run's failure needs no inference.
- *Dealt with, for the class.* `expandReviewPanel(page)` in `tests/e2e/helpers.js` opens the panel and waits for its transitions (the wait `external-comments.spec.js` already had for one click, H171). All 66 calls of `aiPanel.expand()` in 13 specs go through it; no assertion, timeout or expectation was changed, and nothing was skipped. The 13 specs alone: 264 passed (`changed-specs-after-class-fix.log`). `tests/CONVENTIONS.md` has the rule.

**The three failed executions of the repeats, explained and dealt with (session 2).** Each was seen once in the 5,400 executions of session 1's three repeat stages on its final tree; each is a test of an earlier phase; each was 0 of 300 alone, with the fix and without it (`h174/stress-failures/`). Session 2 found each cause by measurement (`h174/session2/`; the probe specs are in `probes/`, none is in the tree), measured each with the two production files as they are and at `bed90ca1`, and changed test code for it. No assertion, expectation or timeout was changed and nothing was skipped.

- *A. `collapse-landing.spec.js:174`* (Local mode), "the collapsed header is where it was stuck in the page task that collapsed the file, before a frame is drawn": the header at −0.5 px, against `toBeCloseTo(…, 0)`, which asks for less than 0.5.
  - *Cause.* The collapse sends the viewer the file as a scroll target, and the viewer rounds the position to a whole pixel (`roundToDevicePixel`, `Math.round`): the header lands at the file's top minus the rounded top, from −0.5 px to just under +0.5 px. The top is the sum of the heights above it, all multiples of 1/64 px (a row is 17.390625 px). Which heights is left to chance: in the background a file is given its contents only once the viewer has drawn it (`_upgradeFilesWithContents`), the test reaches the thirty-first file by the application's animated navigation, and which of the thirty files on the way that animation draws depends on its frames. In 300 runs of the test's own steps the thirty files were in 49 different states of "holds its contents" and the file's top at 18 different fractions of a pixel (`probe-a/runs3.jsonl`); 55 states and 19 fractions with the production files at `bed90ca1` (`runs-without-the-fix.jsonl`). A file's contents add 74.78 to 213.91 px to it (`a3-heights.jsonl`), never a whole number. One of the fractions is one half: the top at x.5, the position rounded up, the header at exactly −0.5 px. Recorded once in 340 probe runs (`runs.jsonl`: top 25129.5, header −0.5). Made on purpose: with the files numbered 3, 6, 7 and 14 never given their contents and every other file above given them, the top is 25249.5 and the header at −0.5 px in 39 of 40 runs with the fix and 40 of 40 without it (`a2-with-the-fix-jump-without-3_6_7_14.jsonl`, `a2-without-the-fix-…`).
  - *Not a defect of the collapse.* Half a pixel is as near as a whole-pixel scroll position comes to a place at x.5, in any implementation.
  - *Dealt with.* `readFile` of `collapse-landing.spec.js`, and the one test of `collapse-at-end-contents.spec.js` that collapses the thirty-first file, give every file above it its contents before they measure (`ensureContentUpgrades`, in the "collapse landings" block of `diff-dom.js`; it calls the application's `_ensurePierreContentUpgrade`, which main at `6f06bbb1` has too). That is the state the spec was written against on main, where every file is drawn and so every file gets its contents. With it, 300 runs: no file above without its contents; the top at four fractions (25/64 px in 285 runs; 11/64, 36/64 and 39/64 in the others); the header 0.39, 0.17, −0.44 or −0.39 px from its place (`runs-with-contents-above.jsonl`). What is still left to chance is which of the files above were drawn and so measured: a drawn file is 8 px taller than the viewer's estimate of it and one row taller for each wrapped line, and the thirty files have fifteen wrapped rows between them (`a3-heights.jsonl`, the same in 4 of 4 runs). The top is therefore at 25/64 px times one more than the number of wrapped rows drawn above; one half would take thirty-one. The spec was not run on main again with this step (main's application was not built in this session).
- *B. `scroll-landing.spec.js:118`* (Local mode), "a sidebar jump lands the same way in split layout": `setDiffView(page, 'split')` waited 5 s for the split layout; "Split" focused and not pressed, the pane unified. The entry on the list since phase 9 (`split-view.spec.js:130`) is the same state.
  - *Cause and what was done:* H171's second addendum. The options popover travels 160 px sideways in the 0.15 s of its first opening on a page; a press on "Split" in that time is released where the button no longer is. `openDiffOptions` (`tests/e2e/helpers.js`) waits for the popover's transitions, and all six places that opened the popover use it. Not recorded: Playwright's own click landing inside the travel (0 of 400 with twenty workers); what is recorded is the travel, the state such a press leaves (the failed run's), and two frames in a row with the button still at its start in 40 of 300 openings under load.
- *C. `scroll-landing.spec.js:450`* (PR mode), "A press on the pane's edge that holds no scrollbar › dragged straight down to the pane's bottom edge and let go, the reader is where they were": 3,122 px where the test expected 3,000, read while the press was held.
  - *Cause.* The test puts the pane at 3,000 px with a script, waits until two looks 0.15 s apart show the same position, and takes that as where the reader is. The viewer draws the files of that place on the next frame; their contents arrive later; the file the reader is in grows above the reader's line; and the viewer keeps that line where it is by writing the position (H151). Every write to the pane was recorded with its stack (`probe-c/`): in 20 of 20 undisturbed runs there is one, the viewer's (`applyScrollFix`), to 3,122, and it comes before the two looks, so the test's starting position is 3,122 in all of them. In the failed run it was 3,000, and 3,122 under the press: the contents came after the two looks. Made on purpose with every file's contents 0.7 s late: the starting position 3,000, and the pane moved under the held press (by 122, 134 or 256 px, the viewer's writes) in 13 of 20 runs with the fix and 4 of 20 without it (`contents-700ms-late-*.jsonl`; the delay is fixed and the test's steps are not, so the counts differ).
  - *Dealt with.* `waitForPaneSettledInPlace` (`diff-dom.js`): files drawn from the toolbar down to the pane's bottom edge, which says the viewer's pass for the new place has run and what it mounted is queued for its contents, then `waitForDiffSettled`. The describe's `beforeEach` reads the starting position after it, and so does the test of `scrollbar-drag.spec.js` that has the same sequence ("Beside the pane's scrollbar"). With the contents 0.7 s late: the pane moved 0 px under the press in 20 of 20 (`contents-700ms-late-settled-first.jsonl`).
- *What the three have in common.* A slow machine changes an order the test took for granted: which files a scroll animation draws, when a transition's first frames come, when contents arrive. `tests/CONVENTIONS.md` has a rule for each.

**A fourth failed execution of the repeats, explained and dealt with (session 2, resumed on 2026-11-27).** Session 2's sequence of 2026-11-25 (`h174/final2/`) was green in every stage, its one repeat stage included (`1800 passed (12.5m)`). Run again on the same tree with three repeat stages (`h174/final3/`; the same hash of the tracked changes outside `plans/`, `20f9e0db…`, at both ends), every stage was green (unit 11,535; contract 270; E2E `1006 passed` three times; development bundle `1016 passed` with 0 validator lines; golden `no regression`; look check 0 of 480) except the third repeat stage: `1800 passed (12.4m)`, `1800 passed (12.7m)`, then `1799 passed (12.5m)` with `1 failed`.

- *D. `collapse-scroll-anchor.spec.js:161`* (PR mode; main's own spec), "marking a file viewed keeps its collapsed header where it was stuck": `locator.check: Clicking the checkbox did not change its state`, 0.6 s into the test. The failure's own screenshot and page snapshot show the file marked viewed and collapsed, its header at the pane's top (`h174/final3/stress-3-test-results/`).
  - *Cause.* H140, seen from Playwright. The test scrolls halfway into the first file with a script and presses the "Viewed" box at once. The viewer draws the file for its new place a moment later; every draw takes the header out of the document and puts it back; when a draw falls between the press and the release the browser sends no click; and the bridge's repair clicks the control in a timer task set at the release (`PierreBridge._handlePointerUp`, `setTimeout(…, 0)`: that no click is coming is known only when the release's task is over). `check()` reads the box right after its release has been handled, and throws when that is before the timer.
  - *Measured* (`h174/session2/probe-d/`: the test's own steps with a recorder of the pointer events on the box, the bridge's own count of the file's draws at each of them, and every click with whether the browser made it; `runs.txt`, one `.summary.json` per run). With a draw of the file forced into the press (from a `mousedown` listener, after the bridge has read its count): no click of the browser's and one of the bridge's, the box ticked, the file collapsed and its header within 0.5 px of where it was stuck, in 680 of 680 runs; `check()` threw that error in 29 of 240 runs with the fix and in 34 of 200 with the two production files at `bed90ca1`. In the test's own steps with nothing forced, twenty workers: the file was drawn between the press and the release in 1 of 2,000 runs with the fix (Local mode: press at 706.9 ms, an `update` draw of the file at 710.0, release at 717.4, no click of the browser's, the bridge's click at 736.5; `check()` passed that time) and in 0 of 2,000 without it; in 296 of the 2,000 a draw of the file fell between the scroll and the press, which is the draw the press can straddle.
  - *Rate here.* One failed execution with this error in the nine complete repeat stages run on these two production files before the change (16,200 executions; 720 of them are of the two tests of that spec that tick a "Viewed" box right after a scripted scroll, a changed file's and a context file's).
  - *Rate without the fix.* The same within what was measured (forced: 34 of 200 against 29 of 240; unforced: 0 against 1 of 2,000 draws inside the press). Nothing of the fix is on the way: no hold exists before a collapse, `_queueEndHoldLook` returns at once without one, and the repair and the header's re-stamp are phase 6's, unchanged.
  - *For a reader* nothing is wrong: the click is made one task late (19 ms after the release in the recorded run) and the file is viewed and collapsed where it was.
  - *Dealt with, for the class.* `setViewedBox(entry, viewed)` in the "collapse landings" block of `tests/e2e/diff-dom.js` (Playwright locators only, so it runs against main as the block does): it clicks the box when the box is not as wanted, and waits until it is. All twelve `check()` and `uncheck()` calls on a "Viewed" box go through it: `collapse-scroll-anchor.spec.js` (3), `collapse-landing.spec.js` (3), `collapse-at-end-contents.spec.js` (1), `context-files.spec.js` (2), `file-controls.spec.js` (3). With the forced draw: 200 of 200 without an error, ticked and collapsed (`forced-helper-with-the-fix-x100-w8.summary.json`). No assertion, expectation or timeout was changed and nothing was skipped: `check()` was each test's action, and the assertions that follow it are as they were. The other 27 `check()`, `uncheck()` and `setChecked()` calls of the suite are on controls outside the files (the index page, the panels, the popovers), which no draw takes out of the document. `tests/CONVENTIONS.md` has the rule.
  - *Not done.* The repair's timer is as it was (H140). No wait for the viewer's draw was put before the press in main's spec: with the helper the order does not matter, and a draw can follow "settled" in any case (H143).

**The orchestrator's ruling on failures of whole runs (2026-11-27, during the sequence `h174/final4/`).** Four sequences in a row (`final`, `final2` run again as `final3`, `final4`) had each turned up one rare failure in a different test, at a load average of 14 to 28 on a machine of ten cores; each had been answered with a change of test code and a new sequence of an hour and a half. The orchestrator ruled that this does not converge and replaced "a failure in a whole run is explained by measurement and dealt with, then the runs are repeated" by the following (full text in `h174/HANDOFF.md`). (a) A failed execution of a test that exercises code this fix changed, or the diff pane's collapse, end-of-pane or scroll-position behaviour, is explained by measurement and dealt with, as before; a test-file change it forces is proved with the affected spec at `--repeat-each=20` and one whole E2E run. (b) Any other failed execution is run alone at `--repeat-each=50` from a cool machine; with 0 failed it is recorded on the list of known flakes and the test is not changed; if it fails alone, it is measured with the production files of `bed90ca1` too, and only a failure that needs the fix is blocking. No further test-side change outside this fix's own tests; no further whole sequence after `final4`; the 1-minute load average below 6 before each heavy run and never more than eight workers. The proof of record is `final4` and the isolation runs. A to D above were dealt with before the ruling and stand as they are in the tree.

- *E. `global-settings.spec.js:181`* (main's test; the settings page, which loads neither `pr.js` nor the bridge), "Global Settings - Section navigation › scrollspy activates the section scrolled into view", in the development-bundle run of `final4` (load average 14 at its start): the page at its bottom, "From config files & environment" active and not "Repositories" (`h174/final4/e2e-dev.log`, `e2e-dev-test-results/`). Class (b): recorded, not changed. The spec and `public/js/settings.js` are byte-identical to main's at `6f06bbb1`.
  - *Alone rate.* The test itself, alone, eight workers, the 1-minute load average 5.9 at its start: `50 passed (19.5s)` at `--repeat-each=50`, 0 failed (`h174/final4/isolation-global-settings-181-x50.log`). In whole runs: 1 failed execution in the 28 whole E2E runs of the seven sequences of the H174 sessions (`h174/final-run1-…` to `final4`; 21 with the shipped bundle, 7 with the development bundle); it is on no earlier phase's list.
  - *What is known about it* (`h174/session2/probe-e/`; measured before the ruling reached the instance that measured it, see the next paragraph; twenty workers). The test scrolls to the document's end as soon as the side navigation is visible; the snippets list is filled by a request of its own, and when its answer comes after the scroll the page grows by 34 px, the window is no longer at the document's end and the last section is not the active one. In the test's own steps with a recorder: 2 of 1,000 runs with the fix and 1 of 444 with the two production files at `bed90ca1` (that run was cut off); with the answer held back past the scroll on purpose, 9 of 20. Whether the page should follow its own growth is a question for main's settings page (a candidate for #638), not for this change.

**A second instance of the session, and what it did to `final4`.** When the ruling was sent, a second instance of this session was running from the same transcript, without the ruling (the listing of agents showed one agent, started when the ruling arrived: the session seems to have been resumed for the message while the instance already running went on). From 14:00 to 14:21 UTC on 2026-11-27 two instances worked in the worktree. The other one stopped `final4` by hand at 14:07 UTC in its first repeat stage (about execution 1,494 of 1,800), to explain failure E under the old rule; it wrote probe E, ran it with twenty workers (load average 181 at 14:18 UTC) and with the production files swapped, and meant to change the test and start a fifth sequence. The orchestrator stopped it at 14:21 UTC, put the tree back (the hash of the tracked changes outside `plans/` is `1c03e07e…`, as at the start of `final4`) and confirmed that `final4` is resumed from the interrupted stage. Nothing of that instance is in the tree: no test was changed. Its measurements of E are kept and quoted above. The interrupted stage's log is kept as `stress-1-interrupted-at-1407utc.log`; the nine executions it marks as failed, each at 0 ms, are the ones in flight when the processes were killed (1,446 had passed).

Performance was not run again: the hold is a frame callback that exists only between such a collapse and its end.

The list after phase 10 stands otherwise.

**What a verifier should know before committing.** The proof of record is `h174/final4/` with its isolation run; `final2` and `final3` ran on the same two production files with the test code before `setViewedBox`, and session 1's `final` with the test code before that. On the final tree unit, contract, the three whole E2E runs, the three repeat stages, the golden comparison and the look check have 0 failed. The development-bundle run has 0 validator lines and one failed test, E: main's test of the settings page, 50 of 50 alone, on the list of known flakes by the orchestrator's ruling. The brief's "0 failed" for that run is therefore not met to the letter, and a second development-bundle run was not made, by the ruling (no further whole sequence); the three development-bundle runs before it on the same production files (`final`, `final2`, `final3`) were `1016 passed`. The orchestrator's ruling asks the verifier to judge a failure by running the test alone: a failure alone is blocking. One thing was found that is not this task's and is not fixed: H177 (the pane with `prefers-reduced-motion`). `collapse-landing.spec.js` and `collapse-scroll-anchor.spec.js` were not run on main again after `readFile` got its new step and the "Viewed" boxes their helper (both use what main has: `_ensurePierreContentUpgrade`, and Playwright locators only). Evidence of the sessions: `h174/HANDOFF.md` names every directory.

Unit: 16 cases fail, 14 of the 15 new bridge cases and both new page cases (the fifteenth, "holds nothing for a collapse that is not drawn when it returns: inside a batch", states what is also true without a hold). `collapse-at-end-contents.spec.js`: 12 of 18 fail, the twelve in which contents arrive; the six that state that nothing is held, and the two new tests of `scrollbar-drag.spec.js`, pass, as tests of what the hold must not do should.
- *A probe of the verifier's own* (`verify/probe/`: two specs and a library, copied into `tests/e2e` to run and removed; it records every write to the pane's position with its stack, every input event, what the bridge made of the input, and the pane after every drawn frame; 178 executions in three runs with the fix, 26 without). With no request held, by the chevron and by "Viewed", pointer away and resting, a changed file and a context file as the last item, unified and split layout, device scale factors 1, 1.5 and 2, both modes (48 executions; in 42 of them no request was held): the pane 0 px short of its end at rest and in every drawn frame, the header at most 0.66 px from where the collapse put it in every drawn frame (502.84 to 503.19 px; a context file 502.67 to 503.19; split 503.39 to 502.73), one write of the hold per collapse that contents followed, 0.1 ms after the viewer's own (`applyScrollFix`) and in the same frame, and the hold over within 0.6 s of the contents being shown (10.4 s after the collapse with the pointer resting). The same probe with the production files of `bed90ca1`: 595.19 px and 92 px short (context file 572.19 and 69; split 560.73 and 58) in 26 of 26. The reader scrolling up by the wheel, by Page Up and by the thumb, at once and 0.3, 1.5, 2, 4 and 6 s after the collapse, with the contents released before the input (0, 40, 100 and 200 ms), after it, or arriving at the application's own ten seconds (84 executions): the hold was over when the bridge's own listener for the input returned in every execution in which the probe read that (50), it wrote nothing after the input in any of the 84, and the pane ended at least as far from its end as the reader left it in all of them. A collapse in the middle of the review: no hold and no write but the viewer's. An expand of the collapsed file, by a click and by a script, a second collapse of the file above (a new hold; both headers in place within 0.6 px, the pane at its end), the whitespace option flipped, and a navigation of the application, each while the hold ran: the hold over, no write of it afterwards, no page error.
- *The limit, measured, and what follows it.* With the request for the contents held, the hold ended 14,998 or 14,999 ms after the collapse (4 of 4) without having written anything. Contents that arrive after that are not followed: the header at 595.19 px and the pane 92 px short, which is the defect as it was. Section 15, row 16, now names this (with the application's own longest wait, the ten seconds for a resting pointer, the contents were shown 10.2 to 10.3 s after the collapse in 16 of 16).
- *Also measured, as this entry says:* a key that scrolls nothing (Shift), a press on a code row, and a wheel step down at the pane's end, each between the collapse and the contents, end the hold, and the contents then leave the header at 595.19 px (10 of 10).
- *Notes that block nothing.* (1) A hold begins when the pane is within 1 px of its end, and a look counts the pane as at its end within 0.5 px: a hold begun between the two would count no frame at rest and last its 15 s, writing only if the pane grew. Not seen: at scale factor 1 the hold was over 0.45 and 0.48 s after a collapse with nothing to wait for, and at 1.5 and 2 it was over 0.66 to 0.67 s after a collapse whose contents came at once (4 of 4). (2) `expandReviewPanel` calls `window.aiPanel?.expand()`; the two calls it replaced in `inline-feedback-context.spec.js` had no `?.`, so a page without the panel no longer throws there (the assertions that follow still fail).

### Review fixes: the owner's review of PR #646

Done on 2026-11-27 in the worktree `code-view-h174` (branch `fable-code-view-h174`), on top of the H174 commit `80b1705a`, in two stages; not committed by the sessions (a verifier commits, one commit on top of the H174 commit). The owner reviewed PR #646 and found six defects (`review-2026-11-27/findings.md` in the evidence directory); a seventh is H177 (`prefers-reduced-motion`), found by the H174 work. A unit stage, in a worktree of its own from `bed90ca1`, fixed the six with a failing unit test first each (`review-2026-11-27/HANDOFF.md`); its uncommitted diff applied here with `git apply --3way` without a conflict (it touches no hunk of the H174 commit), its six new files copied. The browser stage (this part) measured main, wrote the end-to-end tests, settled what only a browser could, fixed H177 and made three small changes. Evidence: `review-2026-11-27/stage-b/` (`HANDOFF.md`, `on-main.md`, `red/`, `green/`, `proof/`).

**What was built, per finding** (the hazard entries have the mechanism, the callers and the tests):

| # | Finding | Fix | Hazard | Main at `6f06bbb1`, measured |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | A GitHub thread on a changed line of a viewed or generated file went to the file's comment zone as a fallback card and stayed there | `PierreBridge.isLineInDiff` (data only, the collapse left out); the external-thread manager and `ensureLinesVisible` ask it | H178 | The same (fallback card in the zone, stays after opening): older |
| 2 | Suggestion blocks had an empty line after every line and lost drawn lines outside the hunks | `getCodeFromLines` answers every line of the range, without its ending, or null; the button refuses null with a warning | H181, F32 | Empty lines: the same, older. A drag cannot cross a separator on main, nor on the branch since H184 |
| 3 | Tour stops in collapsed files were skipped; files the tour opened stayed open after it when unmounted | The anchor scan asks `isLineInDiff`; open and re-collapse from data through `setFileCollapsed` | H178, H179 | Changed files: skipped, older. Context files: shown and re-collapsed, so the skip was the migration's |
| 4 | A double Save posted twice; a failed save of a scrolled-away form left Save disabled | The hook returns the save's promise; the guard's `finally` resets unconditionally | H183 | Double post: the same, older. No unmounted forms on main |
| 5 | `_canonicalFilePath` asked the mounted elements before the data | Data only: exact, normalized, suffix; changed before context; pane order | H180 | Function absent; every file had an element and `./src/index.js` resolved right: the migration's |
| 6 | The form id had no last line: a range form reused the line form and saved the line; + twice gave two annotations on one element | Id with the last line; an open form gets the caret instead of a second one | H182 | The same for changed files, older; main's context files closed an open form first |

**Small changes decided by the orchestrator.** (i) The refusal warning has a second sentence: "Expand the hidden lines first." (`comment-manager.js`; its unit test asserts the whole text). **Every failed execution, with its rate (the orchestrator's ruling of 2026-11-27).** Two, both in tests of this work, both explained by measurement and dealt with; no failure of any other test in any run of this part.

- *B. `collapsed-file-annotations.spec.js:108`* (PR mode, "a thread on a changed line of a generated file and of a viewed file ..."), 1 of 20 executions in its `--repeat-each=20` run (1-minute load 4.9 at the start, 5-minute 8.6): `isFileCollapsed` read once, right after Playwright's click on the chevron, answered "collapsed". Cause, measured (`chevron-read.jsonl`, 50 runs of the two chevron clicks alone, load 3.7): in 1 of 100 clicks the browser sent no click (the file was drawn between the press and the release) and the bridge made it in a task of its own after the release (H140; an untrusted click); a read made before that task sees the file still collapsed. The same class as the H174 fix's `check()` failure. The test now polls the state after every action that changes it (six reads); with that, 200 of 200 alone. The whole E2E runs above ran with the corrected test.

*Probes in a headless browser through the E2E test server, both modes* (`verify/probes/`, results `*.jsonl`; the probe specs were removed from the tree). Threads (PR mode; Local mode made no thread request): on a generated and on a viewed file, a thread on a changed line, on the deleted side of that line, on a context line of a hunk, on a line in a hidden gap (revealed) and an outdated thread whose original line the diff shows are annotations on their lines, 0 px from the row's bottom edge through three collapse and expand cycles; a thread on a line the file does not have is the fallback card in the zone; a second `render()` duplicates nothing; with the contents held, one contents request per file. Suggestion blocks: one line, a range, the deleted side (the old text), lines expanded by hand in both directions and on both sides in a file whose old and new numbers differ by two, CRLF contents (no carriage return in the block), a line of three backticks (a four-backtick fence) and an empty line, all exact; a range with a hidden line, a line past the file's end, line 0 and a patch-only gap are null; through a comment's Edit the block is what the PUT carries and what is stored. Tours: stops in a generated and a viewed file and in a collapsed context file that had no element are mounted; a tour of only such stops starts at stop 1; exit collapses what the tour opened, with no element, and leaves no stop annotation; "Viewed" stays set. Collapsing two files that have no element above a reader at rest moves the reader's place by 0.31 and 0.09 px (no tour; `setFileCollapsed` called directly), and a tour's exit far below them by 0.41 px against the stop's landed place. Saves, against the worker's database: a double click followed by two more clicks and Cmd/Ctrl+Enter, and Cmd/Ctrl+Enter three times, with the POST held, each one POST and one row; a 500 and a dropped connection leave the form with its text and Save usable, and the retry makes one row; Save and then Cancel with the POST held makes one row and one card; the edit form's double click one adopt request, a refused adopt an alert and a usable form, the retry one adopted comment. Paths: nineteen forms of path give the same answers with either index file mounted; a collapse, `scrollToFile` and `scrollToContextFile` by a path in another form act on the right file while it has no element. Forms: ids `form-<file>-14-14-RIGHT` and `form-<file>-14-16-RIGHT`, saved in either order as 14-14 and 14-16, no form annotation left; Cancel then + gives an empty form; a form survives its file's collapse and its unmount with its text, and + then gives it the caret. Reduced motion, unified and split: 0 hole frames of about 200 wheeling down and of about 180 wheeling up; the collapsed header within 0.3 px in every frame, 0 animations in the click's task; the only property seen animating while wheeling is the gutter buttons' `visibility`; the list and its sticky child have `transition-property: none` and every other element sampled 0.01 ms; without the setting nothing differs from before.

*Corrections and observations of the verifier (nothing changed for them).* (1) Failure B above: in `chevron-read.jsonl` all 100 reads made right after the click saw the file open, and one click of the 100 was the bridge's own (untrusted). The probe shows that the repair happens at about that rate; that the failed read fell before the repair's task is inferred from it, not observed. (2) The chat panel's file links match a path exactly (`ChatPanel._handleFileLinkClick`, as main's did at source level): a link whose path is in another form (`[[file:./src/index.js:20]]`) does not reach `_canonicalFilePath`; it asks to add that path as a context file, here as on main. (3) A whitespace toggle rebuilds the diff and drops an open comment form with its text, in both modes; + on the line then opens a new, empty form (no form annotation is left behind to take the caret). Not this work's. (4) At a tour's exit the pane came to rest up to 40 px from where two equal reads 200 ms apart had found it at the last stop: -40 to +20 px over eight exits of a tour with no stop in a collapsed file, the end position the same every time, and the same spread with stops in collapsed files. The re-collapse adds nothing to it. Taken to be the stop's own landing still settling when the probe read; not measured further, and not this work's. (5) `PRManager._canonicalFilePath` copies the changed files' paths into an array before its exact lookup, which needs none; a cost of one array per call, not a fault. (6) The changeset does not mention that the suggestion button now refuses a range with a line the diff does not show (a comment saved over a gap, edited): on main that inserted a partial block.

### Rebase onto main v5.4.4 (`8e3c7a0e`) and the nested file tree

Done on 2026-11-27 in the worktree `code-view-h174` (branch `fable-code-view-h174`), from `213bc3f3` (the review-fixes commit; 15 commits on main `6f06bbb1`). Evidence: `/Users/tim/pair-review-codeview-evidence/rebase-2026-11-27/` (below: `rebase/`; `HANDOFF.md` names every file). The branch as it was is kept as `fable-code-view-h174-pre-rebase` (`213bc3f3`).

**What main brought.** Six commits after `6f06bbb1`: `b5a5286f` (a Gemini model in the Antigravity provider: `src/ai/antigravity-provider.js`, its unit test, the provider-models skill, `config.example.json`), `97002236` (a close button on every toast: `Toast.addCloseButton`, `Toast.FADE_MS`, a dismiss that clears the toast's timer; `tests/unit/toast.test.js`, one test in `review-submission.spec.js`), `d32cb4a5` (the toast rules moved from `public/css/pr.css` into a new `public/css/toast.css`, linked by `index.html`, `local.html` and `pr.html`; one test each in `bulk-actions.spec.js`, `global-settings.spec.js` and the new `repo-settings.spec.js`), `84dd7f13` (release v5.4.4), `63d8ffc9` (the file navigator as a nested folder tree: `PRManager.buildFileNavigatorTree` replaces `groupFilesByDirectory`, `renderFileGroup(node)` recurses, `FileOrderUtils.buildDirectoryTree` and `compareFilePaths`, and `sortFilesByPath` now sorts by `compareFilePaths`: segment by segment, a folder's own files before its subfolders) and `8e3c7a0e` (the nested chevrons and the sort order of merged folders; two tests in `pr-page.spec.js`). None of them touches the diff pane, the bridge or the comment code.

**Conflicts: none.** `git rebase origin/main` replayed all 15 commits without a conflict (`rebase/rebased-commits.txt`). `git range-diff 6f06bbb1..fable-code-view-h174-pre-rebase origin/main..HEAD` (`rebase/range-diff.txt`): 14 commits `=`; phase 2 `!`, and only in `tests/e2e/pr-page.spec.js`, where git placed the branch's rewritten "Diff Display" block (both modes) next to main's new "File Navigator Tree" block; the range-diff shows the branch's hunks re-aligned around that block, with no line of either side changed. The commit ids of this ledger before this part are those before the rebase; after it: phase 0 `0472f1d9`, phase 1 `b0643f0c`, phase 2 `077404ab`, phase 3 `02045035`, phase 4 `4961927b`, phase 5 `2e410cde`, phase 6 `d10605cd`, phase 7 `6e1a4c03`, phase 8 `74eb13e4`, phase 9 `08d91977`, phase 10 `332cbaa0`, the two ledger commits `19c8dd0f` and `7368f8ad` (was `bed90ca1`), the H174 fix `f6bc15a1` (was `80b1705a`), the review fixes `30381e61` (was `213bc3f3`).

**Integration review: what a textual rebase cannot see.**
- *The navigator's entries.* The branch changes none of `updateFileList`, `buildFileNavigatorTree`, `renderFileGroup` or `renderFileItem`; an entry's click still calls `scrollToFile` or, for a context entry, `scrollToContextFile`, and then `setActiveFileItem`. Everything that finds an entry again looks it up by `.file-item[data-path]` anywhere in the document (`setActiveFileItem`, `updateFileItemViewedState` with its `context-file-item` match, the drawer's close on a click inside `.file-item`), which a nested folder does not change. The sidebar shows the Viewed state, not the collapse; the active entry is set by a click (and `scrollToFile`), on main and here alike: no code marks the active entry from the pane's scroll.
- *Order.* `sortFilesByPath` is the one source of the changed files' order in both modes: its result is the navigator's input, `canonicalFileOrder` (the review panel's order) and the order of `changedFilesByPath`, from which `_buildPaneEntries` takes the changed files. Main's new unit test pins that the tree flattens to the order of `sortFilesByPath`, so the pane and the navigator agree on changed files, here as on main. A context file is placed by `_buildPaneEntries` before the first entry whose path sorts after it by code unit, which is main's rule (`allWrappers.find(w => w.dataset.fileName > contextFile.file)`, unchanged at `8e3c7a0e`); the navigator puts it in tree order. The two can differ, on main as here and before this rebase too: with changed files `src/z.js` and `src/a/b.js` and a context file `src/a/c.js`, the navigator lists `src/z.js`, `src/a/b.js`, `src/a/c.js` and the pane draws `src/a/c.js` first. Nothing on the branch relies on that agreement: the review panel's order holds changed files only, and `_canonicalFilePath` is data only (H180), its tie-break the pane's order. The specs that read the sidebar's order (`diff-render.spec.js` "every file is listed, in path order, in the pane and in the sidebar"; `context-files.spec.js` "In the sidebar: always by path" and its two lists of the changed files) use corpora whose paths are each one folder deep (`corpus-edge/`; `corpus-evict/`, `src/`, `zz/`), where the tree's order, the old grouped order and the pane's order coincide: they assert what they asserted and pass. The sidebar's `.file-item` selectors of the other specs and of `tests/parity` (by `data-path`, or `first()` and `nth(1)` of fixtures with root-level and one-folder paths) mean what they meant.
- *CSS.* The branch's rules in `pr.css` are as they were: the virtualized-viewer block and the reduced-motion block with its two exemptions for the viewer's list and its sticky child (H177). Main's removal of the toast rules was in lines the branch had not touched; `pr.css` has no `.toast` or `#toast-container` rule left (the chat panel's own `.chat-panel__toast` and its two keyframes are main's and unchanged), so nothing is duplicated between `pr.css` and `toast.css`, which both pages load after `pr.css`. The navigator's new rules (`.file-group .file-group`, `.file-group-name`, the chevron of an expanded group) are main's; the branch has no rule for the sidebar.
- *The refusal warning* (`CommentManager.insertSuggestionBlock`, `window.toast.showWarning`) with the close button, measured in both modes (`rebase/toast-probe/`, a probe spec copied into `tests/e2e` for the run and removed, 10 runs per mode): the warning has its text and a close button (`aria-label` "Dismiss notification", 24 by 24 px); a click on it removes the warning in 0.39 to 0.43 s (main's 0.3 s fade), the comment form stays open with its typed text, and the pane's scroll position is the same before and after (352 px). `suggestion-block.spec.js`'s `toHaveText` on `.toast-warning` passes with the button inside the toast (the button has no text).
- *Shared functions changed:* none. No production file is changed by this part.

**The one adaptation: main's `repo-settings.spec.js` and the branch's console guard.** Main's new test makes `/api/providers` answer 500 on purpose to raise the settings page's error toast. Run on the branch it failed on the console guard of phase 1 (`tests/e2e/fixtures.js`), which fails a test for any console error not named: the browser's "Failed to load resource ... 500" for `/api/providers` and the page's "Error loading providers: Error: Failed to fetch providers" (`rebase/pre/targeted.log`). The describe now names those two lines with `test.use({ expectedConsoleErrors: [...] })`, the guard's route for an error a test provokes (as `comment-form-identity.spec.js` and `context-files.spec.js` do); the test's steps and assertions are main's. Alone at `--repeat-each=20`: `20 passed` (`rebase/pre/repo-settings-x20.log`). The other five new tests of main pass on the virtualized pane unchanged (`rebase/pre/targeted.log`: 18 specs, 386 passed, 11 skipped, that one failed, before the change).

**The changeset.** `.changeset/codeview-review-fixes.md` has the sentence verifier observation (6) of the review fixes asked for: the suggestion button now refuses, with a warning, a range that includes lines the diff does not show, where it used to insert a block of only the shown lines.

**A question of the owner's, measured on main: does a toggle of "Hide whitespace changes" discard an open comment form with typed text?** Yes, on main at `8e3c7a0e` in both modes, 20 of 20 (`rebase/whitespace-on-main/RESULT.md`): a scratch worktree of `origin/main` (`pnpm install --offline`, main's own test server, fixtures and helpers; removed and pruned afterwards), a probe that opens a form with main's `openCommentFormOnLine`, types into it, checks "Hide whitespace changes" and waits for the `?w=1` answer and the rebuilt pane; afterwards no `.user-comment-form` exists in the page (counted through shadow roots too) and no textarea holds the text, with the server's own `?w=1` answer and with `?w=1` answered by the unchanged diff. The branch does the same (section 11, "Review fixes", verifier observation (3)): the behaviour is older than the migration. Nothing was changed for it.
