**Every failed execution.** One, before the proof, in the targeted run: main's `repo-settings.spec.js:14`, on the console guard (the two errors it provokes are logged on every run); dealt with above. No execution failed in any stage of the proof, so nothing was isolated; `global-settings.spec.js:181` did not fail. No new flake; the list of known flakes stands.

**Not done, by the brief.** No three-in-a-row and no stress runs (an independent verifier runs them). Performance was not run again: nothing in the pane changed.

**Corrected by this part:** the status table (a row for this part), section 14's statement of which main it compares with, and the commit ids above.

### Drag clamp: a line selection stops at hidden lines (H184)

Done on 2026-11-27 in the worktree `code-view-h174` (branch `fable-code-view-h174`), on top of the adoption commit `6d7b9973`, in two stages; not committed by the sessions (a verifier commits, one commit of its own on top of `6d7b9973`, so that the owner can drop it). The owner asked for the follow-up of section 14 row 46: on main a drag on the line numbers cannot cross a "N unmodified lines" separator, on the branch it crossed in both directions and the form posted the hidden lines. The decided design: a selection is cut back to the run of shown lines that holds the line where the gesture started, decided in the bridge from data, so every consumer sees one range. A unit stage built it in a worktree of its own (`code-view-review-fixes`, branch `fable-code-view-drag`, uncommitted; `drag-2026-11-27/HANDOFF.md` in the evidence directory); its diff (`git diff HEAD` there, byte-identical to `drag-2026-11-27/fix/tracked.diff`) applied here with `git apply --3way` without a conflict and its two new unit files were copied; `pnpm test` then `11617 passed` (11,593 at the adoption commit, 24 new). The browser stage (this part) settled the seven questions only a browser could, found and fixed one window the unit stage could not see, wrote the end-to-end tests and changed the one existing test the change breaks. Evidence: `drag-2026-11-27/stage-b/` (`HANDOFF.md`, `probes/`, `main/`, `mutations/`, `red/`, `unitstage/`, `green/`, `proof/`).

**What was built** (H184 has the mechanism). `PierreBridge._shownRuns` (the one definition of what a file shows; `_showsLine` asks it), `_cutToShownRun`, `_cutForList`, `_cutViewerSelection`, the viewer's four selection callbacks, `getLineSelection`, `selectLines`, `_restoreView` and `_publish` cutting; `PierreGutter.createGutterButtons`' button drag passing its start line first. Added by this stage: the cut is made against the diff a mounted file draws as well as against its item (`_cutForList` with `getRenderedDiff()`, `_drawnExpansionMap`; an optional `map` argument of `_cutToShownRun`, `_shownRuns` and `_expandedRanges`), and four unit tests of it. Added in round 2 (below, "Round 2"): the cut end is named on the start's side, or in unified layout on the additions side for a range started on a deleted row and cut at its bottom (`_addressCutEnd`, `PierreBridge._unchangedTwin`), and four unit tests of it.

**Callers of every changed function, re-grepped on this tree** (`grep -n` of `public/js` and `src`; nothing outside `pierre-bridge.js` and `pierre-gutter.js` calls any of them; `public/js/local.js` overrides none, so both modes share them):

| Function | Callers | Effect on each |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `_showsLine` | `isLineVisible`, `isLineInDiff`, `_listShowsLine` | None: `_shownRuns` with the default map answers the same lines (the hunks with a count on the side, plus `_expandedRanges`) |
| `_shownRuns` (new; optional `map`) | `_showsLine` (no map: the default), `_cutToShownRun` (both sides of a range, with the map it is given) | n/a |
| `_expandedRanges` (optional fourth argument `map`, default `this._expansionMap(record)`, the expression its body used before) | `_foldManualExpansions`, `addContextRanges`, `_shownRuns`, `getCodeFromLines` | None for the three that pass no map; `_shownRuns` passes the map only for a drawn diff |
| `_cutToShownRun` (new) | `_cutForList` (the item's diff without a map; the drawn diff with `_drawnExpansionMap`) | n/a. Round 2: a cut end goes through `_addressCutEnd`; an uncut range is returned as it is, as before |
| `_addressCutEnd` (new, round 2) | `_cutToShownRun` (only when the end was cut) | n/a |
| `PierreBridge._unchangedTwin` (new, static, round 2) | `_addressCutEnd`; `tests/unit/line-selection-cut.test.js` | n/a |
| `_cutForList` (new) | `getLineSelection`, `selectLines` (inside its `_onList` command), `_restoreView`, `_cutViewerSelection` | Each gets the range cut against the item and, for a mounted file that still draws another diff, against that diff |
| `_drawnExpansionMap` (new) | `_cutForList` | n/a |
| `_cutViewerSelection` (new) | the viewer's `onLineSelectionStart`, `onLineSelectionChange`, `onLineSelected`, `onLineSelectionEnd`; `_publish` | Corrects the viewer's selection with `notify: false` when the cut changed it |
| `getLineSelection` | the gutter's dep in `_renderGutter` (`PierreGutter.createGutterButtons` -> `clickTarget` -> `resolveClickTarget`, comment and chat buttons); `tests/e2e/diff-dom.js` `lineSelection`; contract specs `bridge/gutter.spec.js`, `list-write-in-callback.spec.js`, `reconcile.spec.js` | The cut range: the + and the chat button act on it. Inside one run unchanged |
| `selectLines` | the gutter's dep in `_renderGutter` (the button drag, `onMove`); contract specs | Cut from `start`; nothing when `start` is not shown |
| `_restoreView` | `_writeList` | A selection the new list hides part of is put back cut; one whose first line it hides is not put back |
| `_publish` | `reconcile`, `_restoreTrimmedCards`, `setCollapsed`, `upgradeFileContents`, `materializeDeferred`, `setNotice`, `_dropRetiredExpansions`, `addContextRanges`, `addAnnotation`, `addAnnotations`, `removeAnnotation`, `removeAnnotationsByType` (12 call sites) | Only when the viewer's selection is in the published item and the new diff (or the diff still drawn) hides some of it: one `setSelectedLines` or `clearSelectedLines` with `notify: false`. Of these only `reconcile` (a context file's ranges or contents), `upgradeFileContents` and `addContextRanges` change the shown lines, and those two grow them; the rest cut nothing. The return value is unchanged |
| viewer options `onLineSelected`, `onLineSelectionEnd` | the viewer | The page's `onLineSelect` / `onLineSelectionEnd` get the cut range; no page passes either (`PRManager._bridgeInteractionOptions` does not) |
| `createGutterButtons` (`onMove`) | `_renderGutter` | `selectLines(startLine, current, side)` instead of `(min, max, side)` |

*Duplication flagged, not changed.* `getCodeFromLines` walks the hunks itself to find a line's index and asks `_expandedRanges` for the lines outside them; its "is this line drawn" is `_shownRuns`' answer by a second route (H181's code; a later cleanup can make it ask `_showsLine` per line). `_retireExpansions` and `_cutForList` both read "the diff a mounted file draws" as `record._element?.isConnected ? record._instance.getRenderedDiff?.() : null`, and `_awaitsContents` and `isDiffDisplayed` make the same read with guards of their own; one helper could serve the four. Not done here: it touches H146's and H174's code.

**The seven browser questions** (`probes/zz-probe-drag.spec.js`, a probe spec copied into `tests/e2e` for each run and removed; it records every selection callback of the viewer with the raw range and what the viewer holds after the bridge, and the highlighted rows at every microtask checkpoint at which they changed, by a `MutationObserver` on the file's shadow root, and at every frame; results `probes/fixed-all.jsonl`, summary `fixed-all-summary.txt`, checks `check-sides-fixed.txt`; both modes):

1. *Is the uncut range ever drawn, for a frame, during a drag past a separator?* No. Over the 54 records of the final files (50 of them with recorded states: unified and split, both columns, across columns, from a deleted row, shift-click, the + button's drag, lines expanded by hand, a context file, the content upgrade mid-drag, the two windows of question 4), no recorded state, at a checkpoint or a frame, holds a row on both sides of a separator or a hidden line. Inside the callback, after the viewer's own wrapper had made the uncut range its selection and before the bridge's correction, the rows marked are still the previous row's cut range: the viewer queues its own draw for the next frame and the bridge's `setSelectedLines` draws the cut range at once and takes the queued draw off, so the uncut range does not reach the document at all. The same probe on the production files of `6d7b9973` (`probes/head-q1.jsonl`): 12 of 14 gestures hold both sides (all but the two inside one hunk), so the recorder sees what it is meant to see. The exception was the window of question 4, fixed (below).
2. *Where is the + after a cut drag, and does it open the range form?* On the selection's last row, in both directions (17 after 15 down to 79; 79 after 79 up to 15; 20 and 35 in the context file), placed there by the viewer (`placeUtilityFromSelection`) and not by the pointer, which rests on 79 or 15. *Correction (round 2):* in round 1 this was not true for a drag or shift-click let go on a deleted row past the separator in unified layout: there was no + in the file at all ("Round 2" below); now the + is on the last row there too. The getter the gutter reads answers that row (H130), so the + opens "Lines 15-17" and Save posts `line_start: 15, line_end: 17, side: RIGHT`; 79 up to 15 posts 77-79; 17 to 77 and 77 to 17 open a one-line form ("Add comment", no range indicator) on the line the drag started on. In split layout the same for each column (`side: LEFT` on the left).
3. *A content upgrade in the middle of a drag.* The pointer session survives the item's re-render (the `pre` element is the same before and after, both modes). With the contents held, 15 down to 79 is 15-16 (the patch's run); after the upgrade lands and is drawn, a move back to 12 gives 12-15 and a move to 79 gives 15-17, and the release reports 15-17, the cut of the new diff. (In PR mode the probe's first press landed on 16, the gesture gotcha below; the outcome was the same with 16 as anchor: 16-17.)
4. *The window while a reveal waits for its highlight.* On a file whose contents are in, there is no such window: a reveal is drawn in the frame in which the data shows it, held worker results or not, at 120, 3,000 and 8,000 lines (`probes/q4len.jsonl`; the renderer draws the new diff of the same contents at once, F28's `getDiffForNextRender`). A window exists when the new diff needs a highlight result: the content upgrade, and a reveal that loads the contents first (`ensureLinesVisible` on a file without contents). Opened on purpose (contents held, worker results held, contents released; `probes/q4win.jsonl` with the unit stage's files): during the reveal of 18-76 a drag from 15 to 79 was not cut (the data showed all of 7-85) and the viewer highlighted rows 15, 16, 78 and 79, on both sides of the separator it still drew; during the upgrade the cut end was 17, a row the patch's diff does not draw, and the + was gone. Fixed at the cause (H184, "Against the diff that is drawn"): both windows now give 15-16 with the + on 16, and 15-16 stays once the new diff is drawn (`probes/q4win-fixed.jsonl`). How long the window lasts without holding anything (`probes/q4nat.jsonl`, from the moment the bridge took the contents until the new diff was drawn, a synthetic file with two changes): 26 and 27 ms (2 frames) for an upgrade of 120 lines, 219 ms (14 frames) for an upgrade of 3,000 lines, and 990 ms (60 frames) for a reveal that loaded the contents of an 8,000-line file, through which the data already showed the revealed line; in the other eight runs the window had closed before the probe's first read. So up to about a second on a large file: long enough for a drag that begins as the file appears.
5. *Across columns.* The viewer reports the range with `endSide` as read: a drag from the left column at 15 to the right column at 79 is `{start: 15, side: 'deletions', end: 79, endSide: 'additions'}` on every row past the separator, cut to end 17, and the highlight ends at the right column's row 17 (both columns' rows 15-17 are marked: the viewer marks rows by index). In unified layout a drag from the deleted row 12 to 79 is `{12, deletions, 79, additions}`, cut to 17; `getLineSelection` answers `{start: 12, end: 17, side: 'LEFT'}`. *What main posts for a drag from a deleted row to a context row inside one hunk* (main at `8e3c7a0e`, a scratch worktree, removed and pruned; `main/main-drag.jsonl`): the selection is `{11, 15, LEFT}` (raw `endSide: 'additions'`); the + sits on the last row, the right side's 15, and opens a one-line form there (the range is the left side's, the + the right side's, and `resolveClickTarget` asks for the same side), which posts `line_start: 15, line_end: 15, side: RIGHT, diff_position: 12`; the same in split layout from the left 11 to the right 14 (posts the right 14). The branch does exactly that, with and without the clamp (`probes/fixed-all.jsonl`, `q5-*`). Older than this change; not changed. *Correction (round 2):* for a range cut at a separator, the cut end is now named in the start's column (H184, "Where the cut end is named"): the left 15 to the right 79 is `LEFT 15-17` with the + on the left 17 and "Lines 15-17" (round 1: the + on the right 17 and a one-line form there); the deleted 12 to 79 in unified layout is unchanged (`LEFT 12-17`, the + on the right 17, its one-line form), and so is every range that crosses no separator.
6. *Lines expanded by hand, the file scrolled out of the mounted window and back.* The expansion is kept: after alpha's element is gone and is back, line 72 is shown by data and drawn, the selection 72-79 is still held and highlighted 72-79, both modes.
7. *Shift-click across a separator.* With 14-15 selected, a shift-press on 79 reports `onLineSelectionStart` with `{14, 79}`, cut at once to 14-17; each pointer move while the button is held reports `onLineSelectionChange` with 14-79 again (the cut range differs from the one computed from the anchor), cut again; then the release. So the vendor does not report the press alone; every report is cut. Rows 77-79 are marked at no checkpoint and in no frame.

**End-to-end tests** (`tests/e2e/line-selection-cut.spec.js`, new, 22 tests per mode (18 in round 1); helpers in `diff-dom.js`: `dragLineNumbers`, which returns the selection the application reports with the pointer held and after the release and makes the gesture again when the press did not land on its line, `shiftClickLineNumber`, `dragFromCommentButton`, `watchSelectedRows` / `selectedRowsWatched`, `gutterButtonsLine`). Section (a) of the unit stage's hand-off, scenario by scenario: 1 and 2 in unified layout and in each column of the split layout (selection held and released, every highlighted state, the + on the last row, the form's "Lines a-b", the POST); 3 (one-line form both ways, both layouts); 4 (11 to 13, both layouts; a guard); 5 (72 to 77 and 79 up to 15 after expanding 72-76); 6 (from one column into the other across the separator, both ways: `LEFT 15-17` with the + on the left 17 and "Lines 15-17", `RIGHT 15-17` with the + on the right 17; round 1 had the left-to-right case only, with the + on the right 17 and a one-line form); 7 (the deleted row 12 to 79 and to the deleted 81: `LEFT 12-17`, the + on the right 17, that row's one-line form); 8 (shift-click: 14-17); 9 (the + button's drag, 16 to 79 gives 16-17, 77 to 15 gives 77; never the far side); 10 (a context file with ranges 10-20 and 28-40: 15-20 posted, 35 up to 15 gives 28-35); 11 (the whitespace toggle: a selection started below the separator is put back as its shown part ending at 79; one started on an expanded line is not put back); and the window of question 4 (cut at what is drawn, 15-16, the + on 16, 15-16 once the new diff is drawn). Added in round 2: a drag let go on the deleted 81 from 15 and from 8 (held and released `RIGHT a-17`, the + on 17, "Lines a-17", the POST), a shift-click on the deleted 81 with 14-15 selected (`RIGHT 14-17`, the + on 17, "Lines 14-17", the POST), and a file whose old and new numbers differ (`shiftedCorpus` in the spec), unified (new 12 to the deleted old 60: `RIGHT 12-18`, the + on 18; the deleted 60 up to new 12: `LEFT 56-60`, the + on the left 60) and split (R12 to L61 `RIGHT 12-18`, R64 to L10 `RIGHT 59-64`, L10 to R64 `LEFT 10-15`, L61 to R12 `LEFT 56-61`), each with the form's "Lines a-b" and the line, end and side the form carries. Its header comment says that a drag no longer makes such a range. `selectLineRange` is unchanged (every caller drags inside one run; a drag across a separator now uses `dragLineNumbers`). **Round 2** (2026-11-28, after the independent verification rejected round 1; evidence `stage-b/round2/`). The verifier's blocking finding: in unified layout a drag or a shift-click that ends on a deleted row past a separator left the file with no gutter + and no chat button while the selection lasted, 10 of 10 in each mode (`verify/probes/plus.jsonl`, `zz-verify-plus.spec.js`), because the cut end stayed on the deletions side at an unchanged row; the same end, where old and new numbers differ, made `getLineSelection` answer other lines than those highlighted. Re-reading the verifier's probe records (`verify/probes/clamp.jsonl`, summarized in `round2/probes/verifier-clamp-summary.txt`) found the same cause in three more gestures, two of which post wrong lines: split R64 up to the left column's 10 gave `RIGHT 56-64` ("Lines 56-64", three hidden lines), unified L60 up to R12 gave "Lines 59-60" for L56-L60, split L61 to R12 "Lines 59-61" for L56-L61. The fix and its rule are in H184 ("Where the cut end is named"); in short, a cut end is named on the side the range started on, except in unified layout for a range started on a deleted row and cut at its bottom, which is named on the additions side. Measured on the fixed files with the verifier's own probes, both modes: `zz-verify-plus` 40 of 40 records with the + on 17 after the release and while the pointer hovers line 9 (`round2/probes/plus-fixed.jsonl`); `zz-verify-clamp` 348 records, 0 flagged by its summary, every gesture that ends on a deleted row or in the other column named in the start's numbers with the + on the selection's last row, and no state with rows on both sides of a separator (`round2/probes/clamp-fixed.jsonl`, `clamp-fixed-summary.txt`). The five records of that probe with no + after scrolling far away or collapsing are the same in the verifier's run of round 1 and are not drags past a separator.

*Found and not changed.* A selection whose bottom row is an unchanged line named on the left side (made in split layout) loses the + after a switch to unified layout; measured on round 2's files and, for a drag inside one hunk, on the files of `6d7b9973` with the same result, so it is older than the clamp (section 15 row 19; `round2/probes/zz-probe-layout-switch.spec.js`, `layout-switch.jsonl`, `layout-switch-head.jsonl`). A switch from unified to split keeps the selection and the +.

*Without the clamp* (`verify-2/after-proof.sh red`: the two production files of `6d7b9973` written in place, the tree's files copied out before and put back after, sums in `prod-copy/` and `red/`): the two new unit files and `pierre-shared-modules.test.js` `28 failed | 48 passed (76)`; `line-selection-cut.spec.js` and `suggestion-block.spec.js` `42 failed`, `14 passed` (the cut spec 40 / 4, the four guards inside one hunk; the suggestion spec 2 / 10).

*The rebase* (`range-diff.txt`, `rebase-interdiff.txt`, `probes/zz-verify2-rebase.spec.js`, `probes/rebase.jsonl`). The range-diff is the stored one plus the adaptation commit's line; `git merge-tree --write-tree 213bc3f3 8e3c7a0e` is the tree of `30381e61`, and `git diff 6f06bbb1 8e3c7a0e` and `git diff 213bc3f3 30381e61` are the same text (index and hunk-header lines aside). In a browser, both modes, a ten-file review in nested folders with four context files (three of them in nested folders): the navigator's changed files are in the order of `sortFilesByPath` and of the pane; a context file in a nested folder is placed in the pane by main's rule (before the first entry whose path sorts after it by code unit) and in the navigator by the tree, so `app/core/engine/note.js` is drawn before `app/core/z.js` and listed after `app/core/engine/b10.js` (main's rule, unchanged at `8e3c7a0e`); a click on every entry, far files and the four context entries included, lands the file's header at the top of the pane (0 to 0.36 px below the pinned place; lower only with the pane at its end: the last file, and the file before it once the last is collapsed, `probes/rebase-atend.jsonl`) and marks the entry active; the active entry does not follow the pane's scroll (nor on main: only a click sets it); Viewed is shown in the navigator for a changed file, a far file and a context file, and kept through a whitespace toggle; a collapse by the chevron is not shown there (as on main); folders collapse and open with their own chevrons and the pane keeps every file; with `prefers-reduced-motion` the viewer's list and its sticky child have `transition-property: none` and every other element measured has the blanket rule's 0.01 ms (H177 in force after main's edit of `pr.css`), and no animation runs on the two after a far navigation; the refusal warning has main's close button, which removes it and leaves the form's text and the pane's scroll position as they were.

*The clamp, attacked* (`probes/zz-verify2-clamp.spec.js`: round 1's probe plus tests for what round 2 changed; `clamp.jsonl`, `clamp-summary.txt`, `v2-pr.sorted`, `v2-local.sorted`; the same probe on the files of `6d7b9973` in `head.jsonl`, `tree-vs-head.txt`). 828 records, the same in both modes; 384 gestures with every highlighted state recorded (1,638 states at microtask checkpoints and frames, and every assignment of the selection mark): none holds a row beyond hidden lines, at no moment (the same probe on the files of `6d7b9973`: 278 gestures that do); 228 forms opened with the +, 168 of them saved: for every gesture that reaches hidden lines the form's range and the POST lie within the highlighted rows and name no hidden line (a range started on a deleted row in unified layout and cut at its bottom opens the + row's one-line form, as an uncut one does). Round 1's blocking defect is gone (`probes/plus.jsonl`: 40 of 40 with the + on 17 after the release and while hovering line 9). Every record of a gesture that reaches no hidden line (inside one hunk in both layouts and both columns, from and to deleted rows, across columns, the + alone, the chat button, the + button's drag in both directions, a context file inside one range and across a painted gap, a shift-click inside a hunk) is identical with and without the clamp. Through a whitespace toggle, frame by frame (`probes/zz-verify2-ws.spec.js`, `ws.jsonl`): the rows marked are the range `getLineSelection` answers in every recorded state; the list write cuts the selection against the patch's diff (77-79 becomes 78-79, 15-17 becomes 15-16), and a selection that started on 17 or 77, lines the re-diffed file shows and the patch does not, is not put back.

*Found by this verification and not changed.* (1) In PR mode the last line a cut selection reaches is the outermost line of a hunk of the re-diffed file, which has four lines of context where GitHub's diff has three (correction 20), so that line is outside GitHub's hunk. `buildReviewComments` (`src/utils/diff-annotator.js`) then submits `15-17` as a comment on line 15 alone and `77-79` as a file-level comment "(Ref Lines 77-79)" (`verify-2/submit-mapping.txt`, measured with the corpus' patch; the same rule as for any range that touches those lines, with or without the clamp; before the clamp `15-79` and `16-79` went to line 15 and line 16; main's upward drag opens a one-line form on 79). Local mode submits nothing to GitHub and is not concerned. A decision for the owner: accept, or cut at the patch's hunk in PR mode. (2) Older than the clamp and the same on the files of `6d7b9973`: in split layout a drag from the right column up into the left one inside one hunk is answered in mixed numbers (`getLineSelection` puts `start` and `end` under the start's side); where old and new numbers differ the form names other lines than those highlighted (`src/shift.js`, R65 up to L58: rows R61-R65 highlighted, the form says "Lines 58-65" on the right, and new 58 is hidden). The clamp names a cut end in the start's numbers (round 2) and leaves an uncut end as the viewer reports it, so H184's "no reader ever gets a hidden line" holds for ranges that reach hidden lines, not for this one. On main an upward drag opens a one-line form (its `getLineSelection` does not order the range, so its range test fails for one whose start is the higher line: read in main's `pierre-bridge.js`; measured for 79 up to 15 in `review-2026-11-27/stage-b/on-main.md`), so this is the migration's. (3) Section 15 row 19 confirmed, also for the cut `L15>R79`, which the clamp names on the left: after a switch to unified layout the + cannot be clicked until the selection changes. (4) `tests/unit/line-selection-cut.test.js:167` and `:179` speak of columns and run with the bridge in unified layout, with a range that starts on the deletions side at an unchanged line, which the viewer does not report there; what they assert is the unified rule of `_addressCutEnd`. The split rule is asserted at `:242`.

*Every failed execution of this verification.* Two, in the three whole runs with the shipped bundle; none in the development-bundle run, the repeat run, or the probes. (a) `tests/e2e/split-view.spec.js:574` "paints the header text and body of a stretched one-sided card" (PR mode), run 1: `page.goto` did not reach `load` in the test's 30 s; the page had its shell and stylesheets and no data ("Loading..."), so no line of the diff pane had run. The run started at a 1-minute load average of 2.76 and the average was 19.2 two minutes in (the machine was in use). Alone at `--repeat-each=50` from a cool machine (load 2.86): `100 passed` (both modes). Not explained further; new on the list of known flakes. (b) `tests/e2e/edited-posted-comments.spec.js:95` "the submit dialog updates edited comments after the review, never counting them as new" (PR mode; main's spec and main's `ReviewModal.js`, both unchanged on the branch), run 3: after `focus()` on the dialog's checkbox and a press of Space the box was still checked. Cause, measured (`probes/zz-verify2-modal-focus.spec.js`, `modal-focus.jsonl`, 10 of 10): `ReviewModal.show` focuses the dialog's textarea from a 100 ms timer, 108 to 118 ms after the dialog opens; when the timer fires between the test's `focus()` and its key press, the space goes to the textarea. Alone at `--repeat-each=50` it fails with and without the clamp: 8 of 550 with the tree's files (eleven runs) and 5 of 500 with the production files of `6d7b9973` (ten runs, alternating with the others; the 1-minute load average below 6 at every start; `verify-2/isolation/TALLY.txt`, `isolation/progress.txt`), every failure at the same statement. It was seen before (phase 1, round 2). A flake of main's test; nothing was changed for it and nothing repeated.

**Gotchas for the next session.** The first press of a test can land one row below the cell that was measured (seen in the probes on the first drag after a scroll, anchor 16 for 15 and 80 for 79): `dragLineNumbers` takes the boxes once they are steady over two frames and checks that the press selected its line, and makes the gesture again otherwise. `holdWorkerResults` alone opens no display window on a file whose contents are in (question 4).

**Corrected by this part:** the status table (a row for this part), H130 (its `selectLines` passes the start line first), section 11 "Review fixes" (the drag row of its table and "Also found and left alone"), section 14 rows 43 and 46, section 15 row 18, and the changeset `virtualized-code-view.md` ("Selecting lines across hidden lines").

## 12. The D2 decision: context files are `CodeView` items

Record of the decision of 2026-11-14 and of what was measured for it. The session documents are `D2-AMENDMENT.md` and the amended `PLAN.md` (section 3.15 there is the full design); this section repeats what a later reader needs. Hazards the decision adds are H79 to H92 (section 8); the vendor facts are F20 to F25 (section 4); the gate is G13 (section 5).

**The question.** Where context files render: files and line ranges outside the diff that a chat link, a tour stop, a comment on a non-diff file or the user adds to the diff pane. The plan's first default kept main's table renderer and put them in a non-virtualized footer region after all changed files.

**The decision.** Option B: context files are real `CodeView` items, interleaved by path among the changed files exactly as on main. One renderer. Accepted by the user: the look of context files changes to the vendor's highlighting and separators. Not accepted: any loss of function.

**The design in brief.**

1. One item list, two kinds of record. A context file is a `type: 'diff'` item whose bridge record has `kind: 'context'`; its id is the file path. `PRManager.fileKind(path)` answers the kind from data; `_fileStateKey(path)` builds the persisted `context:<path>` state key.
2. Order is main's: changed files in diff order, each context path (in code-unit order) before the first entry whose path sorts after it.
3. Metadata is `{ ...parseDiffFromFile(file, file), type: 'new' }` with the stored ranges merged in by `PierreContext.mergeContextRanges`. Several rows of one file are one item with one separator per hidden gap.
4. Because a context item is a diff item, everything keyed on `pierreBridge.files.has(path)` takes the path it already has for changed files: comment forms and cards, suggestions, tour stops, external threads, chat about lines, the gutter, line selection, `ensureLinesVisible`, `revealInDiff`, the minimizer. The row-based twins of all of these lose their last user.
5. Expansion is the vendor's: 20 lines per click, Shift+click for a whole gap, and the vendor's own "Expand all" control, shown on context hosts by CSS.
6. Adding or removing a context file at run time is a list replace by the second list writer, `reconcile(entries)`, which keeps every unchanged record, folds manual expansions into ranges first, and restores the first visible line, the line selection, the focused element with its caret, and the hover.
7. `loadContextFiles` becomes data-only with a generation guard (phase 5). `renderDiff` no longer resets the rows.
8. One CSS block, `CONTEXT_CSS` (ten rules, all under `:host(.context-file)`, in `public/js/modules/pierre-styles.js`), and one correction to `pierre-context.js` (section 6, item 10).

**The look, specified.**

| Element | Main | With D2 = B |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Header | Chevron, path, orange `CONTEXT` badge, Viewed, file-comment, chat, dismiss; 45 px | The same markup and height; sticky like a changed file's |
| Left border | Blue, on the wrapper | Blue, on the host |
| Line numbers | Two columns, same number in both | One column |
| Code | highlight.js, line by line | The vendor's Shiki themes, the colours changed files have; the background of an unchanged line of a changed file; no change bars |
| Hidden lines | A row "N hidden lines" with icon buttons; clicking the label reveals the whole gap; between two ranges two such rows and a thin separator | A 32 px grey bar: chevron button(s) at the left, "N unmodified lines", and for a gap of more than 20 lines "Expand all" at the right end; one bar between two ranges |
| Hidden gap of 5 lines or fewer | A gap row | The lines are shown (the rule changed files have, `collapsedContextThreshold: 5`) |
| Comment and chat buttons on a line | Inside the row | The gutter buttons of a changed file, on hover |
| Cards, forms, file-comment zone | The app's own | The same elements, under their line, full width; the zone between header and code |
| Split layout | Unaffected by the toggle | Unaffected: one full-width column, identical to unified |
| Very large (over 400 KB or 12,000 lines) or binary file | Coloured line by line | Not coloured; everything else works |
| While the worker tokenizes | n/a | Rows are plain first, then coloured |
| File ending in a newline | An extra empty last line | No extra line; the count after the last range is one smaller |
| Empty file | One empty row numbered 1 | Header-only item |

**Every function on context lines, and where it comes from after the change.**

| Function | After the change |
| --- | --- |
| Line comment (single and range), edit, delete, restore | Gutter utility and vendor line selection → form annotation → comment annotation; saved with `side: 'RIGHT'` |
| Comment stored with `side: 'LEFT'` on a file that left the diff | Drawn under its line: the bridge publishes every annotation of a context record on the additions side and keeps the stored side in the data |
| Comment or suggestion on a line outside the shown ranges | `ensureLinesVisible` → `addContextRanges` (replaces `expandForSuggestion`) |
| File-level comment, header comment indicator | The zone is the `lineNumber: 0` annotation every item has |
| AI suggestion or finding on a context line; adopt, edit-and-adopt, dismiss, restore; panel click; navigator keys | Suggestion annotation; `revealInDiff` |
| Chat about a line or a selection; header chat button | Gutter chat button; header button |
| Chat link to a context file, a line, a new range, a file not yet shown | `fileKind` and `isLineVisible`, then `ensureContextFile`, then `revealInDiff` |
| Tour stop outside the diff; cleanup when the tour exits | `ensureContextFile`, then the annotation mount path; exit calls `removeContextFile` as today, each ending in a `reconcile` that drops the item and its annotations |
| External thread on such a file (PR mode) | The annotation path with the visibility oracle, file-level fallback |
| Collapse, Viewed, their persistence | `setFileCollapsed` and the viewed toggle under the `context:<path>` key; no wrapper needed |
| Dismiss | The rows of the path from `contextFiles` (not from DOM chunks), deleted as today |
| Sidebar entry, badge, click | Unchanged; the click is a `revealInDiff` call |
| Expansion by step and of a whole gap; start and end of file | Vendor controls, "Expand all" by CSS |
| Reload | Rows and viewed state from the server as today; expansion is view state and is lost, as on main |
| Hunk summaries | None, as on main |

Edge cases, each matched to main unless marked: a file that cannot be fetched has a sidebar entry and nothing in the pane; a binary file is drawn as the text `git show` printed (uncoloured); a path that is also a changed file is never a context item, and its stored rows return when it leaves the diff.

**What is deleted as a result (phase 8; established by reference counts, proven by phase 8's grep gates).** In `pr.js`: `renderPatch`, `parseBlockLines`, `_renderLegacyFileDiffBody`, `renderDiffLine`, `getLineNumber`, `validatePendingEofGaps`, `expandGapContext`, `expandGapRange`, `expandForSuggestion`, the legacy branches of `ensureLinesVisible`, `_loadUserComments` and `showCommentForm`, the row selection delegates; `renderContextFile`, `_buildContextChunkTbody`, `_insertChunkSorted`, `_createChunkSeparator`, `findContextFileWrapper`, the DOM diffing in `loadContextFiles`. Modules: `line-tracker.js` and `gap-coordinates.js` entirely; the gap-row half of `hunk-parser.js`; `DiffRenderer.renderDiffLine` and its highlight.js code; the row-based forms and cards of `comment-manager.js`; the row branches of `suggestion-manager.js`, `external-comment-manager.js`, `tour-renderer.js`, `ChatPanel.js`, `AIPanel.js`. HTML: the highlight.js stylesheets, library and language packs from cdnjs. CSS: the `pr.css` rules for table rows. Kept: the host and header classes (`.d2h-file-wrapper`, `.d2h-file-header`, `.context-file`, `.context-badge`, ...), the parse helpers of `hunk-parser.js` while their callers remain, `FileListMerger`, `DiffContext`, the context-file routes and repository on the server.

**Acceptance checks the decision adds** (no existing bar is lowered): phase 4, the F23 unit and contract tests with the default-engine E2E run and golden dump unchanged, `context-items.spec.js`, gate G13, the context and `reconcile` bridge tests; phase 5, N5's read-only scenarios under `codeview` in both modes, the context stage of the golden dump, a context file in split layout one column as wide as the pane; phase 6, all of N5 under `codeview`; phase 7, N8's context scenarios; phase 8, the grep gates for the table renderer and no highlight.js from a CDN; phase 9, the context golden comparison classified and context files in the live drive through the real routes.

**Planning evidence** (measured before the decision on the real 1.6.4 development bundle, height validator on, with `pr.css`, main's header markup and main's card renderer; session scripts `d2/probe-<n>*.cjs`, results and screenshots in the session's `evidence/d2/`; each is now a contract test, section 4):

| Probe | What it measured | Fact |
| --- | --- | --- |
| D2-1, D2-2 | The zero-change base, a ranged item in unified layout, hover, gutter, selection, scroll targets, annotations, collapse | F20 |
| D2-2b, D2-2c | Each expansion control, Shift+click, the hidden "Expand all", the shape of the expansion map | F22 |
| D2-2d, D2-2e, D2-2f | The layout corruption after two manual expansions, bisected to the line starts; the same sequence on vendor-hydrated metadata (clean); with the starts recomputed (clean) | F23 |
| D2-3, D2-4 | `type: 'new'` in split layout; the final design with `CONTEXT_CSS`: validator 0 and an exact audit in unified and split, light and dark, with wrapped lines, after real clicks and a walk | F21 |
| D2-5 | List replace with a context item inserted above the reader (31 items, pool on): 12.8 ms, 0 px, focus, caret and selection kept, 0 orphans; `parseDiffFromFile` cost and line counting | F24, F25 |
| D2-6 | A hidden gap of 5 lines or fewer is drawn in full at the start, at the end and between ranges; 6 lines keep the separator | the small-gap rule |

Not verified by the planning probes, and where each is closed: `CONTEXT_CSS` with the app's real gutter buttons and real split card layout function (G13, phase 4); whether a list replace loses focus when nothing restores it, and `reconcile` during a pending swap or before the pool is ready (bridge tests, phase 4); a click on the separator label (contract test, phase 4); list-replace cost above 31 items (phase 5 performance run); what main draws for binary, very large and unreadable context files, and the real context routes end to end (phase 5 characterization, phase 9 live drive); the two-column fallback in split layout beyond its picture.

## 13. Future upgrade to `@pierre/diffs` 1.7

The dependency is pinned to exactly `1.6.4` (decision D6). What is written here was measured for the day the pin moves. The 1.7 line was at `1.7.0-beta.4` when it was measured (a bisect of 18 published versions from `1.2.0-beta.0` to `1.7.0-beta.4`, each bundled as a development build and run in headless Chromium; the scripts, bundles and logs were kept in the session's scratch directory under `vendor-bisect/`, which the operating system cleans, so the numbers are repeated here).

**The two defects the design is built around, by version.**

| Defect | Present | Gone |
| --- | --- | --- |
| A (fact F26): a row window that shrinks drops the separator or the card after the row it ends at, and growth does not draw it again | `1.2.0-beta.0` through `1.6.4` | `1.7.0-beta.1` to `beta.4`. The row renderer was rewritten (upstream pull request #1225) |
| B (fact F1): a splice of the item list that shifts a mounted item's index leaves an orphan host or misplaced items | from `1.2.0-beta.0` for `setItems` with an insertion, from `1.3.2` for `removeItem`, through `1.7.0-beta.4` | not gone in any version measured |

No upstream issue exists for either. The distributed files of `1.6.3` are byte-identical to those of `1.6.4`.

**What the 1.7 betas change under the bridge** (each measured on `1.7.0-beta.4` unless a beta is named):

- `resetLayoutCache` is removed. The bridge calls it as `record._instance?.resetLayoutCache?.(...)` after it empties an expansion map (`_clearExpansionMap`), so on 1.7 the call becomes a silent no-op and the item keeps the height it had with the expanded lines. H120 and H146 depend on that call.
- `isLineRenderable` is removed.
- Rows are kept across updates of an item. Fact F16 ("every update of an item replaces all its rows") no longer holds, and with it the assumption behind the re-decoration in `_onPostRender` (flash classes, selection marks and the hover restore are applied to fresh rows after every draw).
- The hover survives `updateItem` (fact F12 no longer holds; the synthetic `pointermove` of the hover restore becomes unnecessary, and may double a hover).
- From `beta.2` the development height validator does nothing in a browser unless `window.process` is defined. The development-bundle E2E run and the contract suite read the validator's console lines: without a shim they would pass with the validator silent.
- The vendor contract suite of this repository run against `1.7.0-beta.4`: **106 passed, 10 failed**; 2 of the 10 are artifacts of the harness (not behaviour changes).

**What phase 8 added to the exposure.**

- *The bridge replaces a vendor method* (H162, fact F30): `PierreBridge.coverPaneWithRowWindow` wraps `VirtualizedFileDiff.prototype.computeRenderRangeFromWindow`. The class is exported from the entry for this alone.
- *The entry exports five names* (`scripts/pierre-diffs-entry.mjs`, pinned by `tests/unit/pierre-diffs-entry-exports.test.js`): `CodeView`, `parsePatchFiles`, `parseDiffFromFile`, `WorkerPoolManager`, `VirtualizedFileDiff`.
- *Pinned internals* (each has a test in `tests/contract/vendor/internals.spec.js` or `row-window.spec.js`): `instance.hunksRenderer.expandedHunks` (a map of `{ fromStart, fromEnd }` by hunk index, `Infinity` for a whole gap) and `getExpandedHunk`; `hunksRenderer.onRenderUpdate`, `pendingHighlightResult` and `getDiffForNextRender` (F28); `instance.getLineIndex`, `instance.getRenderedDiff`, `instance.renderRange`, `instance.height`, `instance.getAdvancedStickySpecs`; `computeRenderRangeFromWindow(fileDiff, fileTop, { top, bottom })` and its answer `{ startingLine, totalLines, bufferBefore, bufferAfter }`; the shadow attributes `data-line`, `data-line-index`, `data-line-type`, `data-column-number`, `data-line-annotation`, `[data-code]`, `[data-content]`, the separator markup `CONTEXT_CSS` selects, the slot name format; the `type` field of the diff metadata (`'new'` draws one column, F21); the `FileDiffMetadata` shape `pierre-context.js` writes (F23).
- *F30* (rows after a 5,000-row block boundary are not drawn while the middle of the pane is before it; a short tail at the pane's end is never drawn) is worked around by the wrapper. *F31* (rows more than 2^18 px from the viewport are measured a 64th of a pixel off, so a file drawn whole is computed too short) is why the row window is 5,000 rows and not "every row". On `1.7.0-beta.4` the standalone reproduction of F30 does not reproduce (3 of 3 runs: a file of 5,300 rows is drawn whole at its top and a file of 5,006 rows shows its last row at the end; on 1.6.4 both reproduce 3 of 3); the measurement of F31 is the browser's and is the same there (697 rows of 16,000 read 17.375 px and 232 read 17.40625 px, the first of them 262,152 px from the pane's top), and what the viewer's layout makes of it on 1.7 was not measured. Facts and a standalone reproduction for the library's maintainers are in the owner's notes (`upstream-notes-F30-F31.md` in the session's `vendor-bisect/` directory); nothing was filed, because the library's contributing rules ask for issues written by a person.

**Order of work for an upgrade.**

1. Bump the pin, `pnpm install`, `node scripts/build-pierre-diffs.mjs` (`tests/unit/vendor-bundle-fresh.test.js` fails until the bundle is rebuilt).
2. Run `pnpm run test:contract` and read `tests/contract/vendor` **first**. A failing test whose title says "vendor defect", "bug", "constraint" or "quirk" may be a fix: read the fact (section 4) before touching application code. Expected on 1.7: the F26 tests (defect A gone), the two F30 tests on the viewer alone if the vendor covers the pane itself, the F12 and F16 tests, the `resetLayoutCache` and `isLineRenderable` reads, everything that reads the validator.
3. If the validator is silent in a browser: define `window.process` in the contract harness and in the development-bundle E2E fixture before anything else is concluded from a green run.
4. Decide per fact, in this order: F26 gone → the 5,000-row window and `_restoreTrimmedCards` can go only if F31 is gone too (measure `row-window.spec.js` "F31"); F30 gone → delete `coverPaneWithRowWindow`, the `VirtualizedFileDiff` export and H162; F16 changed → re-read `_onPostRender`'s re-decoration and the host stamps; F12 changed → remove the hover restore; `resetLayoutCache` gone → find what makes an item compute its height again after an expansion map is emptied (H120, H146), or the folded item keeps a wrong height.
5. `tests/contract/bridge` next (the bridge against the real viewer), then `pnpm run test:e2e` and one run with `PR_VENDOR_BUNDLE=dev`.
6. F1 (defect B) is not fixed in any 1.7 beta: the list is still replaced, never spliced (`_writeList`).

## 14. Differences from main a user can see

One list, for the owner. "Main" is `8e3c7a0e` (v5.4.4 and the nested file navigator) since the rebase of 2026-11-27, and was `6f06bbb1` (v5.4.3) from phase 10; the six commits between them do not touch the diff pane and change none of the rows; the rows were written against `3223ecce` (v5.4.1), and the twelve commits of main in between that do not touch the diff pane change none of them. Row 9 is the one the thirteenth changes. *Decided by owner*: chosen by the owner or the orchestrator before it was built. *Improvement*: a defect of main that is gone. *Limitation*: something the new display does worse or differently without a decision; each is also in section 15.

| # | Difference | Kind | Where recorded |

In exchange the file's first rows are on screen sooner (0.33 s against 1.1 s). Frame times while scrolling are main's. In files of up to 5,000 rows nothing differs (measured at 4,000 lines). Measured, PR / Local: long tasks during the scroll 2 / 2 (main 0 / 0), jump to the middle line 195 / 196 ms (main 50 / 53), rows on screen after 325 / 306 ms (main 1166 / 1074), frame p95 17.5 / 18.1 ms (main 18.2 / 18.4) | Limitation (very long files) | H169, F26 |

Let go after the thumb was at the end of its track, or with the pointer pulled to the pane's bottom edge, the pane goes to its end and stays there. Let go anywhere else after a slow drag, it stays where the thumb is (3,784 px short with the pointer 3 px above the bottom edge; main 0); a second drag reaches the end. Only a held scrollbar is followed: a press at the pane's right edge under which the pane moved and during which the browser told the page of no pointer move. A press on the pane's padding beside the scrollbar moves nothing when it is let go, as on main (session 6) | Limitation (a regression of degree; the release was fixed in phase 9) | H163 |

The wheel and the scroll keys bring a file back before it is needed and show no such frame (0 of 137 and 116 frames). Main draws every file at all times | Limitation, one frame | H170 |

The pane is now held at its end through that, so the collapsed header stays where the collapse put it, in view, with the pane at its end, as on main (measured in both modes, by the chevron and by "Viewed": 502.84 px below the pane's top after the collapse, 503.19 px once the file above has its contents, 0 px short of the end; before the fix 595.19 px and 92 px short). Still different from main: the file above grows on screen (its rows move up by 144 px in the measured review; on main it had its contents before), and a reader who presses, types or scrolls on the pane between the collapse and the arrival has the pane as the viewer leaves it (the line at its top kept, the collapsed header moved down by what the file grew below that line) | Limitation, what is left of it (the reader's own input ends the hold on purpose) | H174 |

Every other transition and animation of the page is still cut to 0.01 ms by the application's rule for the setting; the viewer's own two elements are exempt from it | Regression found late and fixed | H177 |

Expand the hidden lines first."), and what was typed stays | Improvement (review fixes of PR #646) | H181, H52 |

A second + on a context-file line no longer closes the other open form of the page (main's context files, drawn by its table renderer, closed it and lost what was typed in it); forms close only by their own Save or Cancel, as on main for changed files | Improvement; the context-file part decided in the unit stage of the review fixes (keep main's changed-file behaviour for both kinds) | H182 |

A drag from a deleted row to an unchanged row opens the + row's one-line form, as on main inside one hunk. In split layout a drag from one column into the other is cut in the column it started in ("Lines 15-17" on the left for the left 15 to the right 79). Before H184 the drag crossed the separator and the comment covered the hidden lines (15-79 posted, 59 of them hidden) | Better than main downward, the same upward | H184 |

Not differences (checked in the phase-9 live drive and the look check against main itself): the look of changed files in both layouts and both themes (0 of the compared screenshot pairs differ), every comment, suggestion, file-comment, collapse, Viewed and chat flow, in both modes.

## 15. Known limitations of the new display

What is left, with its hazard number. The first five are the follow-up issue drafted in phase 9 (`codeview-followups-issue.md` in the evidence package).

| # | Limitation | Hazard | Reachable when |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | A file whose row count is an exact multiple of 5,000 can lose its last separator when the row window shrinks; a card on the 5,000th, 10,000th, ... drawn row is dropped by the vendor and drawn again by the bridge (`_restoreTrimmedCards`). No application test covers a file of exactly that size | H119 (remainder), F26 | Files that show 5,000 rows or more: changed files between the highlighting cap and the deferral threshold or after "Load diff", context files expanded that far |
| 2 | An expand control clicked in the fraction of a second while a reveal in the same file awaits display expands another gap than the one clicked | H146 | One worker round trip after a navigation revealed a hidden line in that file. Not measured on main (may be main's too) |
| 3 | A position written by code as "the end" (`scrollTop = scrollHeight`) is short of the end once the files there are measured (2,095 px on the measured repository). The reader's own scroll is followed to the end; no application path writes such a position | H163 | Tests and scripts only |
| 4 | The bridge replaces a method of a vendor class to make the viewer draw the rows the pane shows | H162, F30 | Every file of more than 5,000 rows. A maintenance risk, not a visible one; gated by the contract suite |
| 5 | The row window cannot be "every row": rows far from the viewport are measured wrongly by the viewer | F31, H119 | Files of more than about 15,000 rows if the window were raised |
| 12 | In a file that shows more than 5,000 rows, crossing into the next block of 5,000 rows is one task of about 0.13 s, and a jump to a line in a block that is not drawn takes about 0.2 s (main: 0.05 s, with every row in the document). The task is the vendor's alone: it inserts the 5,000 rows and measures each (`reconcileHeights` 87 ms of 135 ms, `render` 28 ms; no function of the application in the profile). **Accepted for this change by the orchestrator's ruling of 2026-11-20 (not the owner's decision; shown to the owner at hand-over).** The options are in the follow-up draft: the 1.7 upgrade (section 13), a smaller row window | H169 (what is left), F26 | Files of more than 5,000 rows |
| 13 | A scrollbar dragged to the end of a pane whose last files have wrapping lines leaves the reader short of the end while it is held (1,891 to 3,784 px measured; main 0 to 159), and after a slow drag let go with the pointer above the pane's bottom edge (3,784 px; main 0). Let go after the thumb was at the end of its track, or at the pane's bottom edge, the pane goes to its end. Cause: the height estimates do not count wrapping lines. **Accepted as a limitation of a viewer that estimates heights before it measures them, by the orchestrator's ruling of 2026-11-20 (not the owner's decision; shown to the owner at hand-over).** What would remove the cause is in the follow-up draft: height estimates that count wrapping lines (work inside the vendor's layout) | H163 | The first drag to the end of a pane whose last files have wrapping lines |
| 14 | A scroll key in the few frames after an End key's scroll has arrived can do nothing (Chromium drops a key in the last frame of the scroll before it on any page; the bridge's writes that keep the pane at its end make the moment a few frames longer) | H163 | A second scroll key within about 20 ms of the End key's arrival |
| 15 | One frame in which the files below a file that is being drawn again are 39 to 45 px above their places | H170 | A jump of the scroll position in one step that brings a file back into the window: a scrollbar drag, a click in the scrollbar's track, a position written by code. Not the wheel, not a scroll key |
| 16 | A file that comes into view because the file below it was collapsed at the pane's end gets its contents then, and grows. **Fixed on 2026-11-25 for the reader who leaves the pane alone:** the pane is held at its end and the collapsed header stays in view where the collapse put it (`PierreBridge._holdEndAfterCollapse`). Left: the file above grows on screen; and after an input of the reader's on the pane (a press, a key, a wheel step) nothing is held, so contents that arrive later move what is below the line at the pane's top (the collapsed header, by 92 px in the measured review). The same holds for contents that arrive more than 15 s after the collapse (`END_HOLD_MAX_MS`; measured by the verifier with the request held back: header at 595.19 px, pane 92 px short) | H174, H151 | A collapse at the very end of a review whose earlier files were never on screen, followed by an input on the pane within the 30 to 100 ms of the request (ten seconds with the pointer resting on the file above) |
| 17 | ~~With `prefers-reduced-motion`, what the viewer draws is one frame behind its window~~ **Fixed on 2026-11-27 in the review fixes of PR #646**: the viewer's two elements are exempt from the application's reduced-motion rule; `reduced-motion.spec.js` counts the frames | H177 | (none) |
| 18 | A comment saved over hidden lines before H184 keeps its range; the suggestion button refuses it (H181). A drag no longer makes such a range (H184) | H181, H184 | A comment saved over a gap before H184, or seeded so |
| 19 | A line selection whose bottom row is an unchanged line named on the left side (made in split layout: a drag in the left column that ends on a context row, or one cut there at a separator) has no gutter + and no chat button after a switch to unified layout, until the selection changes; the highlight is right. The viewer keeps the selection across the switch and places the buttons by the row's side (H184, round 2); unified layout has no left-side unchanged row | H184 | Measured on 2026-11-28 in both modes on the drag clamp's files (`L12>L14` inside one hunk, `L12>L81` and `L15>L79` cut at 17) and on the files of `6d7b9973` (`L12>L14`: the same), so older than the clamp; a switch from unified to split keeps the + (`stage-b/round2/probes/layout-switch*.jsonl`). Not changed |
| 6 | A click on an expand control is lost when the file is drawn between the press and the release | H143 | Also on main (issue #638) |
| 7 | A scrollbar drag in the two seconds after a navigation can be undone by a late correction | (no number; `scrollIntoViewStable`) | Also on main by reading; not measured (issue #638) |
| 8 | Find-in-page reaches only mounted files | Q2 | Accepted; its own follow-up issue |
| 9 | A context file ends with 9 px of blank space | H127 | Every context file |
| 10 | Landings within 0.7 px of main's with wrapped lines above the target | H153 | Not visible as such |
| 11 | What the look check does not cover: hover and focus states, scrollbars (headless Chromium draws none), zoom levels other than 100%, viewport widths other than 1440 px | H126 | Checked by hand only where the live drive went |
